## Story & Purpose Auditor

### efigure1.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Kernels designed for the same resolution (matched half-power main-lobe width) have nearly identical main lobes but skirt/sidelobe levels that span ~40 dB, so leakage — not resolution — is what distinguishes the smoothing recipes.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No —
- The central claim of "same half-power width" cannot be verified from either panel: there is no −3 dB (or half-peak) reference line, and in panel (a) the plateau *heights* differ (multitaper ≈0.137, red ≈0.130, green ≈0.122), so a reader cannot eyeball where each curve crosses its own half-power point.
- The "40 dB" spread in the skirts is asserted in the caption but not marked on the figure; the reader must guess which two curves and at what frequency the comparison is made.
- Panel (b) has no legend; it relies entirely on color matching to (a). Workable but requires the reader to shuttle between panels.
- The legend entries ("Untapered, then box", "25% cosine taper, then parabola", "recipe (b′)") are pipeline shorthand that is meaningless before the methods text. The ν values — arguably the most decision-relevant numbers here — live only in the caption.
- The two panels use different normalizations (𝒦/NΔ vs. dB relative to peak) without on-figure indication that (b) has been re-normalized per curve; a reader may wrongly infer the peaks coincide.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — Overlaid line plots are the right choice for comparing the functional shape of several kernels, and the linear/dB pairing is the standard idiom (linear resolves main-lobe shape and ripple; dB resolves the dynamic range of the skirts). No mismatch.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Dense dotted gridlines in both panels (especially the y-grid in (a) at every 0.025) compete with the data; several curves are thin and dashed and get visually chopped by the grid.
- Panel (a) x-range extends to ±10 where all curves are flat at zero — roughly 20% of the panel width carries no information.
- Panel (a) y-axis tick labels at 0.025 intervals; the exact ordinate values are not part of any claim (the peak heights are an artifact of normalization).
- The dotted vertical ideal-box lines in (b) do little work beyond marking ±R and can be replaced by a light shaded band or single annotated tick.
- Heavy legend frame box in the corner of (a).

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low — Panel (a) and (b) show the same functions, but they answer different questions (main-lobe equality vs. skirt decay), so neither is dispensable. The ν values overlap with Table 2, but the figure contributes shape information a table cannot. If space is tight, panel (a) is the weaker of the two, since the dB panel already shows main-lobe agreement near 0 dB.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The two quantitative claims in the caption ("nearly the same half-power width", "skirts differ by 40 dB") are not readable from the graphic. Without a half-power reference line and an explicit skirt-level annotation, the figure does not establish its own claim.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (b) is unlabeled; a reader entering the figure at (b) (which is where the payoff is) has no key.
- [ADVISORY] Differing plateau heights in (a) invite the misreading that the kernels have different resolutions or different total power; the reason (each kernel is normalized to unit area, not unit peak) is nowhere stated.
- [ADVISORY] Cyan and olive-dashed are close in value over much of (b) and cross repeatedly near the main lobe; distinguishing them at print size will be hard.
- [ADVISORY] The multitaper ripple in (a) (≈±0.005 about the plateau) reads as a visual feature but is not commented on — either it matters (and should be annotated) or it is a distraction.
- [ADVISORY] ν, the single number a practitioner would use to choose among these kernels, appears only in prose.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Draw a horizontal reference line at −3 dB in (b) (and at half of each curve's peak, or simply normalize (a) to unit peak) and mark the common half-power crossing with a short vertical tick or shaded band; this makes "same resolution" visible rather than asserted.
- Add the ν value to each legend entry, e.g. "Hann, then box (ν = 8.9)", and move the legend to (b) or duplicate it there; alternatively label curves directly at their right-hand endpoints in (b), which removes the legend entirely.
- Annotate the 40 dB spread explicitly: a vertical double-headed arrow at a stated frequency (e.g. fNΔ = 20) spanning green to olive, labeled "≈40 dB".
- Truncate panel (a) to |fNΔ| ≤ 8 and thin the grid to a light solid line at major ticks only; drop intermediate y tick labels.
- State the normalization convention on-figure or in the first caption clause (area-normalized in (a), peak-normalized in (b)) so the differing plateau heights are not misread.
- Replace the olive dashed line with a distinct hue/linestyle pairing that separates it from cyan, or plot the two lowest-skirt curves with an inset if their difference matters.
- Rewrite the caption so the first sentence is the claim, not the configuration: "Matched-resolution kernels have nearly identical main lobes but skirts spanning 40 dB." Push N, R, and the recipe labels to a second sentence.

### efigure2.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Spectral leakage — not variance — sets the high-frequency error floor of smoothed spectrum estimates, and that floor drops markedly when the leakiest (7th) Slepian taper is dropped, adaptive weights are used, or a cosine-tapered periodogram is smoothed instead.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — Panel (b) does communicate the ranking of biases at a glance (green worst → black → cyan/olive/red ≈ 0), which is the real payload. But several essential decoding aids live only in the caption: (i) the legend appears only in (a), and the grey periodogram is silently absent from (b) with no note; (ii) the phrase "dropping the leakiest taper" maps onto "L=7 vs L=6" only if the reader already knows the Slepian ordering — nothing in the figure says the 7th taper is the leaky one; (iii) the equivalent degrees of freedom (ν), which are the whole point of the fairness comparison (all estimators have comparable ν ≈ 12–17, so differences are bias, not smoothing), exist nowhere in the graphic; (iv) the "20 dB / 10 dB / halved" floor values in the caption are not marked on panel (b), so the reader must eyeball them.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — This is a comparison of estimators as a function of frequency, so overlaid line plots are the right family, and the (b) residual-versus-truth panel is exactly the right companion: it converts a hard visual subtraction against a dashed reference into a direct read-off. Two caveats: (a) is close to a seven-line spaghetti plot in the region where the lines matter most (f∆ > 0.25), and a small-multiples version of (b) (five stacked mini-panels sharing axes, one per estimator) would remove all occlusion at no cost to the message. If (a) is retained, consider plotting the periodogram in a lighter grey behind everything and clipping the lower y-limit so the −30 dB spikes do not stretch the axis.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- The grey periodogram in (a): it consumes the majority of the data-ink, obscures the red/olive/cyan traces in the 0.2–0.5 band, and its role (variance baseline) is already implicit. Reduce to a light background trace or move to a separate inset.
- Dotted gridlines in both panels — the claim is about tens of dB differences, not precise readings; they add clutter behind already dense traces.
- Duplicated axis label "f∆" is fine, but the two panels use different y-ranges and different tick densities with no visual linkage.
- Olive vs. green and cyan vs. blue-dashed are near-collisions in hue; the olive/red pair overlaps almost everywhere in (b), so one of them carries no incremental information in that panel.
- In (b), the black zero line is drawn but not labeled as the target; the flat horizontal segment at y=0 to the left of f∆≈0 is visually ambiguous.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — Panel (a) is largely subsumed by panel (b): every quantitative claim in the caption ("floors 20 dB high", "floors 10 dB high", "halves that", "track the truth") is read off (b) alone. Panel (a)'s only unique contribution is showing the bimodal AR(4) shape and the periodogram's variance, which may already appear in an earlier figure of the same manuscript (an AR(4), N=1024 periodogram is a standard recurring exhibit). The ν values are tabular content and would be better as a small table or in-panel annotations than as a caption list. This being an eFigure, the appropriate action is trimming rather than relocation.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Caption–figure numerical mismatch: the caption states the untapered box estimate "floors 20 dB high," but the green curve in (b) asymptotes near 18 dB and is still rising at the Nyquist limit; similarly "L=7 floors 10 dB high" describes a noisy band centered near 10–12 dB. Either quote the measured floor values (with the frequency band over which they were averaged) or mark them on the panel.
  - [BLOCKING] The figure's central concept — that the 7th Slepian taper is the leakiest and that L=6 vs L=7 is the *only* difference between the black and cyan curves — is not recoverable from the graphic. Without that, a reader cannot connect the title phrase to any visual element.
  - [ADVISORY] No ν is reported for the adaptively weighted L=7 estimate (olive), breaking the equal-resolution premise that makes the comparison fair; a reader may suspect the olive curve simply smooths more.
  - [ADVISORY] The legend is in (a) only, and the periodogram entry is misleading for (b), where it is not plotted. Add "(a) only" or a note in (b).
  - [ADVISORY] Occlusion in (a) for f∆ > 0.25: red, olive, cyan, and the dashed truth are mutually overplotted, so the claim "track the truth" cannot be verified there.
  - [ADVISORY] Colors are not colorblind-safe (green/olive, cyan/blue) and will collapse in grayscale print.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Annotate panel (b) directly with the floor values: light horizontal reference lines at the measured asymptotes (green ≈ 18 dB, black ≈ 11 dB, cyan ≈ 5 dB) plus short text labels. This turns the caption's numbers into figure content and resolves the 20-vs-18 dB discrepancy.
  - Put ν in the legend text for every trace (e.g. "Multitaper, L = 7 (ν = 14)"), including the adaptive estimate, so the equal-resolution premise is visible without the caption.
  - Add a one-line in-panel note or bracket identifying L=6 as "7th (leakiest) taper dropped," with an arrow from the black to the cyan curve showing the ≈6 dB improvement.
  - Demote the grey periodogram in (a) to a pale background trace (or a small inset) and raise the lower y-limit so the estimator curves are legible in the 0.25–0.5 band.
  - Consider replacing (b) with five stacked small multiples on a shared frequency axis and shared ±10 dB y-scale, ordered worst-to-best; this eliminates all overplotting and makes the ranking the visual gestalt.
  - Drop the gridlines, recolor to a colorblind-safe sequence, and distinguish the near-identical olive and red traces by linestyle (or drop one from (b) if they are genuinely indistinguishable there, noting the equivalence in the caption).
  - If an earlier figure already shows the AR(4) periodogram and true PSD, shrink or remove panel (a) and let (b) carry the eFigure, with the true PSD shape shown as a thumbnail inset.

### efigure3.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** At equal measured resolution (equal half-power width), spectral smoothing methods trade variance reduction against leakage — multitaper achieves the highest degrees of freedom while Hann/Bohman-then-box achieve orders-of-magnitude lower leakage and side-lobe levels, and no method wins on both axes.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the panels are individually readable (labeled axes, shared x-variable, single legend), but three things block unaided interpretation:
- The vertical dotted line at x≈8 appears in all three panels with no in-figure annotation; the reader cannot know it marks `8/(N∆)` or that it corresponds to a table row.
- Panel (c) plots a quantity that means *different physical things* for different curves (side-lobe peak for flat-top kernels, main-lobe skirt for Gaussian/parabolic/Welch). A reader comparing the magenta line to the yellow line at −7 dB vs −55 dB will draw a false conclusion. This caveat lives only in the caption.
- Which direction is "good" is never marked. Panel (a) is higher-is-better, (b) and (c) are lower-is-better. Readers must infer this from the parenthetical in the caption for (a) only.
- Minor: `L = 2α − 1` requires knowing α from body text; "Untapered, then box" presumes familiarity with the two-stage (periodogram → smoother) framing.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate, but sub-optimal for the claim — Small-multiple line plots against a common x-axis correctly implement the "compare at matched resolution" design, and that is the right choice for showing each metric's dependence on bandwidth. However, if the claim is the *trade-off*, the most direct chart is a trade-off (Pareto) plot: ν on one axis, leakage on the other, with each method drawn as a trajectory as its bandwidth parameter is swept, and iso-resolution points marked. That renders the frontier and the "no method wins both" conclusion in a single panel instead of asking the reader to mentally join panel (a) to panel (b). Consider that as the main figure with the current three panels as supplement.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Dense dotted grid on both major and minor divisions in all panels; on the four-decade log axis of (b) it competes with the data.
- X-axis extended to ~26 in all three panels solely to accommodate the single magenta (Welch) endpoint at 24.6; ~40% of each panel's width is empty, compressing the region (2–16) where all comparisons actually happen.
- Nine series with low-discriminability encodings: black solid/dashed/dotted circles-squares-triangles are hard to separate in (c) where they cross repeatedly; dark green vs. dark grey and red vs. magenta are risky in print and for colour-vision deficiency.
- Unexplained truncation of curves at different x-values (grey stops at 9.5, black solid at 15, magenta runs to 24.6) reads as missing data rather than as a parameter-range limit.
- Empty lower-right quadrant — acceptable use for the legend, but the legend box could be shrunk and the panels enlarged.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — Panels (b) and (c) are two measures of the same phenomenon (out-of-band leakage) and rank the methods almost identically; one of them is a candidate for the supplement. More importantly, the caption's reference to the `8/(N∆)` line suggests Table 2 already tabulates these same quantities at that resolution, meaning the dotted-line intersections duplicate the table. That is defensible (the figure adds the full sweep the table cannot show), but the overlap should be acknowledged rather than repeated.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (c) places non-comparable quantities on one axis (side-lobe peak vs. main-lobe skirt) with no visual distinction; invites a wrong reading of the magenta/grey/red curves.
- [BLOCKING] Unlabelled vertical dotted reference line in all three panels.
- [ADVISORY] No indication of preferred direction on any axis.
- [ADVISORY] Curves terminate at different x-values with no stated reason; looks like incomplete computation.
- [ADVISORY] Colour/linestyle palette is not robust to greyscale printing or colour-vision deficiency; nine series is at the upper limit of what line-plot encoding supports.
- [ADVISORY] Wasted horizontal extent driven by one outlier point degrades resolution in the region of interest.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- In panel (c), split or mark the no-flat-top methods: either draw them with open/hollow markers plus a shaded legend group labelled "main-lobe skirt (not a side lobe)", or move them to a separate sub-panel. At minimum add an in-panel text note.
- Annotate the dotted line directly, e.g. `8/(N∆)` rotated alongside it in one panel, and state "Table 2 resolution" once.
- Add small directional cues: "less variance ↑" beside the (a) y-axis, "less leakage ↓" beside (b) and (c).
- Clip the x-axis to 2–17 in all panels and either break the axis for the Welch endpoint or mark it with an off-scale arrow and value; this recovers ~40% of plotting width.
- Drop minor gridlines; keep light major gridlines only (decade lines on the log axis).
- Consider promoting a single ν-versus-leakage trade-off panel to the main text and demoting (a)–(c) to the supplement; alternatively drop (c) to the supplement since it duplicates (b)'s ranking.
- Re-encode the three multitaper variants as one colour with three line styles and the periodogram-based methods as distinct colours, so the two families are visually separable at a glance.
- State in-figure or in-caption why each curve's parameter sweep terminates where it does.

### efigure4.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** UNCLEAR — The figure functions as a four-way diagnostic reference (four taper banks × four diagnostics) rather than asserting one claim; the closest single sentence would be "The four taper banks differ in how their eigen-weights are distributed and in the main-lobe width vs. out-of-band leakage of the resulting spectral kernel," but the figure does not visually privilege that comparison, and the numbers that would support it (half-power widths, % leakage) appear only in the caption.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No —
- The four columns are never named. There are no column headers, so a reader cannot tell which column is the sliding sinc, which is Thomson's estimate, etc., without going to Fig. 5 and then back. This is the single largest barrier.
- The four rows are likewise unlabelled; the reader must infer "as constructed / eigen-tapers / eigen-weights / kernel" from axis symbols alone.
- Row 1 is internally inconsistent: (a) plots $h_{k-n}$, (b) $h_k\cos(2\pi f'k\Delta)$, (c) $h_{k-n}/\max h$, (d) $h_k^{(i)}$ — four different quantities with three different legend schemes in what is presented as one row. Nothing on the figure explains why.
- The headline quantitative results (5.84, 8.03, 8.03, 7.41/(N∆); 0.05–1.39% out-of-band power) are caption-only. Row 4 cannot be read as a leakage comparison without them, because all four panels look qualitatively "peaked with skirts."
- The dotted vertical lines at $|f|=R/2$ and the horizontal dotted gridlines are unannotated; a reader will not know that the verticals encode the design bandwidth.
- Panels (d) and (h) appear to be the same plot, which reads as an error rather than as a deliberate statement that the bank and its eigen-tapers coincide for that column.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate (with one mismatch) — Small-multiples line plots for tapers and kernels, and bar plots for the eigen-weight ladder, are the right choices; the 4×4 lattice is the correct structure for a two-factor comparison. The mismatch is Row 1, which is not a comparison of like objects across columns (different y-quantities, different legend variables) and so breaks the small-multiples contract that makes the lattice readable. Row 4 would also be better served for the leakage claim by overlaying all four kernels on one axis (or plotting cumulative out-of-band power vs. $f$), since side-by-side dB panels make −30 dB vs. −40 dB floors hard to compare across a 2000-px gap.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Repeated identical axis labels ($k$, $i$, $fN\Delta$) and tick labels on every panel of every row; only the bottom row of each block needs them.
- Duplicate legend in (d) and (e) with identical $i=1\ldots4$ entries.
- Horizontal dotted gridlines at every 20 dB in (m)–(p) compete with the meaningful dotted vertical lines at $|f|=R/2$.
- Empty right-hand region of (i)–(l) (bars stop at $i=7$ or $i=12$ but axes run to ~13); the white space carries no information unless the differing number of nonzero weights is the point, in which case it should be labelled.
- Panel (b)'s legend variable ($f'N\Delta$) and panel (c)'s unlabelled four-colour set are local conventions used once each.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** High — The caption states outright that this is "Fig. 5 in full," i.e. an expansion of a main-text figure; as an eFigure that placement is correct, but the overlap must be made explicit and non-duplicative. Internally, (d) and (h) appear to duplicate each other, and rows 3 and 4 encode information (eigen-weight decay, half-power width, leakage fraction) that could be summarised exactly in a four-row table — the table would in fact communicate the 5.84/8.03/8.03/7.41 and 0.05/0.13/1.39/0.22% comparison far better than the dB panels do.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Columns are unidentified. Add column titles naming each of the four methods; the figure is unusable standalone without them.
- [BLOCKING] Rows are unidentified. Add left-margin row labels ("bank", "eigen-tapers", "eigen-weights", "kernel (dB)").
- [BLOCKING] Row 1 plots four different quantities under one row heading with three different legend schemes; either harmonise the y-quantity or state per-panel what is being shown and why it differs.
- [BLOCKING] (d) and (h) appear identical; either remove the duplicate or annotate that the bank equals its own eigen-basis for that column.
- [ADVISORY] The quantitative comparison that justifies the figure (half-power width, out-of-band power) lives only in the caption; annotate it on (m)–(p).
- [ADVISORY] Unexplained dotted vertical lines at $|f|=R/2$ in row 4.
- [ADVISORY] Cross-panel dB comparison in row 4 is visually difficult at this separation.
- [ADVISORY] $i$-axes in (i)–(l) extend well past the last nonzero bar without explanation.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a header row with the four method names and a left-side label column with the four diagnostic names; this alone converts the figure from "supplement appendix" to self-readable.
- Annotate each of (m)–(p) with its half-power width and out-of-band fraction (e.g. "$B_{1/2}=5.84/(N\Delta)$, 0.05% outside $|f|\le R$") and move those numbers out of the caption; label one instance of the vertical dotted line as "$|f|=R/2$."
- Resolve Row 1: if the four columns genuinely construct their banks from different objects, split it into a separate figure or give each panel a short in-panel title; do not present heterogeneous quantities as a single comparative row.
- Delete the duplicated panel (or shade/mark it as "identical to (h)"), and delete the duplicate legend in (e).
- Strip repeated axis/tick labels to the outer edges of each row block, and drop the 20 dB horizontal gridlines so the $|f|=R/2$ markers read as the only dotted feature.
- Add a fifth compact panel (or a small table) overlaying the four kernels on one dB axis, or plotting cumulative out-of-band power, so the leakage ordering is visible in one eye movement.
- Truncate the $i$-axes in (i)–(l) to the same, justified range, or add a note that the number of nonzero eigen-weights differs by design.

### figure1.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Eigenvalue-weighted multitaper spectral estimation with the full Slepian family is numerically equivalent to box-smoothing the untapered periodogram, and averaging periodograms of a sliding sinc window converges to the same estimate as $1/N_s$.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — three issues. (1) In panel (a) the three curves coincide *by design*, so the visual impression is of a single black trace; a reader cannot see the claimed agreement there and must trust the legend that three curves are plotted. (2) Panel (b) carries the actual evidence, but the reader has no anchor for interpreting $10^{-15}$ dB (double-precision round-off) versus $10^{-3}$ dB (genuine truncation error) — those two floors mean qualitatively different things and nothing on the plot says so. (3) The y-axis label "|difference| (dB)" is ambiguous: it is a difference *of* dB values, not a ratio expressed in dB. Panel (c) is nearly self-sufficient, but its x-axis is $N_s/N$ while the caption discusses "window length $N_s$," forcing the reader to reconcile the two.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — (a) and (b) are overlaid spectra/error traces, the right choice for establishing pointwise identity; (c) is a log–log convergence plot with a reference slope, the standard and correct idiom for a rate claim. One caveat: panel (a) is a "show that nothing differs" plot, which line overlay can never do convincingly; its real job is context (what the spectrum looks like), and it currently occupies ~50% of the figure area to do so.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Heavy dotted grid on all three panels; competes with the thin magenta/green traces in (b), where the traces are the data.
- Panel (a)'s three-entry legend is longer than the information it conveys, since the curves are indistinguishable; two of the three entries are effectively unverifiable from the plot.
- Circle markers in (c) are fine, but the magenta connecting line adds little beyond the markers plus the guide line.
- The full 0–40 Hz spectral detail in (a) (many peaks, fine structure) is not used by any claim in the figure.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — panel (a) is largely redundant with panel (b): everything (a) asserts is proved quantitatively in (b), and (b) additionally distinguishes round-off from truncation. Panel (c) is five numbers plus a slope; the numbers could live in a table, but the visual slope comparison against $1/N_s$ justifies keeping it. Candidate restructuring: shrink (a) to a small inset or context panel, or move it to the supplement and promote (b) and (c) to the main figure.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (b)'s two error floors are the crux of the claim but are unlabelled in-plot; without the caption a reader cannot tell that the green trace is at machine precision (i.e., exact equivalence) while the magenta trace is a real approximation error.
  - [BLOCKING] Axis-label ambiguity: "|difference| (dB)" and "max. |difference| (dB)" should state that these are absolute differences between dB-valued estimates (e.g., "$|\Delta \hat S|$ (dB)").
  - [ADVISORY] Caption/axis mismatch in (c): caption says "against the window length $N_s$"; axis is $N_s/N$. Also state $N$ on the axis or in the panel so the absolute lengths are recoverable.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) cannot demonstrate agreement; as drawn it risks reading as a single-curve spectrum, and a skeptical reader may suspect only one curve was plotted.
  - [ADVISORY] The $1/N_s$ guide line in (c) is offset from the data and slightly shallower than the measured trend; if the claimed rate is exactly $1/N_s$, the deviation at $N_s/N=16$ should be acknowledged, and the guide line's vertical placement should be stated as arbitrary.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) is visually much smaller than (a) despite carrying an independent claim (the convergence rate); the size hierarchy inverts the evidential hierarchy.
  - [ADVISORY] Green/magenta as the only distinguishing channel in (b) is a colour-vision-accessibility risk; the two traces are separated by 12 decades so this is recoverable, but line style should also differ.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add horizontal reference lines with in-plot text in (b): "double-precision floor ($\sim10^{-13}$ dB)" on the green trace and "$0.06$ dB" on the magenta, so the exact-versus-approximate distinction is readable without the caption.
  - Re-balance the layout: make (b) and (c) the dominant panels (they carry the claims) and reduce (a) to a narrow context strip or inset, or move (a) to the supplement.
  - If (a) is retained, add a short zoomed inset over ~2 Hz so the reader can actually see three coincident curves, and reduce the legend to one line ("three estimates, overlaid and indistinguishable").
  - Harmonise (c) with the caption: label the axis $N_s/N$ *and* annotate $N=1024$, or relabel as $N_s$ with tick values $1024\ldots16384$.
  - Lighten or remove the grid in (b) and (c); keep at most decade gridlines on the log axes.
  - Clarify axis labels to "$|\Delta \hat S(f)|$ (dB)" and "$\max_f |\Delta \hat S|$ (dB)".
  - State in the caption that the green trace in (b) is at the numerical noise floor, i.e. the equivalence is algebraic rather than approximate — that is the strongest sentence available and it is currently buried mid-caption.
  - Differentiate the two traces in (b) by line style as well as colour.

### figure2.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Every spectral estimator is the true PSD convolved with an expected-value kernel plus noise, so the kernel's main-lobe width and skirt height set a bias–variance trade-off in which a kernel as wide as the spectral peak is optimal (col. 3) while narrower kernels leak (col. 1) and wider ones merge peaks (col. 4).

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — The 2×4 grid structure (estimate on top, its kernel below; kernel width increasing left to right) is well conceived and legible, but nothing in the graphic identifies *which estimator* each column is. A reader sees only (a)–(h) and must map eight letters onto four prose sentences. The key quantitative claims (ν = 2, 2, 16.7, 98.4; half-power widths 0.8–47.8/(NΔ); leakage 1.3%, 0.02%, 0.12%, 65%) exist only in the caption, and the "our measure of broadband leakage" band |f| ≤ 8/(NΔ) is not drawn anywhere. The caption is effectively doing the figure's labeling work.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — The purpose is a mechanism explanation plus a four-way comparison, and paired line plots (estimate vs. truth above, kernel below) are the right choice: the vertical pairing lets the reader read cause (kernel) against effect (estimate) in one saccade. Log-magnitude y-axes are correct for a 65 dB dynamic range. One caveat: in (e) and (f) the kernel oscillations are denser than the pixel grid, so the trace renders as a solid filled block in (e) and a near-solid mass in (f). The visual message becomes "black rectangle" rather than "slowly falling skirts," which undercuts the very comparison being made. Plotting the upper envelope (peak-hold) of |K(f)|, or a log-frequency axis, would recover the intended shape.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Repeated y-tick labels on all four columns of each row (shared axis; keep only the leftmost).
- Dotted grid at every 0.1 in x and every 20 dB in y across all eight panels — dense enough to compete with the data in (c), (d), (g), (h).
- Inset frames plus dashed connector lines in all four bottom panels; four insets is a lot of furniture for what is essentially one message (main-lobe width grows left to right).
- The y-axis range −40 to 60 dB in the top row leaves large empty margins in (c) and (d).

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — The three numeric series buried in the caption (ν, half-power width, fractional leakage) are tabular data masquerading as prose. They are the quantitative backbone of the claim and deserve a small 4-row table in the main text (or annotation in-panel), not a caption sentence. The figure itself is not redundant with anything and should stay in the main text.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] No column identification. Add a header row naming each estimator so the figure can be read without the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (e) renders as a solid filled region; the "slowly falling skirts" claim is not visible in the graphic that is supposed to demonstrate it.
  - [ADVISORY] The inset in (h) spans 0.36–0.44 while (e)–(g) span 0.39–0.41. Different zoom factors in visually identical frames invite a false direct comparison of main-lobe widths.
  - [ADVISORY] The leakage criterion |f| ≤ 8/(NΔ) is asserted but never shown; the reader cannot verify the 1.3%/0.02%/0.12%/65% figures against the plots.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption mixes the thesis, the simulation setup, four panel descriptions, three numeric series, and four interpretive conclusions. It is doing too much.
  - [ADVISORY] The 40 dB-down leakage floor in (a) — the central defect of the periodogram — is not marked, so the contrast with (b) must be eyeballed.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a column title row: "Periodogram", "25% cosine taper", "Taper + smoothing, R = 8/(NΔ)", "Taper + smoothing, R = 48/(NΔ)".
  - Annotate each top panel in-corner with its ν, and each bottom panel with its half-power width and leakage percentage; then delete those number lists from the caption.
  - Replace the raw kernel trace in (e) and (f) with its upper envelope (or overlay the envelope in a heavier stroke), so skirt decay rate is readable at print size.
  - Shade or bracket the band |f − 0.4| ≤ 8/(NΔ) in (e)–(h) to make the leakage measure visible.
  - Use a single inset x-range for all four bottom panels, or if (h) must be wider, change its frame style and label the span explicitly.
  - Drop duplicate y-tick labels, lighten the grid, and tighten the top-row y-range to roughly −30 to 50 dB.
  - Add a light horizontal reference line 40 dB below the peak in (a)–(d) to anchor the "power leaks into the region 40 dB below" statement.
  - Shorten the caption to the claim sentence, the process/record specification, and the column key; move interpretation to the body text.
---

### figure3.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** UNCLEAR — the figure makes two separate claims: (a) "a 25% cosine taper suppresses the record's edges" and (b) "smoothing width trades noise against bias, so the best $R$ is a compromise that shifts toward smaller $R$ if you care about the spectral peaks." Panel (b) carries the argument the caption's title ("Choosing the resolution") promises; panel (a) is a methods aside that belongs elsewhere.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No —
- Panel (a) has **no legend**. Three curves (black, red, black-dashed) are distinguishable only from the caption. A reader scanning the figure cannot tell which is the raw record and which is tapered, and the black trace is almost entirely hidden under the red except at the edges — the very comparison the panel exists to make is only visible in ~5% of the plot width.
- The dashed taper is plotted on the $x_k$ axis "scaled to the record's peak amplitude." It therefore has no physical value on that axis; a reader will misread it as a data envelope.
- Panel (b): the two circled markers are unlabeled. The caption says they are panels (b), (c), (d) of another figure — but only two circles are visible, and nothing on the figure ties them to anything. This is a cross-figure reference the reader cannot resolve without leaving the page.
- The two dotted vertical lines are unlabeled ("peak width" and "peak separation"). These are the most interpretable anchors in the panel and they are silent.
- The abscissa $RN\Delta$ is a compound of three undefined symbols; even a domain reader needs the text.
- The peak region ($0.09 \le f\Delta \le 0.16$) is defined only in prose, so the dashed curve's meaning is not recoverable from the graphic.

**CHART TYPE:** Mismatch (panel a) / Appropriate (panel b) —
- Panel (b) establishes a relationship and a decomposition (total = noise + bias); overlaid line plots are the right choice, and the crossing of blue and red against the black minimum is a genuinely good piece of visual reasoning.
- Panel (a) is trying to show the *effect of an operation* (a process step), but a raw-vs-tapered time-domain overlay is nearly the least informative way to do it, because the two series are identical over 90% of the record. If the point is edge suppression, plot the taper on its own normalized axis in a thin strip, or show only the first/last ~100 samples, or show the resulting spectral leakage difference — which is what actually matters for the argument.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Dotted grid in panel (b): it collides visually with the dotted vertical reference lines *and* with the dashed "peak region" curve. Three dotted/dashed encodings competing in one axes.
- The mirrored (negative) branch of the taper envelope in (a): one branch conveys the same information.
- Dense y-gridlines at every 1 dB in (b) — the claim is about shape and minimum location, not about reading values to 1 dB.
- Non-round x-ticks in (a) (0, 255, 511, 767, 1023); these encode FFT indexing trivia, not anything the reader needs.
- The black untapered trace over the interior of panel (a), which is fully occluded.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panel (a) plots "the record of Fig. \ref{fig:motif}," i.e., it re-displays a time series already shown in an earlier figure, adding only the taper. That is a strong supplement candidate, or a two-line strip appended to the motif figure. Panel (b) is not redundant with a table: the crossing/minimum structure is the content and a table would destroy it.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Two unrelated claims in one figure; the caption title advertises only the second. A reader cannot state one takeaway.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (a) has no legend and no in-panel labeling; it is unreadable without the caption.
- [BLOCKING] Circled points in (b) reference panels of a different figure with no visible key, and the count (two circles vs. three named panels) does not match the caption.
- [ADVISORY] Dotted vertical reference lines unlabeled, though they encode the physically meaningful scales (peak width, peak separation).
- [ADVISORY] Dashed "peak region" curve, dotted gridlines, and dotted verticals are mutually confusable line styles.
- [ADVISORY] Caption states "the curves carry no error bars" — a defensive note that signals the figure is hiding sampling variability across the 300 realizations.
- [ADVISORY] The taper in (a) is plotted on a data axis it does not belong on.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Split the figure. Keep panel (b) as the standalone "choosing the resolution" figure; move panel (a) to the supplement or fold it into the motif figure as a short taper strip.
- If (a) stays, add an in-panel legend ("record", "tapered", "taper (scaled)"), plot the taper on a right-hand axis from 0 to 1 with a single branch, and inset a zoom on the first/last 64 samples where the two traces actually differ.
- Label the circled points directly in (b) ("Fig. 2b", "Fig. 2d") with leader lines, and make the number of circles match the number of referenced panels.
- Label the dotted verticals in-panel: "peak width" and "peak separation."
- Drop the grid, or reduce to faint solid horizontals, so that dashed/dotted is reserved exclusively for data and for the two reference lines.
- Annotate the minimum of each black curve with a small tick and the value of $RN\Delta$; that location *is* the claim.
- Replace the caption's "no error bars" disclaimer with a light shaded inter-quartile band across the 300 realizations, or state the standard error of the mean once in the methods.
- Retitle the caption to assert the finding rather than the topic, e.g. "The optimal smoothing width balances noise against bias, and narrows when the peak region alone is scored."

### figure4.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** Summing the eigenvalue-weighted Slepian taper kernels fills in the frequency band until the multitaper smoothing kernel becomes (essentially) a boxcar of width $R$, so the multitaper estimator is a band-average — with $L\approx 2\alpha$ tapers being the point past which tapers contribute only out-of-band leakage.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the four panels read as a logical chain only if the reader already knows the Slepian/multitaper setup. Specific gaps:
- $\alpha$, $R$, $\lambda_i$, $L$ are never defined on the figure; panel (d)'s vertical lines ("$L=2\alpha-1$", "$2\alpha$") are meaningless without the text.
- The dotted vertical lines at $fN\Delta=\pm4$ in (b) and (c) are unlabelled; the reader cannot know they mark the band edge $\pm R/2$.
- Normalization mismatches between axis labels and caption: (b) axis reads $|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/N\Delta^2$ but caption says $/\Delta$; (c) axis reads $\mathcal{K}(f)/N\Delta$ but caption writes $\sum_{i\le L}\lambda_i|H^{(i)}|^2/2\alpha\Delta$. One of these is wrong, and the reader cannot tell which.
- Panel (a) shows tapers $i=1,2,7$ while (b) shows kernels for $i=1,2,3$. The switch is unexplained and breaks the visual link between the two panels.
- The "gaps filled by the previous ones" argument in (b) is asserted in the caption but not annotated in the graphic (no shading, no arrows, no cumulative overlay).
- The key claim that the $L=N$ curve "cannot be distinguished from the box" is visibly contradicted by the plot: the thick black curve has clearly rounded shoulders and visible ripple relative to the dashed box. As drawn, the figure argues against its own caption.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate (mostly) — line plots are correct for (a)–(c), which show functional shape and progressive change; (d) is a monotone sequence on a log axis and is also fine. One mismatch of emphasis: panel (c) is doing the real work (accumulation toward a target shape) but is rendered as six overlapping same-weight curves. A small-multiple strip (one mini-panel per $L$, each with the dashed box as reference) or plotting the *residual* $\mathcal{K}_L(f)-\text{Box}(f)$ would make the convergence claim far crisper than six superposed traces.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (a): y-axis extends to $\pm0.20$ while data spans roughly $\pm0.12$ — ~40% of panel is empty; the legend then has to sit inside that void.
- Panel (d): y-axis runs to $10^{-15}$ although the lowest datum is $\sim3\times10^{-10}$ — five empty decades.
- Panels (b) and (c): x-range $\pm8$ with all structure inside $\pm5$; the outer thirds are flat zero.
- The $L=9$ (magenta) curve in (c) is listed in the legend but is essentially invisible beneath $L=N$ — legend ink with no data ink.
- Heavy dotted grids on all four panels compete with the data, especially with the dotted band-edge markers in (b) and (c) (same line style used for two different meanings).
- Panel (d): open circle markers *and* a connecting line for a smooth monotone sequence; one or the other suffices.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panel (b) is largely subsumed by panel (c), whose $L=1$ and $L=3$ curves already display the individual/partial-sum kernels; and panel (a) is standard textbook Slepian material that supports the argument only indirectly. Panels (a) and (b) together are candidates for the supplement, leaving (c) + (d) as the two-panel main-text figure that actually carries the claim. Panel (d) could alternatively be a three-number statement in text ($1-\lambda_7$, $1-\lambda_8$, $1-\lambda_9$) if space is tight.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Axis labels in (b) and (c) contradict the normalizations given in the caption; the quantity plotted is therefore ambiguous.
- [BLOCKING] The caption's central assertion — $L=N$ kernel "cannot be distinguished from the box at this scale" — is visually false in panel (c) (rounded shoulders, ripple plateau). Either the claim must be softened/relocated or the panel must show the comparison at a scale where the statement holds (e.g., a residual inset).
- [BLOCKING] The band edges ($\pm R/2$) and $\alpha$, $L$ are unlabelled/undefined in-figure, so panels (b)–(d) cannot be read before the text.
- [ADVISORY] Taper indices differ between (a) ($1,2,7$) and (b) ($1,2,3$) without explanation, weakening the (a)→(b) narrative link.
- [ADVISORY] $L=9$ curve in (c) is non-visible yet legended.
- [ADVISORY] Large empty plot regions in (a) and (d) dilute data-ink ratio.
- [ADVISORY] Dotted line style is overloaded (grid, band edge, $L=2\alpha-1$ marker).

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Reconcile the y-axis expressions in (b) and (c) with the caption; state one normalization and use it verbatim in both places.
- Add an inset to (c) plotting $\mathcal{K}_N(f)/\text{normalization} - \text{Box}(f)$ (or a zoom on $fN\Delta\in[3,5]$) so the "exactly the box $\ast$ Fejér" claim is demonstrated rather than asserted; alternatively change wording to "agrees with the box except for Fejér-width shoulders."
- Annotate the band edges directly: shade $|fN\Delta|\le4$ lightly in (b) and (c) and label it "$|f|\le R/2$" once; reserve dotted lines for the grid only.
- Make (a) and (b) use the same taper set (e.g., $i=1,2,3$ in both, with $i=7$ added to (a) only if $2\alpha-1=7$ is annotated as such on the curve).
- Tighten axis ranges: (a) to $\pm0.13$, (d) to $10^{-10}$–$10^{0}$, (b)/(c) to $fN\Delta\in[-6,6]$.
- Drop the $L=9$ curve from (c) (or plot it dashed above $L=N$) and remove it from the legend if invisible.
- Consider demoting (a) and (b) to the supplement and promoting (c)+(d) to a two-panel main figure; add short in-panel titles ("individual kernels leave gaps", "the sum is a box", "tapers beyond $2\alpha$ are leakage") so the chain of reasoning is legible without the caption.
- Add in-figure definitions of $\alpha$ and $R$ (e.g., "$2\alpha=NR\Delta=8$") in a corner annotation.
---

### figure5.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Four familiar spectral estimators (Welch averaging, tapered-and-smoothed periodogram, sliding sinc window, and Slepian multitaper) are all banks of tapers derived from a single window, and the flatness of each bank's eigen-weight ladder is what sets its effective degrees of freedom ν.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the figure's organizing idea (four *methods*, one per column, with construction on top and weight ladder below) is invisible in the graphic itself.
- No panel carries a method name. A reader cannot know that (a) is Welch, (b) is cosine-taper + parabolic smoothing, (c) is the sliding sinc, (d) is Slepian without reading five lines of caption.
- The payoff quantity, ν = 13.4 / 16.7 / 17.2 / 14.0, appears *only in the caption*. This is the number the whole figure exists to deliver, and it is not on the plot.
- The top→bottom pairing is conveyed by column position alone; nothing visually binds (a) to (e).
- Legends are inconsistent in meaning across panels: (b) legend indexes frequency shifts (f′NΔ), (d) legend indexes taper number (i), and (a) and (c) have *multiple colored curves with no legend at all* (7 shifts and 4 positions respectively) — the same color palette therefore means three different things across the row.
- (c) is normalized (h/max h, peak = 1) while (a), (b), (d) are in absolute units; the reader will misread relative amplitudes.
- The empty x-range out to i ≈ 12 in (e) and (h) reads as missing data rather than "only 7 nonzero weights."

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate (with one structural suggestion) — line plots for taper shapes and bar plots for a discrete weight sequence are the right primitives. The mismatch is at the *layout* level: the bottom row's purpose is a **comparison of flatness across four methods**, but it is drawn as four separated small-multiples, forcing serial eye movement to judge the very property the claim rests on. Alternatives: (i) collapse (e–h) into a single axes with four step/line series (or grouped bars) so the ladders overlay and flatness is directly comparable, freeing space to enlarge the top row; or (ii) keep the four panels but add a light shared reference line at the flat value 1/L and print ν in each panel.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Repeated y-tick labels on the second, third and fourth panels of each row (all share identical scales) — label once per row.
- Legend boxes/frames in (b) and (d).
- Empty x-axis space to i = 12 in (e) and (h).
- Color identity across (a)–(d) is purely for curve separation in (a) and (c), yet is semantically loaded in (b) and (d); the shared palette is distracting rather than informative.
- Redundant "k" x-label repeated four times in row 1 and "i" four times in row 2 (defensible, but a single shared label per row is cleaner).

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — (e–h) encode only ~7–12 numbers apiece and their entire message reduces to four ν values plus a qualitative "flat vs. sloping" statement; a one-panel overlay plus a four-entry ν table would carry the same content at a fraction of the area. The existence of Fig. S4 ("adds the eigen-tapers and kernels") indicates this material is already a family; if the manuscript's argument needs only the ν ordering, (e–h) is a supplement candidate and the main text keeps (a–d) plus annotated ν.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] No method labels on panels. The central claim ("these four things are the same kind of object") cannot be read from the visualization; the figure is caption-dependent.
- [BLOCKING] ν values, the quantitative payoff, live only in the caption text.
- [BLOCKING] Panels (a) and (c) show multiple colored curves with no legend or annotation of what distinguishes them (time shift, window position).
- [ADVISORY] (c) uses a normalized ordinate while its row-mates do not, breaking cross-panel amplitude comparison.
- [ADVISORY] Same color palette carries three different meanings across row 1.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-row comparison is spatially separated when the claim requires direct visual comparison of flatness.
- [ADVISORY] Empty bar region in (e), (h) is ambiguous (zero weight vs. undefined).
- [ADVISORY] The caption's (c) sentence packs three distinct claims (limit = box-smoothed periodogram; = Thomson with λ-weighting; eigen-tapers are Slepians) that the panel does not show — these belong in the text, not the caption.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a short method title above each column: "Welch", "Taper + smooth", "Sliding sinc", "Slepian (L=7)". This single change removes most caption dependence.
- Print ν directly in each of (e–h) (e.g. "ν = 13.4" in the upper right), and consider a bracket or shared column header tying (a)↔(e).
- Replace the four bottom panels with one axes showing the four normalized ladders as step lines (one color per method, matched to the column titles), with a dashed flat reference at 1/L; keep ν in the legend. Use the reclaimed width to enlarge (a–d).
- Give (a) and (c) minimal annotation of the varying parameter (e.g. arrow labeled "segment shift" in (a); "window position n" legend in (c)).
- Plot (c) on the same absolute ordinate as (a), (b), (d), or state the normalization in the axis label and explain why it differs.
- Use one consistent encoding for "member of the bank" (e.g. a single sequential colormap, light→dark, in all of a–d) and reserve the categorical palette for the method comparison in the merged bottom panel.
- Drop duplicate y-tick labels and legend frames; clip the i-axis in the merged ladder panel to the largest L actually used, or mark the zero-weight region explicitly.
- Rewrite the caption to one claim sentence plus per-panel identifiers, moving the equivalence arguments for (c) into the body text.

---

### figure6.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Four practical spectrum estimators with matched resolution give very different accuracy on the same record, and the difference is explained by the leakage in their equivalent smoothing kernels — the untapered box and 7-taper multitaper kernels have high tails/skirts and bias the estimate 10–20 dB above the true spectrum, while the cosine-taper-plus-parabola and Welch kernels do not.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the figure is structurally clear (matched 4-column small multiples, estimator on top, its kernel below) but the panels are anonymous. Nothing on the graphic says which column is the box-smoothed periodogram, which is Thomson's multitaper, which is the cosine/parabola, and which is Welch. A reader cannot extract the claim without decoding (a)–(h) against a dense caption. Additional gaps:
- The legend ("Estimate" in green) appears only in panel (a), yet the estimate is black, red and magenta in (b)–(d); the reader must infer that the colored curve is always "the estimate."
- The key quantitative outcome — the high-frequency bias (≈20 dB, ≈10 dB, ≈0 dB) and the median absolute errors — lives only in the caption/table, not on the plot.
- The kernels in (e)–(h) are centered at *f*Δ = 0.4, which corresponds to nothing in the top row (peak near 0.12). The reader is left to guess that this is an arbitrary evaluation frequency.
- The relationship "each bottom panel is the kernel of the estimator directly above" is implied by layout only; no connecting label, bracket, or shared column title states it.
- ν (equivalent degrees of freedom) and half-power widths — the basis of the "matched resolution" fairness argument — are caption-only.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — the purpose is (i) compare four methods against a known ground truth over frequency and (ii) establish a mechanism via the kernel. Overlaid line plots in a small-multiples grid with a common ground-truth reference is the right choice, and the vertical pairing of estimate/kernel is the correct way to expose the mechanism. Two refinements worth considering: a fifth "overlay" panel or difference panel (estimate − true, in dB) would make the 20 dB vs 10 dB vs 0 dB bias immediately readable instead of requiring visual subtraction against a dashed curve; and the kernel row could share a single y-axis-aligned overlay of all four kernels to make the tail/skirt comparison direct rather than across-panel.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Arbitrary per-panel curve colors (green/black/red/magenta). Color is doing no work — all four are "the estimate" — and it costs the legend its validity across panels. One consistent estimate color is cleaner, or use color to encode something real (e.g., leaky vs. clean).
- Heavy dotted grid at every major tick in all eight panels; competes with the fine structure of the estimates and, in (f)/(h), with the dense side-lobe fill.
- Empty y-range in the top row: axes run −40 to 60 dB while data occupy roughly −25 to 45. Two tick decades of blank ink per panel.
- Repeated y-tick labels are already suppressed (good), but repeated full tick label sets on x-axes of the top row are redundant since limits are identical.
- Four separate insets with four sets of identical axis limits (0.39–0.41, −50 to 0) and four dashed callout pairs — necessary content, but the duplicated scaffolding is heavy. Label the limits once.
- The dashed "True PSD" is drawn with a very thick line that in (c)/(d) largely occludes the estimate it is supposed to be compared against.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the bottom kernel row is explicitly described as "as in Fig. motif," so part of it restates an earlier figure's format; and the quantitative payload (median absolute errors over 300 realizations, ν, half-power widths) is duplicated in Table:ensemble. The eight-panel figure is not itself a duplicate — the estimate↔kernel pairing is the argument and belongs in the main text — but consider whether all four insets are needed in the main figure or whether two (the leaky case and the clean case) suffice with the rest moved to the supplement.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] No method names on the figure. Panels are identified only by letter, so the central comparison is unreadable without the caption.
- [BLOCKING] Legend valid for one panel only; "Estimate" is keyed to green while three of four estimates are other colors.
- [BLOCKING] The main quantitative claim (the high-frequency floor sits ~20 dB / ~10 dB / ~0 dB above truth) is asserted in the caption but never marked on the plot.
- [ADVISORY] Kernels are shown at *f*Δ = 0.4 with no stated reason; no visual link to the top row's spectral features.
- [ADVISORY] Caption states "This realization has more leakage than is typical," then gives both single-realization and 300-realization errors. A single atypical realization is being used to carry a general claim; the figure should either show a typical realization or show both.
- [ADVISORY] The matched-resolution premise (half-power widths 8.2, 7.2, 8.2, 7.8) is invisible on the graphic; the fairness of the comparison must be taken on trust.
- [ADVISORY] Thick dashed truth curve occludes the estimate in the two good cases, understating how well they agree.
- [ADVISORY] Caption is doing analysis, not description — roughly 200 words including three separate numeric series. It will not survive as a caption.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a short column title above each pair: "Box-smoothed periodogram", "Multitaper, L = 7", "Cosine taper + parabola", "Welch, 9 × 192 Hann". Put ν and the half-power width in small text inside each top panel.
- Use one color for all four estimates; reserve the legend for "Estimate / True PSD" and place it once, outside the panel grid.
- Annotate the bias directly: a short vertical arrow with "+20 dB", "+10 dB", "≈0", "≈0" at *f*Δ ≈ 0.45 in panels (a)–(d), or add a thin fifth row showing estimate − truth on a common ±25 dB scale.
- Tighten the top-row y-range to about −30 to 50 dB and thin or drop the grid (a single reference line at 0 dB would do more work than the full grid).
- Label the kernel evaluation frequency once: "kernels evaluated at *f*Δ = 0.4" as a row label, and mark that frequency with a faint tick in the top row so the pairing is explicit.
- Add a left-hand row label: "Estimate" for (a)–(d) and "Kernel 𝒦(f)" for (e)–(h), so the two-level structure reads without the caption.
- Reduce the truth curve's linewidth and plot it beneath the estimate so agreement in (c)/(d) is visible.
- Cut the caption to: what is plotted, the record parameters, and the single mechanistic point. Move the per-realization vs. ensemble error numbers and the out-of-band power percentages to Table:ensemble and refer to it.
- Decide whether the atypical realization is essential; if the argument is about typical behavior, show a median-error realization and relegate the high-leakage one to the supplement.

---

### figure7.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Three practical spectral estimators tuned to comparable resolution and equivalent degrees of freedom (multitaper, smoothed cosine-tapered periodogram, Welch) give essentially the same EEG spectrum, differing by less than their own statistical noise.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the overlay in (a) and the residuals in (b) are readable without text, but the *actual claim* ("agree to within their own noise") cannot be checked from the graphic: the noise level (~1.5–1.6 dB) exists only in the caption. A reader sees residual spikes up to 12 dB and, with no reference band, will conclude the estimators *disagree*. The claim currently lives in the caption, not the figure.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — an overlay plus a difference (residual) panel is the right idiom for "these methods agree." The only structural gap is the missing tolerance band that turns the residual panel from descriptive to decisive.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Dense dotted grid in both panels competes with the curves, especially in (a) where line crossings are the information.
- Panel (b) y-axis spans ±15 dB while data occupy roughly −5 to +12.5 dB; ~40% of the panel is empty, visually shrinking the residuals' structure but also wasting ink.
- Second legend in (b) largely restates (a)'s color key; could be replaced by inheriting colors plus a one-line note.
- Panel (a) is drawn at full height even though the fine wiggles of the black multitaper trace are not what the claim rests on.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panel (a) re-plots the same EEG epoch already shown in the abstract/overview figure, and the numerical summaries (half-power widths, ν, median and 95th-percentile differences) are a three-row table hiding in the caption. Panel (b) plus a small table would carry the claim; panel (a) is largely confirmatory.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] No visual representation of the "own noise" threshold in (b). Without a shaded ±1.6 dB band (or per-estimator bands), the figure appears to refute its own caption.
- [BLOCKING] Draw-order occlusion: magenta appears plotted over red in both panels, so wherever the two agree the red curve is invisible. The reader cannot tell whether red is absent, hidden, or coincident — which matters because the claim is about relative agreement between the two non-multitaper methods.
- [ADVISORY] The caption's key mechanistic point — "largest differences are in the valleys between harmonics, filled in by the seventh Slepian side lobes" — is unmarked on the plot, though it is visually checkable.
- [ADVISORY] Caption carries seven numbers that the reader must hold in memory while scanning two panels.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) lacks any summary statistic annotation (median lines), so the reader must eyeball a central tendency from a spiky trace.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a light gray shaded band at ±1.6 dB (or ±2σ of the estimator noise) across panel (b), labeled *in-panel* as "estimator noise." This single change makes the figure prove its own title.
- Fix draw order or use offset/transparency: plot magenta first, red on top, or thicken the lower curve; alternatively split (b) into two stacked traces with a small vertical offset so both are always visible.
- Tighten panel (b) to roughly −6 to +13 dB and lighten or thin the grid to faint dotted or drop vertical gridlines entirely.
- Add horizontal dashed lines (or right-margin ticks) at the two median absolute differences, annotated "0.6 dB" and "0.9 dB," so the central claim is read off the axis rather than the caption.
- Mark two or three inter-harmonic valleys in (a) with small arrows or a shaded frequency stripe carried down into (b) to make the side-lobe explanation visible.
- Move ν, half-power width, median, and 95th-percentile values into a compact three-row table (in-figure inset or adjacent table), reducing the caption to the claim plus the side-lobe explanation.
- Consider reducing panel (a) to ~60% of current height (or moving it to the supplement if the abstract figure already shows this epoch), promoting (b) to the primary panel since it carries the claim.

### figure8.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Across a seizure recording, the multitaper estimate and the frequency-smoothed periodogram produce nearly identical, low-variance time–frequency and spectral estimates, whereas the raw tapered periodogram is dominated by estimation noise.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — the three spectrograms (c, e, g) carry no in-panel identification of which estimator produced them, so the central comparison cannot be read without the caption. Several other cues are also caption-dependent: the meaning of the blue vs. red dashed lines in (a) and their correspondence to (d) vs. (f); the fact that the white dashed lines in (c, e, g) are the same instants; the shaded band in (a) as the seizure; the colourbar units (dB) are unlabelled; the legend appears only in (f) though (d) shows the same four curves; and (h) does not state which two estimators are being differenced.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — spectrograms for time–frequency evolution, overlaid line spectra for point-in-time estimator comparison, and a histogram for the pixelwise agreement between two estimators are all well matched to their purposes. One caveat: the stacked spectrogram layout invites row-to-row comparison of variance, which works, but the difference is easier to see in (h); consider whether all three spectrograms need to be full width.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Three separate colourbars for one shared 10–45 dB scale; one shared colourbar suffices.
- Dotted grid lines in (d) and (f) add ink without supporting the claim.
- Jet colourmap introduces perceptual banding that can be mistaken for spectral structure (not just aesthetic — it manufactures apparent features near the yellow/cyan transitions).
- Repeated "Time (s)" / tick labels on (c) and (e) when axes are shared with (g).
- Panel (b) is illustrative context rather than evidence for the estimator claim; it competes for space with the comparison.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — (e) and (g) are intentionally near-duplicates, and their equivalence is quantified more compactly in (h); (d) and (f) repeat the same four-curve comparison at two instants. The figure could carry its claim with two spectrograms plus (h), moving the third spectrogram and one spectrum panel to the supplement. A small table of effective degrees of freedom (ν) per estimator would replace the parenthetical ν values now buried in the caption.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panels (c), (e), (g) are unlabelled as to estimator. Add in-panel text ("Periodogram", "Multitaper", "Smoothed periodogram", with ν) so the row comparison is readable without the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] (d) and (f) are not identified as pre-ictal and ictal, and the marker colours in (a) (blue, red) are not reused in (c, e, g) (white) or in (d, f) panel frames. Establish one colour/label convention linking instant → panel.
  - [BLOCKING] Colourbars have no units label; add "Power (dB)" once.
  - [ADVISORY] (d) and (f) use different y-ranges (≈ −5–45 dB vs 20–85 dB), which obscures whether the estimators' relative behaviour changes between states. Either match ranges or state explicitly that the offset is a power change, not a scale change.
  - [ADVISORY] The x-axes of (a) and (c, e, g) do not start at the same value (window-centre convention), so the vertical alignment across panels is slightly off; the eye reads it as misregistration.
  - [ADVISORY] (h) shows a heavy-tailed difference distribution but no summary statistic; a reader cannot tell whether "nearly identical" is supported. Annotate with median and an interquartile or 95% interval, and mark 0 dB.
  - [ADVISORY] ν is used throughout the caption without definition; the term "Recipe (b′)" in the legend of (f) is an internal cross-reference that will be opaque at a glance.
  - [ADVISORY] The statement "the channel is the one with the largest ictal rise in 2 to 20 Hz power" is a selection rule that affects interpretation; it belongs early in the caption or in the text, not as a trailing sentence.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Label each spectrogram in-panel with estimator name and ν; delete two of the three colourbars and place one shared, unit-labelled bar to the right of the stack.
  - Adopt a single colour convention for the two analysed instants and apply it to the dashed lines in (a), the dashed lines in (c, e, g), and the titles/frames of (d) and (f); label (d) "pre-ictal" and (f) "ictal".
  - Replicate the four-curve legend in (d), or place one legend outside both panels; rename "Recipe (b′)" to the method's descriptive name.
  - Switch to a perceptually uniform colourmap (e.g. viridis/turbo) so apparent spectral features are attributable to the data.
  - Remove grid lines in (d, f); harmonise their y-ranges or add a note on the power offset.
  - Annotate (h) with median and 95% interval of the difference and a zero reference line so the agreement claim is quantified on the figure.
  - Consider promoting (h) to a larger panel and demoting one spectrogram plus one spectrum panel to the supplement, since the figure's claim is agreement between two estimators and excess variance in the third.
---

### figure9.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: UNCLEAR — Two claims are competing: (i) window length sets the time–frequency trade-off for sleep spindles (short = sharp in time, blurred in frequency), and (ii) multitaper and smoothed-periodogram estimators achieve nearly the same resolution/degrees-of-freedom trade-off except in the small-α regime (1 s windows), where multitaper is narrower. Either would make a strong figure; together they dilute each other. If forced to pick one sentence, the caption's final sentence suggests (i) is the intended headline, but panels (c,e,g) and (h) exist only to serve (ii).

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — The 2×3 spectrogram grid has no row or column labels. Nothing in the image tells the reader that rows are 1/2/4 s windows or that the left column is multitaper and the right is a smoothed periodogram; that information lives only in the caption. The white rectangles are uninterpretable without the caption's explanation (window length × half-power width, area ≈ ν/2). The grey shading in (a) is unlabelled (spindles). In (h), "Achieved half-power width" and ν are undefined on the plot, and the inline note "α=1, L=1: small-α regime" presumes the reader already knows the argument. A reader scanning the figure cold would see six similar-looking heatmaps and not know what to compare.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate (with one caveat) — Spectrograms are the right vehicle for a resolution argument, and the dumbbell/lollipop plot in (h) is a good match for comparing two estimators across three settings on a common quantitative axis. Caveat: the perceptual differences between left and right columns are very subtle at this size, so the chart type is doing poorly at the comparison it is asked to make. A jet colormap further hurts: it manufactures false contours and makes small differences between paired panels look like large ones (and vice versa). Consider perceptually uniform colour (viridis/turbo alternative) and, for the method comparison, either a zoomed inset on a single shaded spindle in the 11–16 Hz band or an explicit difference panel (multitaper − smoothed, dB) instead of asking readers to mentally subtract six full spectrograms.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- The 2 s row (d,e) is the interpolated middle case; it adds little beyond the 1 s and 4 s endpoints and is the row where the two methods differ least.
- Numeric annotations in (h) duplicate the positional encoding of the markers (double encoding of six values).
- Dotted vertical gridlines every 0.25 Hz in (h) are denser than the claim requires.
- The black broadband EEG trace in (a) is contextual only; the red 11–16 Hz trace plus shading carries the spindle information.
- Spectrogram frequency axis stops near 24 Hz while (a) is filtered to 35 Hz — the unexplained mismatch invites a "what was cropped?" question.
- Six separate x-axes with repeated 0–40 tick labels; only the bottom row needs them.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — Panel (h) contains only six numbers, all of which are also printed as text inside the panel and restated in the caption; it is close to being a three-row table. It earns its place only because the common-axis layout makes the "widths converge at α=2" point visible at a glance — keep it, but stop restating the numbers three times. The 2 s row is a strong supplement candidate. If a neighbouring figure already establishes the multitaper-vs-smoothing bandwidth/ν relationship on synthetic data, (h) is redundant with it and should move to the supplement.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Spectrogram grid is unlabelled: no row labels for window length, no column headers for estimator. The figure cannot be read without the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Compound claim — the figure argues both a window-length trade-off and an estimator comparison. Split into two figures, or demote one argument to the supplement.
  - [BLOCKING] The white resolution boxes are the conceptual key to the whole figure but are completely unexplained in-image and are easily mistaken for ROI markers.
  - [ADVISORY] Grey shaded spindles in (a) are not tied to anything in (b–g); the reader has no guide for where to look in the spectrograms.
  - [ADVISORY] Jet colormap with a large dark-red saturated band below ~4 Hz dominates every panel and draws attention away from the 11–16 Hz spindle band that the claim is about.
  - [ADVISORY] Differences between paired panels are near the limit of visual detection at print size.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption ends with the interpretive punchline ("Short windows keep the spindles brief and blur them in frequency") that should be stated in the figure itself.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add left-side row labels ("1 s", "2 s", "4 s") and column headers ("Multitaper, L = 2α−1", "Smoothed periodogram") to the spectrogram grid; delete the redundant per-panel x-axes except on the bottom row.
  - Label the white box once, with a small leader line reading "window length × half-power width (area ≈ ν/2)".
  - Carry the spindle markers from (a) down into the spectrograms as thin vertical ticks or a dashed 11–16 Hz band, so the reader knows what feature to judge.
  - Move the 2 s row (d,e) to the supplement and keep the 1 s vs 4 s contrast, which is where the claim lives.
  - In (h), drop the per-marker numeric labels (or keep them and drop the gridlines), and replace the inline "α=1, L=1: small-α regime" note with a shaded band or bracket marking that row as the exception.
  - Switch to a perceptually uniform colormap and consider clipping or separately scaling the <4 Hz band so the spindle-band contrast is visible.
  - Add a one-line in-figure annotation stating the takeaway (e.g. "short window: sharp in time, blurred in frequency") so the claim survives without the caption.
  - Extend or explain the spectrogram frequency range to match the stated 0.3–35 Hz filter.

## Composition & Layout Inspector

### efigure1.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — Both panels carry a full four-sided frame with mirrored tick marks on all four spines (top/right ticks encode nothing beyond the left/bottom ones). Both panels are filled with a full dotted grid; in panel (a) the horizontal grid at 0.125 and the vertical grid at ±4 sit almost exactly on top of the "ideal box" data curve, which is itself drawn as a black dotted line — data ink and non-data ink are visually identical there. Panel (a) has nine labelled y-ticks at three decimals (0.000–0.200); five labels (0.00, 0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20) would carry the same information with less ink.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — Flow between panels is naturally left→right, but panel (b) carries no series identification at all, forcing the reader to jump back to the legend in panel (a) (and across a wide gutter) for every one of five curves. The x-ranges also differ between panels (±10 vs ±25) with no visual cue (e.g., a shaded or bracketed span in (b) marking the extent shown in (a)), so the reader must silently rescale.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Boxed, opaque-filled legend with border in panel (a).
- Top and right spine ticks (mirrored, redundant).
- Dense full-field dotted grid in both panels, at similar weight/style to the dotted "ideal box" series.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall, but inefficiently distributed — in panel (a) the kernels are essentially zero for |fNΔ| > 6, so roughly 40% of the panel width and the entire lower-left/lower-right quadrants are empty. Trimming to ±8 would enlarge the informative main-lobe region. Conversely, the inter-panel gutter is wider than needed.

**AXES:** OK — Panel (a) y begins at zero (correct for a non-negative kernel). Panel (b) is dB relative to peak, so a 0 → −100 range is standard and does not need a zero origin; the clipping of the olive curve at the −100 floor should ideally be acknowledged. No inverted axes. Note only that the mismatched x-limits between panels are undocumented in the caption.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate for deterministic kernel functions.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a) and (b) both present, upper-left, outside the axes, same size and style.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — wide, low panels suit these symmetric spectral kernels; panel (b) in particular benefits from the width to resolve the multitaper ripple.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — the only boxed element is the legend; see below. No callouts overlap data.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — Two-column bordered legend in panel (a); it does not overlap data (data peak ≈0.14, legend sits above 0.19), so this is not blocking. However, six entries are looked up rather than read: in panel (a) the curves separate cleanly at the shoulder near fNΔ ≈ ±3 to ±5, and in panel (b) the five curves are widely separated at the right edge (green ≈ −32, black ≈ −40, red ≈ −77, cyan ≈ −92, olive ≈ −97 dB), which is an ideal position for direct colour-matched endpoint labels.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) has no series identification; all five curves require a cross-panel lookup to the legend in (a).
- [ADVISORY] Detached bordered legend box on a multi-line chart; direct labelling is feasible in both panels.
- [ADVISORY] Dotted black "ideal box" series is stylistically indistinguishable from the dotted gridlines, especially where it coincides with the grid at 0.125 and ±4 in (a) and along the vertical dotted lines at ±4 in (b).
- [ADVISORY] Full dotted grid plus four-sided frame with mirrored ticks lowers the data-ink ratio without aiding any specific comparison.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) x-range extends to ±10 where all kernels are flat at zero, wasting ~40% of the panel area.
- [ADVISORY] Red/green pairing plus green/olive similarity is a deuteranopia risk; the figure currently relies on colour alone for four of six series.
- [ADVISORY] Nine three-decimal y-tick labels in (a) are more precision than the comparison requires.
- [ADVISORY] Differing x-limits between (a) and (b) are not stated in the caption and are not visually cued.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete the legend box and label directly: in panel (b), place short colour-matched labels ("Untapered", "Multitaper L=7", "25% cosine + parabola", "Hann", "Bohman") at each curve's right-hand endpoint, staggered vertically as the data already separate them; in panel (a) label only "Ideal box" beside the dotted shoulder at fNΔ ≈ +4.2 with a thin leader line. Keep a minimal two-entry legend in (a) only if any label cannot be placed without overlap.
- Distinguish the ideal box from the grid: draw the box as a thin solid or dash-dot grey line, or keep it dotted but remove the grid entirely (see next point).
- Reduce grid to a small number of light horizontal reference lines (e.g., at the −3 dB level in (b) and at 0.125 in (a)) drawn at lower weight than any data line; drop the vertical grid.
- Remove the top and right spines and all mirrored ticks; retain left and bottom spines only.
- Trim panel (a) x-limits to about ±8 and reduce y-tick labels to 0.00, 0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20 (minor ticks unlabelled).
- Add a faint vertical span or tick pair in panel (b) marking the ±10 window shown in (a), and state both x-ranges in the caption.
- Add redundant encoding beyond colour: give each series a distinct dash pattern (or vary line weight) so the figure survives greyscale printing, and re-check the red/green pair under a deuteranopia simulation.
- Note in the caption that the Bohman curve is clipped by the −100 dB axis floor in (b).
- Narrow the inter-panel gutter slightly and use the recovered width for the panels.

### efigure2.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — the data themselves are dense and well served (high-information spectral traces), but there is removable non-data ink: (i) a framed, white-filled legend box with 7 entries in panel (a); (ii) complete four-sided box frames on both panels (top and right spines carry no information); (iii) full dotted x- and y-gridlines in both panels, which cross directly through the noisy traces and compete with the low-amplitude structure in (b) near 0 dB. Gridline weight is lighter than the data lines, so this is a secondary issue, but the y-gridlines in (a) add little given the labeled ticks.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — (a) → (b) left-to-right, frequency increasing rightward in both, shared abscissa definition. One minor backtrack: the legend lives only in (a), so a reader parsing (b) must jump back leftward across the full figure width to decode five colors. Acceptable, but avoidable (see fixes).

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Legend frame + opaque fill in (a) (the box outline and fill encode nothing).
- Top and right spines on both panels.
- Redundant y-gridlines in (a) duplicating labeled ticks.
- Near-duplicate color semantics: the heavy black zero-reference line in (b) is the same color and comparable weight as the "Multitaper, L = 7" series, so the reference line reads as data (or vice versa).

**WHITE SPACE:** Mixed — panel (a) is crowded in its lower-right (five noisy traces superimposed in the −20 to 0 dB band, hard to separate cyan/olive/red), while panel (b) wastes roughly the upper third of its area: y-limit is 30 dB but no trace exceeds ~19 dB. The inter-panel gutter is also wider than needed.

**AXES:** Mostly OK, with issues — dB (log) ordinate means a zero baseline is not required, and no inverted axes are used; both fine.
