Issues: (1) panel (a) appears to clip the grey periodogram's deepest nulls at the lower axis limit (~−33 dB) without any indication of clipping; (2) the two panels use different y-ranges and tick intervals (20 dB vs 5 dB), which is defensible since they show different quantities, but should be made unmistakable; (3) panel (a)'s lowest tick is unlabeled below −20, leaving the clipped region unquantified; (4) abscissa label "fΔ" is repeated identically on both panels — correct but redundant if panels are ever stacked.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — variability is conveyed instead by equivalent degrees of freedom (ν) quoted in the caption. This is legitimate for spectral estimates, but no graphical uncertainty indicator (e.g., a single chi-square-based CI bar, or the ν values annotated near each trace) appears in the figure itself. Not blocking, since ν is defined in text.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a) and (b) both present, both upper-left outside the axes, same size/style/parenthetical form.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — wide, short panels suit a frequency-domain trace with fine structure; ~2.8:1 per panel is well matched to the 1024-point series.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — the only boxed element is the legend in (a). Its lower-left corner sits close to the periodogram spikes around fΔ ≈ 0.18–0.22 (spikes reach ~25 dB against a box floor of ~22 dB), so at reduced publication width it will likely occlude data. No callouts or brackets otherwise.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — a detached, framed 7-entry legend inside the plotting area of (a), serving both panels. Seven noisy, mutually overlapping series make full inline labeling in (a) impractical, so a legend is defensible there; however panel (b) is an ideal candidate for direct labeling, since the five smoothed traces separate cleanly at the right edge (green ≈ 18 dB, black ≈ 13, cyan ≈ 7, olive/red ≈ 0). The box frame and fill should go regardless.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Legend box in (a) risks occluding the grey periodogram spikes near fΔ ≈ 0.18–0.22 at final scale; frame and opaque fill add non-data ink.
- [ADVISORY] Grey periodogram appears clipped at the lower axis limit in (a) with no clipping indication; readers cannot tell whether nulls extend further.
- [ADVISORY] Black zero-reference line in (b) is visually indistinguishable in color/weight from the black "Multitaper, L = 7" trace.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) carries ~11 dB of empty headroom (limit 30 dB, max data ~19 dB), wasting real estate while (a)'s lower band is crowded.
- [ADVISORY] Five series in (a) below 0 dB (black, cyan, olive, red, grey) overplot heavily; cyan-on-white and olive-on-white have weak luminance contrast, and the red/green pairing is problematic for deuteranopic readers.
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend forces a cross-figure lookup when reading (b).
- [ADVISORY] Full box frames and y-gridlines in (a) add non-data ink over dense traces.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Strip the legend frame and fill (`frameon=False`) and move it into the genuinely empty region of (a) above ~48 dB, or relocate it to a narrow shared strip beneath both panels / to the right of (b) so it occludes nothing.
- Direct-label the five smoothed traces in (b) at their right endpoints in matching colors ("untapered + box", "L = 7", "L = 6", "L = 7 adaptive", "cosine + parabola"), staggering "adaptive" and "cosine + parabola" vertically (or adding short same-color leader lines) since both land near 0 dB. This removes the lookup cost for (b) entirely and lets (a)'s legend shrink or disappear.
- Render the zero reference in (b) as a thin grey or dashed line, clearly lighter than any data trace, so it cannot be mistaken for the L = 7 series.
- Extend (a)'s lower limit to include the full periodogram range, or state the clipping explicitly in the caption; add a labeled tick at −40 dB.
- Tighten (b)'s y-limit to about −10 to 20 dB and reclaim the freed height for panel depth, easing the congestion in (a)'s −20 to 0 dB band.
- Drop the top and right spines and the y-gridlines; retain light x-gridlines only if frequency read-off matters, at a weight clearly below the thinnest data line.
- Replace cyan with a darker blue-green and olive with a darker gold, and consider a colorblind-safe set (or distinguish the red "cosine + parabola" trace by a dash pattern) so the red/green contrast does not carry unique meaning.
- Optionally annotate each trace's ν value inline next to its label rather than only in the caption, so the variance/bias trade-off central to the figure's message is visible in the graphic.

### efigure3.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The data themselves are dense and well-earned, but there is a substantial amount of non-data ink: (1) all three panels carry complete four-sided bounding boxes; the top and right spines encode nothing. (2) Full two-way dotted gridlines in every panel — in panel (b) this means ~9 horizontal decade lines plus 6 vertical lines, a lattice that competes with the thin curves at the low end (the Bohman/Hann traces below 10⁻⁶ sit in a mesh of gridlines). (3) The x-axis label "half-power width × *N*Δ" is printed three times on three identical axes; two instances are redundant. (4) The legend is enclosed in a heavy black frame that adds no information. (5) Triple encoding (colour + marker + line style) for 9 series is redundant, but is defensible here for greyscale printing — leave it.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — (a) top-left → (b) top-right → (c) bottom-left → legend bottom-right follows the standard Z-path, and placing the legend in the otherwise-empty fourth quadrant is a good use of real estate. One caveat: the legend is diagonally maximally distant from panel (b), so a reader decoding the top-right panel must traverse the whole figure for every series lookup.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Heavy rectangular frame around the legend.
- Top and right spines on all three panels.
- Dense dotted gridlines in both directions, especially the decade grid in (b).
- Duplicated x-axis titles on three co-scaled axes.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall (the empty quadrant is well used by the legend), but the interiors are unevenly filled: in every panel the region x > 17 is empty except for a single long magenta segment reaching 24.6, while all the interesting structure is compressed into x ∈ [1.5, 16]. Conversely, the low-x region of (a) and (c) is overcrowded — 9 series and their markers collapse into a knot below x ≈ 5.

**AXES:** OK — (a) y begins at 0, correct for a count-like quantity (ν). (b) is log-scaled, which is documented by the 9-decade span of the leakage measure. (c) is in dB, i.e. log-ratio data, so a zero baseline is neither required nor meaningful; no axis is inverted. Minor: the x-axes of the three panels are numerically identical but are drawn and labelled independently rather than shared; also the x-axis is not annotated at 0 and starts implicitly near 1 — harmless, but a shared axis would make the co-registration explicit.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — and correctly so: the caption states these are exact kernel and degrees-of-freedom computations with no sampling variability. This is exactly the documentation a reviewer needs; keep that sentence.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a), (b), (c) are present on all three data panels, uniformly parenthesised, same size, same outside-top-left position. The legend quadrant is deliberately unlabelled, which is correct.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — panels are near-square, suitable for these monotone trend/scatter-line relations; the 2×2 arrangement is efficient.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — The only annotation is the vertical dotted reference line at 8/(*N*Δ), and it is drawn in the *same* dotted style and weight as the gridlines, so it is essentially invisible as an annotation; a reader cannot tell it apart from the x = 8 gridline without reading the caption. It also carries no in-figure label.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — With 9 series, a keyed legend is the right choice and full inline labelling is not realistic; the Tufte 2–5 series inline rule does not apply. The box itself, however, is over-built (heavy frame) and maximally distant from panel (b). No legend/data overlap.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Reference line at 8/(*N*Δ) is stylistically identical to the gridlines and unlabelled, so the figure's single most important annotation reads as chartjunk.
- [ADVISORY] Legend frame is heavy and the legend sits diagonally opposite panel (b), maximising lookup cost for that panel.
- [ADVISORY] Gridline lattice (especially the 9-decade grid in (b)) is visually comparable in weight to the thinner curves.
- [ADVISORY] Top/right spines on all panels are non-data ink.
- [ADVISORY] x-axis title repeated three times on three identical axes.
- [ADVISORY] Severe curve/marker congestion for x ≲ 5 in panels (a) and (c); the black, dashed-black, dotted-black, green and red series are nearly indistinguishable there.
- [ADVISORY] Roughly one-third of each panel's horizontal extent is empty, driven solely by the single Welch point at x ≈ 24.6.
- [ADVISORY] Olive/yellow and cyan on white, and dark-grey vs. black, are low-contrast pairs; discrimination currently leans on marker shape.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Restyle the 8/(*N*Δ) line so it reads as data-relevant rather than as grid: make it a solid thin grey vertical rule (or a light vertical band), and label it inline at the top of panel (a) as "8/(*N*Δ) (Table 2)". Then it needs no caption explanation.
- Lighten the gridlines to ~0.3 pt light grey, or drop the vertical gridlines entirely and keep only the decade lines in (b); ensure every gridline is visibly lighter than the lightest data curve.
- Remove the legend frame; keep the keys on white space. Optionally move the legend nearer the geometric centre of the 2×2 block (e.g. upper-left of the fourth quadrant) to shorten the trip from panel (b).
- Drop the top and right spines on all three panels.
- Share the x-axis across the column/row: label "half-power width × *N*Δ" once per column (or once, centred under the bottom panels) and delete the duplicates.
- Consider truncating the x-axis to ~0–17 and either (i) plotting the Welch point at 24.6 with a break marker, or (ii) using a log x-axis, which would simultaneously decompress the crowded x ≲ 5 region and absorb the lone far-right point. This recovers ~30% of panel width for the data.
- To relieve the low-x knot, directly label the 2–3 most-discussed series (e.g. "Multitaper, L = 2α−1", "Bohman, then box", "Welch, 50% overlap") at their right-hand endpoints in panel (a), in their own colours; the legend then only has to be consulted for the remaining curves.
- Check the palette against a colour-blind simulation; darken the olive and consider replacing dark grey with a dashed variant of a distinct hue so that colour alone is not carrying the black/grey distinction.
- Retain the caption sentence stating that all curves are exact computations with no sampling variability — it pre-empts the "undefined error bars" objection.

### efigure4.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — several classes of erasable ink:
- Full four-sided frames on all 16 panels; the top/right spines encode nothing and, at 16× repetition, add substantial non-data ink.
- Redundant axis labels: "$k$" appears 8 times, "$i$" 4 times, "$fN\Delta$" 4 times, and the tick labels repeat under every panel in a column even though every column shares an identical x-domain. One x-label + one tick row per column block (rows a–d and e–h share the same $k$ axis) would suffice.
- The $i=1\ldots4$ legend is printed twice (panels d and e) for the same four series in the same colors — fully redundant data ink.
- Bar panels (i)–(l): solid dark-navy fills with black outlines are a heavy encoding for 7–12 scalar values; the outline is redundant with the fill, and the fill is redundant with the bar top.
- Row 4: dotted horizontal gridlines at −20/−40/−60 plus dotted vertical gridlines at −20/0/20 are drawn in *the same dotted style* as the substantive $|f|=R/2$ reference lines, so decorative ink is visually indistinguishable from data ink.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — the grid itself reads naturally (row-major, left→right, top→bottom), but the figure is not self-describing: the four columns are unlabeled and the caption defers to "Columns as in Fig. 5," forcing the reader out of the figure and into another figure to decode what varies horizontally. In a 16-panel extended-data figure this is a costly jump. Add short column headers (one line above row 1).

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Duplicate legend box (d and e) for identical series.
- Bordered legend boxes (b, d, e) — three separate framed boxes.
- Bar outlines in (i)–(l).
- Gridlines in row 4 that compete with, and mimic, the meaningful $R/2$ markers.

**WHITE SPACE:** Mostly adequate, with two localized problems. Panel (b)'s y-axis has been stretched to ~0.13 while the data span only ±0.07, purely to make room for the legend — roughly 40% of the panel is empty and the data are compressed accordingly. Panels (i) and (l) carry ~40% empty plot width to the right of the last bar (defensible if the shared $i$-axis is deliberate for cross-column comparison, but worth a note in the caption so it reads as information, not as missing data).

**AXES:** Issues —
- Inconsistent y-limits within the eigen-taper row: (e), (g), (h) run to +0.2 while (f) tops out near +0.1. Panels placed side by side to be compared must share a scale, or the visual amplitude differences are artifacts. Same issue between (d) (−0.1 to 0.2) and its row-2 counterparts.
- All y-axes include zero; dB axes in row 4 are correctly peak-normalized to 0 at top (log scale, documented) — acceptable.
- No inverted axes. Tick decimal precision ("0.00", "0.05") is more than the data warrant; one decimal would do.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate for deterministic filter/kernel quantities.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(p) all present, uniform parenthetical style, uniform size, consistently placed top-left outside the axes. This is the strongest element of the layout.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Needs adjustment — the row-4 kernel panels are essentially square, but a frequency response with fine oscillatory structure (note the dense ripple in (p), which is nearly unreadable at this width) is a "time-series-like" object and benefits from a wider, shorter panel. Consider giving row 4 ~1.5:1 width:height, recovering the height from the over-tall bar panels in row 3, which have low information density.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — the only annotation elements are the three framed legends. The $|f|=R/2$ dotted verticals are good Tufte practice (reference lines rather than callouts) but are undistinguished from gridlines; give them a distinct weight/style/color and label one of them inline in panel (m) only.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — three detached, bordered legend boxes on line charts with 4 series each.
- (b): four well-separated curves ($f'N\Delta = 0..3$); the blue plateau and the distinct phases make inline endpoint labeling easy. Eliminating this box also lets the y-axis shrink back to the data range.
- (d)/(e): the $i=1..4$ eigen-tapers cross repeatedly, so inline labeling is harder — but one legend for the whole figure (placed once, unframed, e.g. in the empty upper region of panel (h) or as a figure-level key above the grid) replaces two boxes. If kept inline, label each taper near $k\approx 30$ where the curves are still ordered and separated, matching series color, with short leader lines.
- No legend currently overlaps plotted data (verified in b, d, e) — so no blocking legend violation.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Columns are unlabeled and the caption outsources their definition to another figure; the panel matrix cannot be read on its own.
- [BLOCKING] In row 4, the substantive $|f|=R/2$ reference lines and the purely decorative gridlines use an identical dotted style, so the reader cannot distinguish data ink from non-data ink.
- [ADVISORY] Inconsistent y-limits among (d)–(h) (panel (f) differs) undermine the cross-column comparison the layout invites.
- [ADVISORY] Duplicate $i=1..4$ legend in (d) and (e).
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b)'s y-range inflated to accommodate the legend, compressing the data.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant x-axis labels/ticks repeated 16 times across shared column domains.
- [ADVISORY] Four-sided frames and heavy filled/outlined bars depress the data-ink ratio.
- [ADVISORY] Dense ripple structure in (p) is under-resolved at the current square aspect ratio.
- [ADVISORY] Empty right portion of (i) and (l) may read as missing data rather than as a deliberately shared $i$-axis.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add four concise column headers above row 1 (the same labels used in Fig. 5) and state them in the caption so the figure stands alone.
- In row 4, delete the gridlines entirely and keep only the $|f|=R/2$ verticals, rendered as thin solid or dashed grey lines; annotate one of them inline in (m) as "$|f| = R/2$" and drop the note from the caption's parenthesis.
- Harmonize y-limits within rows 1–2 (use −0.12 to 0.20 for d–h) so amplitude comparisons are valid.
- Delete the legend in (d); keep a single unframed key, ideally as a figure-level legend above the grid or placed in the whitespace of one panel without a border, with labels colored to match the series.
- Replace (b)'s legend with inline endpoint labels in matching colors, then reset its y-limits to ±0.08.
- Remove top and right spines from all panels; leave only the L-frame.
- Label x-axes and x-ticks only on the bottom panel of each shared-domain block (row (d) for $k$, row (h) for $k$, row (l) for $i$, row (p) for $fN\Delta$) — or at minimum print the axis *name* once per column.
- Lighten (i)–(l): drop bar outlines, use a mid-grey or thin vertical lines/dot plot, and reduce y-tick precision to one decimal.
- Reallocate vertical space: compress row 3 slightly and widen/shorten row 4 to ~1.5:1 so the ripple in (p) resolves at publication width.
- Add one clause to the caption noting that the $i$-axis in (i)–(l) is fixed across columns so bar counts are directly comparable.

### figure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — Three legend boxes, each with a full rectangular border, account for a large share of the non-data ink. Every panel carries a complete four-sided frame; the top and right spines encode nothing and can be erased (Tufte's "erase non-data ink"). Panels (a) and (b) each carry a full "Frequency (Hz)" label plus identical 0–40 tick sets, which is redundant across a shared abscissa. Gridlines are appropriately light dotted and do not compete with the data — good. Panel (b) devotes roughly 60% of its vertical extent (≈10⁻¹³ to 10⁻⁵ dB) to empty space with no data in it, a low-yield use of plot area.

READING FLOW:     Natural — (a) spans the full width on top, then (b) left → (c) right. The reader proceeds top→bottom, left→right with no backtracking. The (b)→(c) transition (difference spectrum → convergence of that difference) is logically ordered.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Bordered legend boxes in all three panels (three separate lookup keys in one figure)
  - Full bounding boxes (top/right spines) on all three axes
  - Duplicate x-axis title and tick labels in (a) and (b) for the same variable and identical range
  - In (c), the magenta marker+line legend entry duplicates information that a single inline label would convey

WHITE SPACE:      Mixed — Adequate between panels; the horizontal gutter between (b) and (c) is generous but justified by (c)'s y-label. However, panel (b) contains a very large void between the two traces (≈8 decades of empty log axis), and there is excessive dead vertical space between panel (a)'s x-label and the second row.

AXES:             OK — Log ordinates in (b) and (c) and the log₂-spaced abscissa in (c) are legitimate (ratio/decade data), so the zero-baseline rule does not apply; dB ordinate in (a) is itself logarithmic. No inverted axes. Two notes: (1) panel (b)'s 15-decade range is far wider than the data require and invites a broken/split axis or two stacked sub-axes; (2) panel (c)'s abscissa is labeled Nₛ/N while the caption discusses Nₛ — align notation between caption and axis.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — The caption cites ν = 17 equivalent degrees of freedom, so a 95% CI half-width for the multitaper estimate is computable and could be shown (e.g., a single vertical CI "jackknife" bar in a corner of (a)). Not blocking, since the figure's claim is about agreement between estimators rather than about spectral uncertainty, but its absence should be a deliberate choice.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a), (b), (c) all present, all top-left, same weight and size, all outside the axes. Panel (a)'s label sits noticeably farther left of its axes than (b)'s and (c)'s do; nudge for exact alignment.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — (a) is wide, correct for a 0–40 Hz spectrum; (b) and (c) are near-square, correct for a broad-dynamic-range comparison and a 5-point convergence plot respectively.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — The three bordered legends are the only annotation elements; none overlap plotted data, but all three can be reduced to inline text (see below). No callouts, shadows, or fills.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — three detached, bordered legends.
  - (a): Justified in principle, because the three curves are deliberately coincident and cannot be labeled inline. Keep it, but drop the border and the background fill, and consider moving it below the axes or into the low-amplitude region above 28 Hz (where it already sits) as borderless text.
  - (b): The box sits in the empty band between the magenta and green traces. It should be deleted and replaced with inline labels — "Sliding sinc − multitaper" in magenta just below its trace near 20–25 Hz, and "Box − multitaper" in green just above its trace near 20–25 Hz. This eliminates the lookup cost entirely and fills otherwise dead space with information.
  - (c): The box's left edge sits very close to the descending magenta curve near Nₛ/N = 2; at final publication width this clearance will shrink and may clip the marker or the 1/Nₛ guide. Replace with "Maximum |difference|" in magenta beside the Nₛ/N = 8 point and "1/Nₛ" in black at the right end of the dotted guide, staggered vertically so the two labels do not crowd near the same y.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Detached bordered legend boxes on line/convergence plots in (b) and (c); both are eliminable by inline labeling.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend box in (c) has minimal clearance from the magenta curve and the 1/Nₛ guide line near Nₛ/N = 2; risk of overlap when scaled to column width.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant abscissa: identical "Frequency (Hz)" title and 0–40 tick set in (a) and (b).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) wastes ~8 decades of empty log axis between the two traces.
  - [ADVISORY] Full bounding boxes on all panels; top and right spines are non-data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] No uncertainty indication anywhere despite ν = 17 being stated in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Axis notation Nₛ/N in (c) versus Nₛ in the caption text.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the (b) and (c) legends; place series labels directly on the traces in matching colors, with short thin leader lines only where needed. Stagger the (c) labels vertically.
  - Strip borders and fills from the surviving (a) legend; render it as three lines of borderless colored text.
  - Remove top and right spines on all three panels; keep the light dotted gridlines as they are.
  - Compress panel (b)'s ordinate: either a broken axis between ~10⁻¹² and ~10⁻² dB, or two thin stacked sub-panels sharing one abscissa. Reclaiming that void lets the panel shrink vertically and tightens the whole figure.
  - Consider re-laying out as a shared-x column: widen (b) to match (a)'s width and stack them with a single "Frequency (Hz)" label and one tick set, moving (c) to its own row or inset. If the current 1-over-2 layout is kept, at minimum delete (a)'s x tick labels and title, since (b) reproduces them.
  - Reduce the vertical gap between the first and second rows.
  - Add a single vertical 95% CI bar (from ν = 17) in a quiet corner of (a), or state explicitly in the caption why no CI is drawn.
  - Align panel (a)'s "(a)" label to the same left offset from the axes used by (b) and (c).
  - Make the (c) abscissa label and the caption notation agree (use Nₛ/N in both, or Nₛ in both).

### figure2.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — (1) Every one of the eight panels carries a complete four-sided frame; the top and right spines encode nothing and, multiplied by eight, add a substantial grid of non-data ink. (2) x-tick labels (0, 0.1 … 0.5) are printed on all eight panels even though the four columns are vertically aligned and share an identical x-range; the top row's labels are redundant with the bottom row's. (3) Panel (e) is a solid slab of dark green — the kernel's rapid oscillations fully saturate the fill between −50 and −150 dB, so ~40% of the panel area is ink conveying only "oscillations reach the floor." The envelope plus a light fill (or a thinner line / reduced alpha) would convey the same fact with a fraction of the ink. (4) The zoom-indicator rectangles in (g) and (h) are drawn in heavy solid black and are visually louder than the green kernel they mark. Gridlines are appropriately light dotted — good.

READING FLOW:     Natural — (a)→(d) across the top, (e)→(h) beneath, with each kernel sitting directly under the estimator it explains. The column correspondence is the figure's strongest design feature. Insets are uniformly top-left with a leader to the boxed region, so the eye path within each lower panel is consistent.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Top and right spines on all eight panels (8 × 2 = 16 redundant rules).
  - Duplicated x-tick labels on the top row.
  - Full boxes around each inset, doubling the frame count in the lower row.
  - Heavy black zoom rectangles in (g), (h) competing with the data.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate, with one issue — the top row's y-limit of +60 dB leaves ~15 dB of empty headroom above the tallest peak (~45 dB) in panels (b)–(d); only (a) uses that space, for the legend. Tightening to −40…+50 would enlarge the data region by roughly 10%. Lower row spacing is well judged.

AXES:             OK — all quantities are in dB (log measure) and the kernels are normalized to their own peak, so a non-zero origin is scientifically justified and needs no defense. Ranges are shared within each row, y-labels appear only on the leftmost panel, and no axis is inverted. Two minor points: the inset x-label "0.39" in panel (e) sits hard against the inset's left edge and appears to touch/clip the frame; and the inset in (h) uses a different x-range (0.36–0.44) from the other three insets (0.39–0.41), which is necessary but is not flagged visually, so a reader may mis-compare main-lobe widths across (e)–(h).

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate for single-realization spectra; the uncertainty is instead quantified as equivalent degrees of freedom (ν = 2, 2, 16.7, 98.4) in the caption, which is the correct convention for this field.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a) through (h) all present, all outside the axes at upper-left, identical parenthesized style and size.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — each subpanel is close to square (~520 × 550 px), suitable for these single-decade frequency traces; the 4 × 2 arrangement keeps the estimator/kernel pairing vertically adjacent, which is worth more than per-panel width optimization here.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — the insets are genuine data, not annotation, and are well placed; but their frames plus the two leader lines plus the source rectangle make three graphical elements per zoom. Reduce to a hairline inset frame and a hairline rectangle. No text box overlaps plotted data.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — a two-entry legend box ("Estimate", "True PSD") floats in the upper-right of panel (a). It does not overlap the data (the periodogram peaks near 45 dB, the box sits above 48 dB), so this is advisory, not blocking. With only two series and a 2 pt-wide dashed blue curve that is unmistakable against a thin black trace, the lookup cost can be eliminated entirely by labeling inline. Panel (d) is the best host: there the black estimate and the blue true PSD separate cleanly near fΔ ≈ 0.25–0.35, allowing "estimate" in black just above the solid curve and "true PSD" in blue just below the dashed one.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend box on panel (a) for only two series; recommend inline color-matched labels instead.
  - [ADVISORY] In panel (a) the blue dashed true PSD is largely buried under the dense black periodogram between fΔ = 0.15 and 0.5 — the reference curve, which is the whole point of the comparison, is the least visible element. Draw the true PSD last (on top) in all four upper panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (e)'s solid saturated fill destroys the data-ink ratio and hides the kernel's oscillatory structure that panel (f) makes visible; the two panels are therefore not visually comparable despite being the same kind of object.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant x-tick labels on the top row (columns share the bottom row's identical axis).
  - [ADVISORY] Top/right spines on all eight panels and heavy black zoom rectangles in (g), (h).
  - [ADVISORY] Unused headroom to +60 dB in panels (b)–(d).
  - [ADVISORY] Inset in (h) spans 0.36–0.44 while (e)–(g) span 0.39–0.41; the change of magnification is not signposted and invites false width comparisons.
  - [ADVISORY] Inset x-tick label "0.39" in (e) appears clipped against the inset frame.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the legend; label the two curves inline in panel (d) where they diverge, black text on the estimate and blue text on the dashed true PSD, offset ~4 dB vertically so neither clips its curve at final column width.
  - Raise the z-order of the true PSD above the estimate in (a)–(d), or plot it in a slightly lighter blue with a white halo, so it reads through the noise.
  - Replot (e) with a hairline (0.5 pt) stroke and no fill, or overlay the analytic envelope; if the fill is retained, use ~25% alpha so the internal oscillation density remains legible.
  - Remove top and right spines throughout; reduce inset frames and zoom rectangles to hairlines in the series color rather than solid black.
  - Suppress x-tick labels on the top row (keep the ticks) and rely on the aligned bottom-row axis; this also recovers vertical space.
  - Tighten the upper-row y-limits to −40…+50 dB once the legend is removed.
  - Either match the inset x-range in (h) to the others, or annotate the inset with its span (e.g., a small "×4 wider" note or explicit mention in the caption) so main-lobe widths are not mis-read.
  - Nudge the inset tick labels inward, or drop the first inset x-tick, so "0.39"/"0.36" no longer touches the frame.

### figure3.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — (1) Both panels are enclosed in full four-sided frames; the top and right spines carry no information and duplicate the axis ranges. (2) Panel (b) has a full dotted grid in both directions; the grid competes with, and uses the same line style as, the two dotted vertical reference lines that *do* encode data (peak width, peak separation). (3) Panel (b)'s legend is a bordered box in otherwise empty plotting area. (4) Panel (a) has no grid while (b) has a dense one — inconsistent non-data ink treatment across panels. (5) The y-range of (b) extends to 8 dB while the largest datum is 6.1 dB; ~25% of the panel height is empty, existing only to host the legend box.

READING FLOW:     Natural — vertical (a)→(b) stack reads top→bottom; within (b) the decomposition (noise falling, bias rising, total) reads left→right as intended. One backtrack: the circled markers in (b) send the reader out to another figure and then to the caption to learn which circle is which panel.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Full bounding boxes on both panels (top/right spines).
  - Dense dotted grid in (b), heavier in visual weight than the thin blue/red curves it underlies.
  - Legend border box in (b).
  - Dashed taper envelope in (a) drawn "scaled to the record's peak amplitude": it is decorative in amplitude (its y-value is arbitrary), so it borrows the data axis without encoding a value on it.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, with two local problems — excessive empty band at the top of panel (b) (y = 6.5–8.5 dB), and a wide unused margin to the left of the (a)/(b) panel labels.

AXES:             OK — both axes in (b) begin at zero; (a) is symmetric about zero, appropriate for a zero-mean record. No inverted axes. Minor: (a)'s x tick set (0, 255, 511, 767, 1023) is defensible for a 1024-point record, but 0/256/512/768/1024 would read more cleanly; (b)'s upper y limit is not justified by the data.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — and explicitly addressed in the caption ("the curves are ensemble averages and carry no error bars"). This satisfies the journal requirement; no action needed, though a note of the 300-realization ensemble size in the axis label or panel is a cheap alternative.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a) and (b) both upper-left, outside the axes, same size and style.

ASPECT RATIO:     Needs adjustment — panel (a) is a 1024-sample time series and would benefit from being wider and shorter (it currently reads as roughly 2.2:1; 3:1 or more would separate the taper envelope from the oscillation envelope). Panel (b) is close to square, which is appropriate for a trade-off/decomposition plot; the stacked layout means (a) is forced to (b)'s width.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — the two dotted vertical lines are unlabeled, so their meaning lives only in the caption; the three circled markers likewise. Neither uses heavy fills or overlaps data (good), but both require caption lookup.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — a detached, bordered four-entry legend in the upper right of (b). It does not overlap the data (the dashed peak-region curve plateaus at ~5.5 dB, below the box), but it forces a lookup between marker style and curve, and it is the reason the y-axis is padded to 8 dB. All four series are well separated at the right edge (dashed ≈5.5, solid ≈5.0 and rising, red ≈5.0, blue ≈0.75), so direct labeling is straightforward.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend box on a four-series line chart; replace with inline labels at curve endpoints.
  - [ADVISORY] The dotted grid uses the same stroke style as the two dotted vertical reference lines, so a reader cannot distinguish decoration from data annotation.
  - [ADVISORY] Grid weight equals or exceeds the weight of the blue (noise) and red (bias) curves in the right half of (b), where the blue curve is nearly flat and gets lost among the horizontal gridlines.
  - [ADVISORY] Unlabeled circled markers and unlabeled vertical lines require the reader to leave the figure for the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) y-axis padded to 8 dB with no data above 6.1 dB; ~25% empty panel area.
  - [ADVISORY] Full bounding boxes on both panels; non-data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] In panel (a), the red tapered record overplots and largely conceals the black untapered record in the interior, so the only visible difference is at the two ends; the comparison the panel is meant to make is hard to see at publication size.
  - [ADVISORY] The dashed taper is plotted on the amplitude axis at an arbitrary scale, which invites a false reading of its y-values.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the legend in (b). Label at the right edge, each in its series colour and matching line style: "Bias" beside the red curve (~5.0 dB), "Total, whole band" beside the solid black (~5.0 dB, stagger vertically or use a short thin leader line to separate it from the red label), "Total, peak region" beside the dashed curve (~5.5 dB), "Noise" beside the blue curve (~0.75 dB). Because black/red converge near x = 64, place "Total, whole band" at x ≈ 45 above the solid curve with a short black leader line rather than at the endpoint.
  - With the legend gone, cut the y-range of (b) to 0–6.5 dB and reclaim the vertical space, or give the reclaimed height to panel (a).
  - Remove the dotted grid entirely, or reduce it to light grey horizontal lines only at major y ticks, drawn thinner than the thinnest data curve. Then change the two reference lines to a distinct, heavier style (e.g. thin solid grey) so they read as annotation, not grid.
  - Label the two vertical reference lines inline, rotated and set small near the top of the panel: "peak width" at RNΔ ≈ 7 and "peak separation" at RNΔ ≈ 30. This removes two caption lookups.
  - Label the three circled markers directly with the corresponding panel letters of the motif figure — small italic "b", "c", "d" set just outside each circle — so the cross-reference resolves in the figure.
  - Remove the top and right spines on both panels; keep only left and bottom axes with inward ticks.
  - In panel (a), avoid overplotting: either offset the untapered and tapered records vertically into two stacked traces sharing one x-axis, or plot the untapered record in light grey behind the red tapered record so the difference is legible across the whole span, not just at the ends.
  - Draw the taper as a thin envelope on a separate right-hand axis scaled 0–1, or annotate it inline ("25% cosine taper, scaled to peak amplitude") so its arbitrary amplitude is unambiguous.
  - Widen panel (a) relative to its height (target ≈3:1) so the 1024-sample structure and the tapered roll-off regions are distinguishable.
  - Optionally note the ensemble size inline in (b) (e.g. small grey text "300 realizations" in the lower right) so the panel is self-contained.
---

### figure4.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — Several sources of non-data ink:
- All four panels carry a complete four-sided frame with mirrored ticks on the top and right spines. The top/right spines and their ticks encode nothing; two-sided (left + bottom) axes would recover ink.
- Dotted gridlines appear in (b), (c) and (d) but not in (a). The grids in (b) and (c) are dense (every 0.1 in y, every 1 in x in (b)) and in places are visually comparable in weight to the low-amplitude tails of the curves they underlie (e.g. the near-zero tails in (b) and (c) sit on top of a gridline and become hard to read). Either drop the grid entirely or lighten it and reduce to a few reference values.
- Three bordered legend boxes ((a), (c), (d)) plus one in (b) — four framed lookup tables in a figure whose series are individually labelable.
- Panel (d): y-axis decades are ticked and labeled from 10⁻¹⁵ to 10⁰ but the data occupy only 10⁻¹⁰ to 10⁻¹. Roughly one-third of the panel is empty axis with labeled-but-unused ticks — redundant axis ink and wasted area.
- Panel (a): y range −0.20 to 0.20 while data span only −0.11 to +0.13; ~40 % of the panel height carries no data.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — (a)→(b)→(c)→(d) reads left→right, top→bottom, and the argument (tapers → individual kernels → running sum → eigenvalue ladder) follows that order. Minor friction: (b) and (c) share the identical abscissa *fNΔ* with identical limits (−8…8) but are diagonal to each other, so the reader must jump across the figure to compare band edges. Consider placing the two *fNΔ* panels in the same column (or same row) so the shared axis can be aligned and the duplicate axis label/tick set eliminated.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Mirrored top/right tick marks on all four panels.
- Legend frames (4×), all removable in favor of direct labels.
- Dense dotted grid in (b)/(c) that competes with the near-zero tails of the plotted kernels.
- Labeled empty decades (10⁻¹¹…10⁻¹⁵) in (d).

**WHITE SPACE:** Mixed — Excessive within panels (a) and (d) (unused axis range) and in the upper third of (c); the (c) legend is itself crowded, packing six entries into a 4-column box.

**AXES:** Issues —
- (a) y-limits are symmetric and include zero: OK, but over-wide for the data.
- (b), (c) begin at zero: OK.
- (d) log ordinate is justified by the ~9-decade range of 1−λᵢ, but the limits should be truncated to ~10⁻¹⁰…10⁰ rather than 10⁻¹⁵.
- (d) abscissa: tick labels are even integers only (2,4,…,16) while the data are at every integer *i* including i = 1 and i = 17; minor ticks at odd *i* (or labeling 1,3,5,…) would let the reader locate i = 7 and i = 8 — the two values the vertical reference lines are about — without counting.
- No inverted axes. No unexplained non-zero baselines.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate; these are deterministic quantities.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(d) present, all upper-left, same parenthesized style and weight. One nit: in (a) and (c) the label sits immediately adjacent to the topmost y tick label ("0.20", "0.200") and nearly collides with it; nudge the labels left/up or into the panel corner.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — 2×2 grid of near-4:3 panels suits the single-peaked/banded functions; none of these are long time series requiring extra width.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — The vertical reference lines are used well (dotted ±R/2 in (b), ±4 in (c), L = 2α−1 and 2α in (d)) and none overlap data destructively. However, in (d) the two reference lines are explained by a bordered legend in the lower right; these should be labeled in situ ("L = 2α−1", "2α") at the top of each line, which removes the box entirely. In (c) the dashed "Box" outline is a data element but is referenced through the legend rather than labeled at its own shoulder.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (all four panels) — none currently overlaps data, but all four are lookup boxes on curve plots with 3–6 series and are replaceable:
- (a) 3 series, well separated at k ≈ 20–60: label h⁽¹⁾, h⁽²⁾, h⁽⁷⁾ directly at their first peaks in matching colors.
- (b) 3 series: the black i = 1 peak at f = 0, blue i = 2 at its right lobe (fNΔ ≈ 1.2), green i = 3 at its outer lobe (≈ −1.9) are all uncrowded label sites.
- (c) 6 series, nested plateaus: label L = 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, N at the right-hand flank of each plateau (they are vertically ordered, so labels stagger naturally); label "Box" at the dashed shoulder near fNΔ = −4. This is the single largest ink saving available.
- (d) 2 reference lines only: direct-label as above.
No inline labels are present yet, so no label/curve collisions to report.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Four bordered legend boxes where 2–6 series per panel could be labeled inline; the (c) legend is a 4-column block occupying the top ~12 % of the panel.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (d) ordinate extends five decades (10⁻¹¹–10⁻¹⁵) below the smallest datum; wasted panel area and labeled ticks with no referents.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) ordinate range (±0.20) is ~1.7× the data range; compress to ≈ ±0.13.
- [ADVISORY] Gridlines present in (b), (c), (d) but absent in (a) — inconsistent treatment across panels of one figure; in (b)/(c) the dotted grid is heavy enough to compete with the near-zero kernel tails, the very feature the panel is about (leakage/gap-filling).
- [ADVISORY] Mirrored top/right spines and ticks on all panels are non-data ink.
- [ADVISORY] Color semantics are inconsistent across panels: red = 7th taper in (a), red = L = 5 in (c), red dashed = 2α in (d); green = i = 3 in (b) but L = 3 in (c); black = i = 1 in (a)/(b) but L = N in (c). A reader tracking "the 7th taper" from (a) to (c)/(d) has no color continuity.
- [ADVISORY] In (c), the magenta L = 9 curve is almost entirely hidden beneath the thick black L = N curve, so a labeled series is effectively invisible — the reader cannot verify the claim that L = 9 already approaches the box. Consider a thin dashed magenta over a slightly thinner black, or an inset showing the L = 7/9/N shoulder region.
- [ADVISORY] Shared abscissa *fNΔ* is drawn twice, in diagonally opposite panels, preventing visual alignment of the band edges at ±4 between (b) and (c).
- [ADVISORY] Panel labels (a) and (c) nearly touch the top y tick labels.
- [ADVISORY] Caption/axis mismatch: the caption writes the panel-(b) quantity as |H⁽ⁱ⁾(f)|²/Δ and the panel-(c) quantity with a 1/2αΔ normalization, whereas the axes read |H⁽ⁱ⁾(f)|²/NΔ² and 𝒦(f)/NΔ. Reconcile caption and axis labels so the normalizations agree exactly.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete all four legend boxes; place colored inline labels at the positions noted above (h⁽¹⁾/h⁽²⁾/h⁽⁷⁾ at their peaks in (a); i = 1,2,3 at their distinct lobes in (b); L = 1,3,5,7,9,N at the right flank of each plateau plus "Box" at the dashed shoulder in (c); "L = 2α−1" and "2α" at the top of their respective vertical lines in (d)). Add short thin leader lines in the series color anywhere a label would otherwise sit within ~1.5 text-heights of a curve.
- Truncate (d) y-limits to ≈ [10⁻¹⁰, 10⁰] and (a) y-limits to ≈ [−0.13, 0.13]; reclaim the freed height for the data.
- Drop top and right spines and all mirrored ticks; keep left/bottom axes only.
- Either remove the dotted grids or reduce them to a light gray at ~0.4 pt and to a sparse set of reference values (e.g. y = 0 and the band edges), ensuring no gridline is heavier than the thinnest data curve.
- Harmonize colors across panels so a given taper index keeps one color throughout (e.g. reserve black/blue/green for i = 1,2,3 everywhere, use a distinct gray dashed for the box and a single accent for reference lines).
- Re-render L = 9 as a thin dashed magenta above a 1.2 pt black L = N so both are readable where they coincide; optionally add a small zoomed inset of the shoulder at fNΔ ≈ 3.5–4.5.
- Rearrange to put the two *fNΔ* panels in one column with a single shared x-axis and one x label, removing the duplicated axis and easing the ±R/2 comparison between individual kernels and the running sum.
- Add minor ticks at every integer *i* in (d) (or label odd integers) so i = 7 and i = 8 are directly readable.
- Nudge the (a) and (c) panel letters clear of the top tick labels.
- Bring the caption's normalizations into exact agreement with the printed axis labels.

### figure5.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — Every one of the eight panels is enclosed in a complete four-sided frame with inward ticks on all sides; the top and right spines encode nothing and could be erased (or reduced to Tufte's open L-axis). In the bottom row, the identical y-axis (0.00–0.20) is tick-labeled four times even though (e)–(h) share the same scale and the same y-axis label ($c_i/\sum c_i$) is written only once — the three right-hand tick-label sets are redundant data ink. The bars are filled solid dark navy, a large ink mass for seven-to-twelve scalar values; a lighter fill or thinner bars would carry the same information. The two legend boxes (b, d) each add a border and two columns of keys that direct labeling could replace.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — the column-wise pairing (a→e, b→f, c→g, d→h) is well exploited by the aligned 4×2 grid, and the left-to-right ordering matches the caption. One backtrack exists: panel (c) plots four colored curves with no key at all, forcing the reader to look back to the legend of (b) and guess whether the color mapping carries over (it apparently does not — (c) shows window positions, not $f'N\Delta$ values). That is a genuine jump-and-guess.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Bordered legend boxes in (b) and (d).
- Top and right spines plus their mirrored tick marks on all eight panels.
- Repeated y-tick labels in (f), (g), (h) duplicating (e).
- Empty right-hand thirds of (e) and (h) enclosed by frame, drawing a box around nothing.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall; locally Excessive in (e) and (h), where the bars stop at $i=7$ but the frame runs to $i\approx12$. The shared x-range is defensible (it makes ladder length directly comparable across e–h), but it should be stated in the caption so the blank region reads as information ("the ladder terminates") rather than as missing data.

**AXES:** Issues —
- Tick-label formatting is inconsistent: (a) uses 0.20/0.15, (d) uses 0.2/0.1, (c) uses 0.0/0.5/1.0. Use one decimal convention per figure.
- In (c) the green and cyan curves reach exactly 1.0 and are clipped by the top spine; the peak of a normalized quantity should not sit on the frame. Extend ylim to ~1.05.
- (e)–(h) x-axis ticks appear only at 5 and 10 while the data are integer indices 1…12; a reader cannot tell whether the first bar is $i=0$ or $i=1$. Tick or label $i=1$.
- Zero-baseline convention: satisfied in (e)–(h) (bars from 0). In (a)–(d) the y-ranges include zero and the signed data justify the ranges — OK.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate, these are deterministic constructions.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, outside upper-left, same weight and size, parenthesized uniformly.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — panels are near-square to slightly wide, suitable for the oscillatory sequences in $k$ and for the short bar ladders. No adjustment needed.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — the only boxed elements are the two legends. Neither overlaps data (blue flat-top in (b) peaks at ~0.068, well below the legend; curves in (d) peak at ~0.125, below the legend block), so this is not blocking, but both are removable.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — two detached, bordered legends in (b) and (d); panel (c) has no legend despite four distinguishable series. In (b) the four curves are cleanly separated near $k\approx90$–110 and could be labeled inline ($f'N\Delta=0,1,2,3$) in matching color at their own crest. In (d) the four Slepian tapers each have an isolated extremum ($i=1$ crest near $k=128$, $i=4$ crest near $k=40$) suitable for an inline colored label with a short leader line where curves crowd.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (c) has four colored curves with no legend, no inline labels, and no color definition in the caption. The reader cannot map color to the four sinc-window positions, and the adjacent legend in (b) invites a false mapping.
- [ADVISORY] Detached bordered legends in (b) and (d) impose a lookup cost that inline labeling would eliminate; the borders are non-data ink.
- [ADVISORY] Data clipped at the frame in (c): green and cyan peaks sit exactly on the upper spine.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant y-tick labels in (f), (g), (h); redundant top/right spines and mirrored ticks on all panels.
- [ADVISORY] Inconsistent decimal formatting of y-tick labels between (a), (c) and (d).
- [ADVISORY] x-ticks in (e)–(h) omit $i=1$, leaving the index origin ambiguous.
- [ADVISORY] The four $\nu$ values (13.4, 16.7, 17.2, 14.0) live only in the caption prose; they are the quantitative point of the bottom row and are not readable from the graphic.
- [ADVISORY] Red/green are used as distinct series in (b), (c) and (d) — the least distinguishable pair for the ~8% of readers with deuteranomaly. The yellow curve in (a) is also low-contrast on white.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Label the four curves in (c) directly, in matching color, with the window position (e.g. $n=0$, $n=64$, $n=128$, $n=192$ or whatever the four offsets are), and state the mapping in the caption.
- Delete the legend boxes in (b) and (d); place colored inline labels at each curve's crest, using a thin same-color leader line where neighbors crowd (e.g. $i=2$ and $i=3$ in (d) near $k\approx60$–100). Stagger vertically so no label sits on another curve.
- Erase the top and right spines and mirrored ticks on all eight panels.
- Keep y-tick labels only on (e); remove them from (f)–(h) and let the shared axis do the work. This buys horizontal room for the bottom row.
- Print $\nu$ inside each of (e)–(h) as small inline text in a corner of the empty upper region ("$\nu=13.4$", etc.) so the flatness claim is visible in the graphic, not just the caption.
- Extend (c) ylim to about 1.05 so the normalized peaks clear the frame.
- Unify tick-label decimals (two decimals throughout, or one throughout) and add an $i=1$ tick to (e)–(h).
- Replace red/green with a color-blind-safe set (e.g. blue / orange / green / purple) and drop yellow from (a) in favor of a darker hue; the six shifted Hann copies in (a) could alternatively be drawn in a single color since they are the *same* window — the color variation there encodes nothing but position, which the horizontal offset already shows.
- State in the caption that (e)–(h) share a common $i$-range so the blank right portion of (e) and (h) is read as a terminated ladder.

### figure6.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The data themselves (curves, kernels, insets) are dense and well drawn, but there is a substantial amount of non-data ink: (i) full four-sided bounding boxes on all eight panels plus four inset boxes; (ii) dotted gridlines at every 0.1 in *f*Δ and every 20 dB in all eight panels, which in (f) and (h) cross fine oscillatory structure and compete with it; (iii) y-tick labels and x-tick labels repeated identically in all four columns of each row, although each row shares one scale; (iv) redundant color encoding — green/black/red/magenta for "Estimate" carries no information not already given by panel position, and the top row's y-range (−40 to +60 dB) leaves roughly a third of each panel empty above and below the data (max ≈ 43 dB, min ≈ −22 dB).

**READING FLOW:** Natural — Method (a–d) above its corresponding kernel (e–h), columns left→right, estimates over kernels top→bottom. The one-to-one column correspondence is the figure's strongest design feature. Minor friction: the numeric characterizations that distinguish the columns (ν, half-power width, leakage fraction, median absolute error) live only in the caption, so the reader must jump text↔figure repeatedly to know which method each column is.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Gridlines in all eight panels, at the same or greater visual weight than the thinnest data traces (notably the kernel skirts in (e) and (g)).
- Four-sided axis frames on all panels (two sides suffice).
- Four different estimate colors where one would do; color is redundant with panel label and creates a red/green pairing (c) vs (a) that is problematic for color-deficient readers.
- Duplicate tick labels across the four columns of each row.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall, but unevenly distributed — excessive empty vertical band in the top row (data occupy roughly −22 to +43 dB inside a −40 to +60 dB frame) and a wide gap between each panel label and its axes; slightly crowded in (e–h) where the insets, their zoom rectangles, and the leader lines sit close to the kernel peaks.

**AXES:** OK — Both rows are in dB (a logarithmic measure), so a zero origin is neither required nor meaningful; no axes inverted; frequency axes start at 0 and are identical across columns, which is essential for the intended comparison. Two issues worth noting: the top row's limits are wider than the data require, and only the bottom row carries the *f*Δ axis label while the top row's tick labels are retained in full — an inconsistency (either label both rows or drop the top row's redundant numerals, since the panels are not vertically stacked on a shared axis).

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — Appropriate here (single realization is stated), but the figure conveys no uncertainty at all; the equivalent degrees of freedom ν, which *is* the uncertainty statement, appears only in the caption. Not blocking, since the caption defines the realization and ν explicitly.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) present on every panel, all top-left, outside the axes, same size and parenthetical style.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — Each panel is approximately square (~520 × 550 px), suitable for spectra plotted over a fixed 0–0.5 band; the 4 × 2 grid preserves the vertical method↔kernel pairing.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — The inset zoom boxes in (e–h) are legitimate data annotation: each small rectangle marks the magnified region and thin dashed leader lines connect it to the inset. None overlaps the plotted curve it annotates, and none carries fill or shadow. The inset frames could be reduced to two-sided axes to shed non-data ink.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — A detached two-entry legend box sits in the upper-right of panel (a) only. It does not overlap data (it occupies the empty region above the 43 dB peak), but its green "Estimate" swatch is valid *only* for panel (a); panels (b), (c) and (d) plot their estimates in black, red and magenta with no key anywhere in the figure. The reader must infer that the differently colored traces are all "Estimate."

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The single legend in (a) mis-keys the rest of the figure: it defines "Estimate" as a green solid line, yet the estimates in (b), (c) and (d) are black, red and magenta with no corresponding key. As drawn, the legend is incorrect for three of the four panels it appears to govern.
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend box on a two-series line chart; the lookup cost can be eliminated entirely by labeling the two curves inline in panel (a).
- [ADVISORY] Gridlines are of comparable or greater weight than the finest data traces and cross the dense oscillatory structure in (f) and (h), reducing legibility at publication width.
- [ADVISORY] Top-row y-limits (−40 to +60 dB) are wider than the data span, wasting roughly a third of the plotting area in four panels and shrinking the visible difference between the four estimates.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant, non-informative color variation across (a)–(d); includes a red/green contrast that is inaccessible to color-deficient readers.
- [ADVISORY] Duplicate y- and x-tick labels in all four columns of each row, and full four-sided frames on all twelve axes (eight panels + four insets).
- [ADVISORY] Method identity and the key numbers (ν, half-power width, leakage percentage, median absolute error) are caption-only, forcing repeated figure↔caption jumps for a figure whose entire purpose is a four-way comparison.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete the legend box in (a). Plot all four estimates in a single color (black or dark gray) and keep the true PSD blue dashed everywhere; then place two inline labels in panel (a) only — "Estimate" in black beside the green/black trace around *f*Δ ≈ 0.35 (where the curve is flat), and "True PSD" in blue immediately below the dashed curve at *f*Δ ≈ 0.45, each color-matched to its series with a 3–4 pt vertical offset so neither clips the line when the figure is scaled to column width. If distinct colors per panel must be retained, add a matching inline label in every one of (a)–(d).
- Add a short inline method tag in the empty upper-left of each top panel: "box-smoothed periodogram, ν = 17.2", "multitaper, L = 7, ν = 14.0", "cosine taper + parabola, ν = 16.7", "Welch, 9 Hann segments, ν = 17.6". This replaces four caption lookups with direct labels and uses space that is currently blank.
- Reduce gridlines to a lighter gray (e.g., 0.5 pt, 25–30% gray) or drop them entirely in (f) and (h), where they interfere with the oscillatory kernel; alternatively keep only the single horizontal reference at 0 dB (top row) and at −50 dB (bottom row), which are the levels actually discussed in the caption.
- Tighten the top-row y-limits to about −30 to +50 dB, which contains all four estimates and the true PSD while increasing data density by roughly 30%.
- Remove the top and right spines on all eight panels and the insets; keep left and bottom only.
- Suppress y-tick labels in columns 2–4 of each row and keep the single y-axis label per row; suppress x-tick numerals on the top row (or add the *f*Δ label to the top row if the numerals are kept), so each scale is stated once.
- Consider annotating the two quantitative claims made in the caption directly on the plots: a thin horizontal reference line with inline text "levels off ≈ 20 dB high" in (a) and "≈ 10 dB high" in (b), and "−22 dB skirt" beside the multitaper kernel plateau in (f). Each replaces a sentence of caption prose with a mark at the value it describes.
- Move the panel labels closer to the top-left corner of each axes box to recover the gap now separating label from panel.

### figure7.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — the core data ink is excellent (thin, unfilled curves, no shading, no 3D, no shadows), but non-data ink accumulates in three places: (i) full four-sided bounding boxes on both panels — the top and right spines encode nothing and can be erased; (ii) two framed legend boxes, each with a border that competes with the thin curves it describes; (iii) dense dotted gridlines on both axes in both panels (~8 vertical × 8–9 horizontal per panel). The gridlines are appropriately light and do not out-weigh the data, but in panel (a) the mesh runs through the busiest part of the spectrum and could be reduced to the vertical 5 Hz lines only, or dropped entirely in favour of the tick marks.

READING FLOW:     Natural — vertically stacked panels with a single shared x-axis, estimate above, residual below; the eye moves top→bottom and left→right without backtracking. Suppressing the x-tick labels on (a) is correct and avoids redundant ink.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Legend frame boxes in both panels (borders are ornament; the colour keys carry all the information).
  - Top and right axis spines on both panels.
  - Full dotted grid mesh, especially the horizontal lines in panel (a).
  - Partial redundancy between the two legends: panel (b) restates "Recipe (b′)" and "Welch, 9 Hann segments" already established in (a); "− multitaper" could live in the y-axis label ("Difference from multitaper (dB)") instead, shrinking the legend to two short colour keys — or eliminating it.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, with one pocket of waste — panel (b) spans ±15 dB while the data occupy roughly −5.5 to +12.5 dB, leaving an empty band below −6 dB that is only partly justified by the legend sitting there. Panel (a) is appropriately open in the upper-right, and the legend uses that void sensibly.

AXES:             OK — panel (a) is in dB (a logarithmic power scale), so a non-zero origin is scientifically documented and correct; panel (b) is difference data centred on zero with a solid zero reference line, which is the right convention. No inverted axes. Tick labelling is clean and non-redundant. Minor: panel (b)'s symmetric ±15 range could be tightened to about −7 to +13 to recover dead space, provided zero stays a visible reference.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — none are required for spectral estimates of this kind, but note that the caption's central quantitative claim ("the medians are smaller than the estimators' own noise, 1.5–1.6 dB") is asserted in text and never shown. This is a missed opportunity: the claim is graphable.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a) and (b) are both present, both at the upper-left outside the plot frame, same size and style.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — each panel is roughly 1.6:1 (wider than tall), correct for a frequency-series display; the tall overall figure is simply the consequence of stacking two such panels and reads as a single-column figure. If column width permits, widening slightly would further separate the closely spaced harmonic peaks below 15 Hz.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — no callouts or text bubbles are used, which is good. The only boxed elements are the two legends (see below). The caption mentions "the valleys between harmonics" as the locus of the largest differences; a single short inline note or a light shaded band on one representative valley in (b) would carry that argument visually without a box.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — two detached, framed legends. Neither overlaps plotted data: in (a) the box occupies roughly 22–40 Hz above 36 dB where the spectrum does not exceed ~28 dB, and in (b) it occupies −9 to −14 dB where the residuals do not go below ~−5.5 dB. So this is ADVISORY, not blocking. However, in panel (a) the bottom edge of the box sits only a few dB above the ~35.5 dB peak near 22.5 Hz; at reduced publication width that clearance will look tight. Inline labelling is the Tufte-preferred replacement, but here the three curves are deliberately near-coincident for most of the band, so naive endpoint labels would collide. The workable version is to label each curve at a place where they separate — e.g., in a deep valley around 27–29 Hz in (a), where black dips well below red and magenta — using text in the matching series colour plus a short thin leader line of the same colour. Failing that, keep a legend but remove its frame and set the key text in the series colours.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Framed legend boxes on both panels impose a lookup cost and add non-data ink; the frames are pure ornament.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend in panel (a) has minimal vertical clearance above the ~35.5 dB peak near 22.5 Hz; will read as crowded when scaled to column width.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant legend content between panels: the two series are identified twice, and "− multitaper" belongs in the y-axis label.
  - [ADVISORY] Top and right spines on both panels contribute no information.
  - [ADVISORY] Dotted horizontal gridlines in panel (a) cross the densest data region without aiding any specific comparison.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) y-range (±15 dB) is wider than the data require, wasting roughly a fifth of the panel height.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption's key quantitative claim — that differences lie within the estimators' own noise (~1.5–1.6 dB) — is invisible in the graphic.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a light grey horizontal band at ±1.6 dB in panel (b), drawn behind the curves, labelled inline "estimator noise, ±1.6 dB". This converts the caption's central claim into something the reader sees immediately and lets the median/95th-percentile numbers be read off the figure.
  - Erase the top and right spines on both panels; keep left and bottom only, with ticks pointing outward.
  - Remove both legend frames. In panel (a), label the three curves inline in their own colours in the separated valley near 27–29 Hz (or near 31–32 Hz), each with a short thin leader line of matching colour, staggered vertically so the three labels do not crowd the same y-position.
  - Rename panel (b)'s y-axis "Difference from multitaper (dB)" and reduce its legend to two colour-matched inline labels ("recipe (b′)" in red, "Welch" in magenta) placed in the quiet region above 33 Hz, well clear of the residual traces.
  - Reduce gridlines to vertical lines at 5 Hz intervals only, or drop the grid and rely on ticks; if horizontal reference is needed in (b), the zero line and the new noise band already supply it.
  - Tighten panel (b) to roughly −7 to +13 dB once the legend is moved out of the lower region, and give the reclaimed height to panel (a) where the peaks are dense.
  - Consider a light shaded marker on one inter-harmonic valley in (b) with the inline note "valleys: Slepian side-lobe fill-in", so the mechanism named in the caption is located on the graphic.
  - If the layout allows a wider single-column or 1.5-column figure, widen both panels; the sub-1 Hz peak spacing below 15 Hz is currently near the resolution limit of the printed width.

### figure8.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — Three separate colourbars (c, e, g) encode one identical 10–45 dB scale; two of the three are pure redundant ink. The colourbars carry no unit label ("dB"), so the caption is doing work the axis should do. Histogram bars in (h) carry both a dark fill and a heavy black outline — redundant data ink at this bin count (the outlines begin to dominate the narrow bins). Dotted grids in (d) and (f) are acceptable in weight, but the vertical grid adds little given the labelled ticks. Inward-pointing ticks on the spectrograms sit on top of data.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — The 4×2 grid creates a false row correspondence: row 2 reads as "(c) periodogram spectrogram → (d) its spectrum," and row 3 as "(e) multitaper → (f) its spectrum." In fact (d) and (f) are *pre-ictal* and *ictal* time points, each containing **all** estimators. Nothing in the panels themselves signals this; the reader must consult the caption to avoid a wrong inference. Compounding this, the colour legend appears only in (f), i.e. *after* the colours are first used in (d) — a backward lookup.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Two surplus colourbars (single shared scale).
- Jet/rainbow colourmap on all three spectrograms: perceptually non-uniform, creates artefactual banding at the cyan–yellow and yellow–red transitions, and is not colour-vision-deficiency safe. This is decorative colour variation masquerading as data resolution.
- Heavy bar outlines in (h).
- Grey shading in (a) is *not* junk — it encodes the seizure interval. Keep.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate — panel spacing is generous and legible. Panel (b) is the only slightly under-used cell (short 6 s trace in a wide box), but it is defensible as a zoom.

**AXES:** Issues —
- (c), (e), (g) time axes begin at ~1 s with first label at 100, while (a) begins at 0 with a labelled 0 tick. The stacked time axes therefore do not register with (a); an event at t = 320 s does not fall on the same x-pixel across panels. For a figure whose whole purpose is vertical comparison, this is a substantive defect.
- Redundant tick labels on the time axis of (c) and (e): with (g) labelled below, the intermediate labels can be erased.
- (b) shows y ticks only at −500 and 0, implying limits different from (a)'s stated ±500; the zoom should inherit the parent panel's limits or the difference should be stated.
- (d) spans ~0–45 dB while (f) spans ~20–85 dB. Different scales for two panels the reader is explicitly invited to compare. dB is logarithmic so a non-zero origin is fine, but the *ranges* should match or the offset should be annotated.
- Frequency axes (0–30 Hz) and (h) count axis start at zero. Good.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — Defensible for spectrograms, but (d) and (f) compare four estimators whose degrees of freedom (ν = 2, 6, 8.7, 7.7) are the entire point of the comparison. A ν-based confidence interval (or a single representative error bar per estimator, in a corner) would let the reader judge whether the visible differences exceed estimator variance. Strongly recommended, not blocking.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, uniform parenthetical style, all set outside the top-left of their axes. Vertical placement of (a) sits marginally higher relative to its frame than (c)/(e)/(g); trivially correctable.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — Wide spectrograms and time series, near-square spectra and histogram. Well judged.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — No callout boxes. However the event markers are styled inconsistently: blue and red dashed lines in (a) versus two identical **white** dashed lines in (c), (e), (g). The same two instants are thus given three different visual identities, and the white lines erase a full column of spectrogram pixels each.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — Detached four-entry legend in the upper right of (f) only, with a solid border and white fill. Its lower-left corner sits at roughly 62 dB near the 8 Hz region, where the grey periodogram peaks at ~60 dB; it does not clearly overlap, but the clearance is small and will close at reduced publication width. With four series in two panels, inline labelling at curve endpoints is feasible on the right-hand flank (25–30 Hz), where the four traces separate by several dB.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Time axes of (a) and (c, e, g) are not aligned: (a) starts at 0 with a labelled zero tick, the spectrograms start at ~1 s with no zero tick. Vertical comparison across the stack — the figure's central claim — is compromised.
- [BLOCKING] The row-wise grid implies (d) is the spectrum of (c) and (f) the spectrum of (e). It is not; (d) and (f) are pre-ictal and ictal instants each containing all four estimators. Nothing on the panels themselves prevents this misreading.
- [ADVISORY] Legend appears only in (f), after the colours are first used in (d); forces a downward-and-back lookup.
- [ADVISORY] Jet colourmap: non-uniform luminance, false contour banding, not CVD-safe.
- [ADVISORY] Three identical colourbars where one suffices; none labelled with units (dB).
- [ADVISORY] (d) and (f) use different y-ranges despite being presented for comparison.
- [ADVISORY] Event markers change colour between (a) (blue/red) and the spectrograms (white/white); white lines occlude data columns.
- [ADVISORY] (b) y-limits appear to differ from (a) without explanation; no +500 tick.
- [ADVISORY] Detached bordered legend box in (f) with narrow clearance from the 8 Hz periodogram peak.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant time tick labels on (c) and (e); heavy bar outlines in (h).
- [ADVISORY] No indication of estimator variance in (d)/(f) despite ν being the comparison variable.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Set identical x-limits (0 to 599 s) with a labelled 0 tick on (a), (c), (e), (g), and share the axis so that the vertical stack registers pixel-for-pixel. Erase tick labels on (c) and (e), keeping them only on (g) beneath the single "Time (s)" title.
- Break the false row pairing. Either (i) add a short inline title to each spectrum panel — "pre-ictal, t = 246 s" on (d) and "ictal, t = 318 s" on (f) — or (ii) re-order so the three spectrograms form a contiguous left block and the two spectra a separate right block with a visible gutter between the groups.
- Collapse the three colourbars into one, placed to the right of the (c)–(g) stack and vertically centred on it, labelled "Power (dB)". This recovers ink and reinforces that the scale is common.
- Replace jet with a perceptually uniform sequential map (viridis, magma) or, since the variable is power in dB and the interest is in *rise*, a uniform map with an explicit tick at the pre-ictal median.
- Give (d) and (f) the same y-range, or if the ~20 dB ictal offset makes that wasteful, keep separate ranges but add matched grid spacing and a note of the offset so the reader is not misled by apparent peak heights.
- Unify the event markers: use the same blue and red dashed lines in (c), (e), (g) as in (a), drawn thin (0.75 pt) so they mark without erasing. If white is needed for contrast on the dark map, use a blue/red line with a thin white halo rather than replacing the colour.
- Delete the legend box in (f). Label the four traces inline at the right edge (25–30 Hz), each in its series colour, staggered vertically: "Periodogram" (grey, lowest), "Multitaper" (black), "Recipe (b′)" (red), "Welch" (magenta). Add short thin leader lines in the matching colour where any two labels would sit within one text height of each other. Because all four traces converge near 30 Hz, consider instead labelling at ~12–14 Hz in (f), where the traces separate by 5–8 dB, and reserving (d) for a single set of labels since the colour code is then established for both panels.
- In (h), drop the bar outlines (or reduce to a 0.25 pt stroke in the fill colour) so the distribution shape, not the bin scaffolding, carries the ink.
- Add a single representative ν-based confidence interval per estimator in a corner of (d), or light shaded CI bands, so the reader can judge which inter-estimator differences are real.
- Set (b) to the same ±500 µV limits as (a) with ticks at −500, 0, +500, making the zoom directly commensurable with its parent.
- Align the (a) panel label with the (c)/(e)/(g) labels so all eight sit at a common horizontal and vertical offset from their axes frames.

### figure9.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The core content (waveform, six spectrograms, dumbbell plot) is dense and information-rich, and the shared colour scale plus the single white resolution box per panel are efficient, Tufte-friendly devices. Non-data ink that can be reduced: (1) every panel is enclosed in a full four-sided frame; top/right spines on (a) and (h) carry no information; (2) x-axis tick labels are repeated six times in (b–g) although all six panels share the identical 0–40 s range — the middle rows' tick labels are redundant data ink; (3) two detached legend boxes (a, h) each with a bounding rule; (4) panel (h)'s marker values are printed as text *and* readable from the axis — acceptable direct labelling, but then the dotted gridline grid becomes redundant.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — (a) → paired rows (b,c)/(d,e)/(f,g) → (h) reads top-to-bottom, left-to-right, and the left/right = multitaper/smoothing pairing is consistent throughout. One friction point: the shared colourbar is parked at the far right at the vertical mid-height, so readers of rows (b,c) and (f,g) must travel diagonally to the scale and its axis title. A second, minor discontinuity: the five shaded spindles in (a) are not marked in (b–g), so the reader must eyeball time coordinates back and forth between (a) and the spectrograms.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Detached legend box in (a) sitting inside the axes, immediately above the black trace's largest excursion (~+70 µV at t ≈ 7 s).
- Detached legend box in (h) placed inside the data rectangle, with dotted gridlines running behind it.
- Jet/rainbow colourmap in (b–g): not perceptually uniform, not colour-blind safe; the cyan–yellow transition manufactures edges that do not exist in the data, which is a substantive concern when the figure's whole argument is about smoothing and resolution.
- Colour scale clips: the entire <4 Hz band is saturated uniform dark red in all six panels, so a large fraction of the image area encodes "≥12.5 dB" and nothing more.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall, with two local problems — the top-right and bottom-right corners beside the colourbar are empty (the colourbar spans only rows b–e), and panel (h) has a wide empty band between the 0.40–0.75 Hz region and the data, an artefact of the axis limits.

**AXES:** Issues — (1) Panel (h)'s x-axis begins at ≈0.40 Hz, not zero. Since the dumbbell *length* invites comparison of absolute half-power widths, a suppressed origin exaggerates the multitaper/smoothing ratio (e.g. 1.1 vs 2.1 Hz looks like a ~4× gap). Either start at zero or state in the caption that the axis is truncated. (2) Frequency axes in (b–g) correctly start at 0. (3) The y-axis in (a) is quantitatively valid only for the black trace; the red trace is offset, which the caption notes — acceptable, but consider removing the −100 tick label or annotating it as the red baseline. (4) Middle-row (b–e) x tick labels can be deleted in favour of the shared bottom-row axis.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — none required; (h) reports deterministic achieved widths and ν. No undefined uncertainty is shown, so no blocking issue.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, all top-left, all in matching parenthesised roman, all outside the plotting rectangle. No action needed.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — the 40 s waveform and the spectrograms are appropriately wider than tall; (h), a one-dimensional interval plot with three rows, is correctly wide and short. Overall figure is near-square and will survive a single-column-to-full-width reduction, but see the label-size note below.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — The five grey shaded bands in (a) are legitimate data-encoding annotation and are unobtrusive; keep them. The white resolution rectangles in (b–g) are excellent quantitative annotation. In (h), the text block "α = 1, L = 1: small-α regime" is centred at the same height as, and only a few pixels above, the horizontal connector line of the 1 s row; at publication scale the descenders will very nearly touch the rule. The paired "1.1 Hz / ν = 2.0"-style two-line labels sit directly above their markers and are clean, but the 1 s row's right-hand label ("2.1 Hz / ν = 4.7") is crowded against the 2.00 Hz gridline.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — Two detached legend boxes where inline labelling would remove the lookup cost entirely.
