In (a), the two series are already colour-distinct and vertically separated (black at 0 µV, red at −100 µV); the legend can be replaced by "EEG, C4-M1, 0.3–35 Hz" in black beside the upper trace and "11–16 Hz (offset)" in red beside the lower trace, at the right margin. In (h), "Multitaper" and "Smoothing" can be written once, in matching black and red, directly above the two markers of the top (1 s) row. Neither legend currently overlaps plotted data, so this is advisory rather than blocking — but the (a) legend's lower edge is uncomfortably close to the +70 µV peak and will collide if the panel is compressed vertically.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Jet colourmap in (b–g) is not perceptually uniform or colour-blind safe and introduces artificial contours; this undermines a figure whose purpose is to compare smoothness of estimates.
- [ADVISORY] Colour scale saturates: the sub-4 Hz band is uniformly clipped dark red across all six panels, discarding a large share of the dynamic range.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (h) x-axis does not begin at zero and the truncation is undocumented; dumbbell lengths therefore misrepresent relative half-power widths.
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes in (a) and (h) inside the data rectangles; both are replaceable by inline, colour-matched labels.
- [ADVISORY] "α = 1, L = 1: small-α regime" in (h) sits within a few pixels of the 1 s connector line and will clip at reduced publication width.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant x tick labels on rows (b,c) and (d,e), which duplicate the shared 0–40 s scale shown in (f,g).
- [ADVISORY] Full four-sided frames on (a) and (h) add non-data ink; dotted gridlines in (h) are redundant given the direct numeric labels.
- [ADVISORY] Colourbar spans only the middle two rows, leaving empty corners top-right and bottom-right and forcing a long eye-travel from rows (b,c) and (f,g).
- [ADVISORY] The five shaded spindles in (a) are not carried through to (b–g), so the reader must transfer time coordinates by eye.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace jet with a perceptually uniform map (viridis, cividis, or a sequential turbo alternative) and either raise the upper colour limit or apply a compressive transform so the <4 Hz band is no longer uniformly clipped; state the limits in the caption.
- Extend (h)'s x-axis to 0, or keep the truncation and add "x-axis truncated at 0.4 Hz" to the caption; simultaneously delete the dotted gridlines, since every marker is already numerically labelled.
- Delete both legend boxes. In (a), place "EEG, C4-M1, 0.3–35 Hz" in black at the right end of the upper trace and "11–16 Hz, offset" in red at the right end of the lower trace. In (h), label the black circle "Multitaper" and the red square "Smoothing" directly, once, on the top row, using the same colours as the markers.
- Move "α = 1, L = 1: small-α regime" either above the "1.1 Hz / ν = 2.0" label block or outside the axes as a caption sentence; if retained inline, raise it by at least one text height above the connector and set it in a smaller size.
- Remove x tick labels from (b–e), retaining them only on (f,g) with the single shared "Time (s)" title per column, and remove the top and right spines from (a) and (h).
- Lengthen the colourbar to span the full height of rows (b)–(g), or relocate it to a short horizontal bar beneath row (f,g), so the scale is equidistant from all six panels.
- Add faint tick marks or thin grey vertical lines at the five spindle times along the top edge of (b) and (c) (or all six panels) so the shaded events in (a) can be located directly in the spectrograms.
- Check final-width legibility: the two-line "value / ν" labels in (h) and the axis tick labels in (b–g) are small relative to the 1981 px canvas; verify they remain ≥7 pt after reduction to journal column width.

---

## Color & Accessibility Auditor

---
PER-FIGURE:

**efigure1.png** (kernel comparison, 6 curves)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — each hue is one kernel/method; no decorative fill.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive — 5 hues (black, green, red, cyan, olive) + dotted black for 6 series in a small panel. Line style carries information only for 2 of 6 series.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — the primary comparison in (b) is *red* (25 % cosine→parabola, the recipe the paper advocates) vs *green* (untapered→box, the leakiest method). Under deuteranopia/protanopia these two skirt curves become the same ochre-grey and cross each other around ±10, so the headline "skirts differ by 40 dB" contrast collapses. Olive vs green is also near-indistinguishable for protanopes; cyan vs olive survives.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — ad hoc matplotlib-ish set (pure red/green/cyan/olive), not a principled palette. Recommend Okabe-Ito (black, orange, sky blue, bluish green, vermillion, yellow) with distinct dash patterns for every series.

**efigure2.png** (AR(4) realization, 7 series, filled noise bands)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive — grey, green, black, cyan, olive, red, blue = 6 hues + grey.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — worse than efigure1 because in panel (b) the discrimination is between overlapping *filled* noise envelopes (green, black, cyan, olive, red) where hue is the only cue; dash patterns cannot help a filled band. Red (cosine→parabola) vs green (untapered→box) again carries the main message; olive (adaptive) vs green is also confusable. Under achromatopsia green/red/olive bands are indistinguishable mid-greys.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — same ad hoc set. Suggest reducing panel (b) to 3 series, or using luminance-ordered fills with hatching/alpha plus direct labels.

**efigure3.png** (3 panels, 9 methods, shared legend)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive — 7 hues (black, green, cyan, olive, red, grey, magenta) × 9 series. This is the most overloaded figure in the set; it is only readable because marker shape and dash style are also varied.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — red (cosine→parabola) vs green (untapered→box) are adjacent and crossing in (a) and (b); olive vs green and grey vs black are additionally weak. Markers (circle/square/triangle/diamond) do rescue most distinctions, so this is recoverable, but red-green is still the primary categorical contrast.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — recommend Okabe-Ito or ColorBrewer Dark2 (max 8) and keep the redundant marker coding.

**efigure4.png** (16 panels)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Partly decorative — in (a) the seven hues encode only *time-shift position* of one identical window, i.e. an ordered variable rendered as an arbitrary rainbow (blue, green, red, cyan, magenta, yellow, grey). Same for the four frequency-shift copies in (b,c). Rows (i–l) navy bars and (m–p) dark green single-hue lines are fine.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive — 7 hues in (a) for an ordinal sequence.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — (a) red vs green adjacent lobes; in (b,c) green ($f'N\Delta=1$) vs red ($=2$) are the two curves the reader must tell apart. In (d–h) the taper index legend uses black/blue/green/red — green ($i=3$) vs red ($i=4$) again.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — ordered quantities (shift position, taper index) should use a sequential perceptually uniform ramp (viridis/cividis sampled at 4–7 levels), which also fixes the CVD problem.

**figure1.png** (3 routes, EEG)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — black + green + magenta only (3 hues), with dash coding.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Borderline/At risk — black/green/magenta is actually CVD-distinguishable (green→ochre, magenta→blue-grey), and the three curves are deliberately coincident in (a), so the risk is low. Panel (b) green vs magenta separated by 12 decades — safe. Under achromatopsia green and magenta are similar greys but vertically separated.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Acceptable, but the hues are arbitrary; magenta here means "sliding sinc", which conflicts with magenta = Welch elsewhere (see cross-figure).

**figure2.png** (8 panels, estimate vs true PSD)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — black = estimate, blue dashed = truth, dark green = kernel.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 3 hues total, effectively 1–2 accents. Exemplary for the paper.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — black/blue/dark-green all differ in luminance and are safe under all three simulations.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good.

**figure3.png** (record + error decomposition)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — red = tapered record (a); blue = noise, red = bias (b).
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 3 colors max.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — blue vs red is a safe pair; black solid/dashed differ by style. Achromatopsia: blue and red are both mid-grey but they cross only once and are labelled far apart; consider adding dash style to one.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good, though red/blue here carry *different* meanings than red/blue elsewhere in the paper (see cross-figure).

**figure4.png** (Slepian construction)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Mostly meaningful (taper index, $L$ value), but these are ordinal variables shown in rainbow order (blue, green, red, cyan, magenta, black in (c)).
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive in (c) — 6 hues for an ordered sweep of $L$.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — (a) black/blue/red is fine; (b) black/blue/green fine; (c) green ($L=3$) vs red ($L=5$) are adjacent stacked curves whose ordering is the whole point, and (d) uses a *red* dashed line ($2\alpha$) against a *black* dotted line ($L=2\alpha-1$) — under deuteranopia these two reference lines look alike (distinguished only by dash style, which is good practice — keep).
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — panel (c) is a monotone family in $L$; use viridis sampled at 6 levels (light→dark = small→large $L$), which is CVD- and grayscale-proof.

**figure5.png** (taper banks + eigen-weight ladders)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Partly decorative — as in efigure4(a), the 7 hues in (a) encode only segment position; (b,c) 4 hues encode ordered frequency shifts / sinc positions; (d) 4 hues encode taper index.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive — 7 hues for one ordered variable.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — red/green adjacency in (a) and green-vs-red in (b,c,d) legends.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — sequential colormap recommended, as above. Navy bars in (e–h) are good and consistent with figure8(h).

**figure6.png** (four practical estimators + kernels)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful and, importantly, method-coded (green = untapered+box, black = multitaper, red = recipe b′, magenta = Welch; blue dashed = truth).
- COLOR ECONOMY: Acceptable — 1 accent hue per panel, but 4 hues across the row + blue truth + dark green kernels = 6 hues in the figure.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — each panel contains only one estimate against blue truth, so *within-panel* discrimination is safe; however panel (a) green-on-blue and panel (c) red-on-blue are the two panels a reader compares, and green vs blue-dashed for a deuteranope is a weak contrast (dash style saves it). The green/red *method identity* must be recovered from other figures' legends, where it is unsafe.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — replace green with Okabe-Ito orange and red with vermillion/blue-black.

**figure7.png** (EEG, three estimators + differences)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful, method-coded (black/red/magenta).
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 3 hues.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (mild) — red vs magenta is the critical pair in (b), where the two difference traces overlap heavily. For protanopes red darkens and magenta shifts blue-grey, so they remain weakly separable; under achromatopsia they are near-identical greys on a busy overlapping trace. This is the one place where the paper's "they agree" claim depends on separating two similar hues.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — make one of the two traces a distinct dash pattern or use vermillion + blue.

**figure8.png** (seizure: 3 spectrograms + spectra + histogram)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful (PSD in dB), but the colormap choice undermines it.
- COLOR ECONOMY: N/A for the colormap; line panels (d,f) use grey/black/red/magenta consistently with figure7. Good.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: **No** — panels (c,e,g) use *jet*. Jet is not perceptually uniform (false cyan/yellow banding creates artefactual structure and hides real gradients in the green band), and under deuteranopia the red↔green ends of the ramp converge, making high and mid PSD values ambiguous; under achromatopsia jet is non-monotonic in luminance and completely uninvertible. Also, the white dashed event markers on (c) are hard to see against yellow/red.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — replace jet with viridis, magma, or cividis (cividis is explicitly CVD-optimised). Keep the single shared scale (good practice) and consider one shared colorbar instead of three.

**figure9.png** (sleep spindles, 6 spectrograms + summary)
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful.
- COLOR ECONOMY: As above.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: **No** — jet again, over six panels that the reader must compare for subtle resolution differences; the dark-red saturated low-frequency band clips and the mid-range green/yellow transitions dominate perceived structure. Panel (h) uses black circles vs **red** squares, which is safe because marker shape is redundant.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work — viridis/cividis; the shared vertical colorbar and white resolution boxes are otherwise well done (white boxes remain visible on viridis).

CROSS-FIGURE:
- **Same method, different colour.** "25 % cosine taper, then parabola" (recipe b′) is red in efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure6(c), figure7, figure8(d,f) — but **black** in figure2(b,c,d). The periodogram is grey in efigure2 and figure8(d,f) but **black** in figure2(a).
- **Same colour, different method.** Cyan = "Hann, then box" in efigure1 and efigure3, but = "Multitaper, $L=6$" in efigure2, and = "$L=7$" in figure4(c). Olive/yellow = "Bohman, then box" in efigure1 and efigure3, but = "Multitaper $L=7$, adaptive" in efigure2, and a taper-shift copy in figure5(a)/efigure4(a). Magenta = "Welch" in efigure3, figure6(d), figure7, figure8, but = "sliding sinc window" in figure1 and a shift copy in figure5(a).
- **Semantic overload of red and blue.** Red denotes: recipe b′ (6 figures), the *bias* curve (figure3b), the tapered record (figure3a), the 7th Slepian taper (figure4a), taper $i=4$ (figure4b, figure5d, efigure4d–h), the $2\alpha$ reference line (figure4d), event markers (figure8a), and "smoothing" markers (figure9h). Blue denotes: the *true PSD* (figure2, figure6), the *noise* curve (figure3b), taper $i=2$, and shift position 1. A reader who has learned "red = the recommended smoothing recipe, blue = truth" will misread figure3.
- **Two incompatible palette families.** Figures 1–7 and the e-figures use saturated primary line colours (pure red/green/cyan/magenta/olive) while figures 8–9 use jet images; nothing in the set uses a perceptually uniform ramp. The rainbow line palette and the jet images at least "match" in being rainbow-like, but both clash with the restrained black/blue/dark-green style of figure2 and figure3, which are the best-looking figures in the paper.
- Minor consistency positives worth preserving: navy bars (figure5e–h, efigure4i–l, figure8h), dark-green kernels (figure2e–h, figure6e–h, efigure4m–p), blue-dashed true PSD (figure2, figure6, efigure2), black = multitaper (efigure1–3, figure6b, figure7, figure8), green = untapered+box (efigure1–3, figure6a, figure1a).

ISSUES:
- [BLOCKING] Jet colormap in figure8(c,e,g) and figure9(b–g): not perceptually uniform, produces false banding, and is unreadable under deuteranopia/protanopia and in grayscale. These are the two data figures whose scientific claim is "the estimators give the same picture", which a non-uniform colormap can neither support nor refute.
- [BLOCKING] Red/green as the primary categorical contrast for the two methods being contrasted (recipe b′ vs untapered-then-box) in efigure1, efigure2, efigure3 — and in efigure2(b) the contrast is between *filled* bands, where no line-style fallback exists.
- [BLOCKING] Cross-figure colour remapping: cyan, olive and magenta each denote two or three different methods in different figures; recipe b′ and the raw periodogram change colour between figure2 and the rest. This will actively mislead readers.
- [ADVISORY] Excessive hue count: efigure3 (9 series, 7 hues), figure5(a)/efigure4(a) (7 hues for one ordered variable), figure4(c) (6 hues for an ordinal sweep of $L$).
- [ADVISORY] Decorative/ordinal-as-rainbow colour in figure5(a–c), efigure4(a–c): position/shift index is ordered and should be a sequential ramp.
- [ADVISORY] Semantic overload of red (8 distinct meanings) and blue (true PSD vs noise curve).
- [ADVISORY] figure7(b): red vs magenta overlapping traces — weakest within-panel CVD distinction in the paper.
- [ADVISORY] figure8: three separate colorbars for one shared 10–45 dB scale; white dashed markers poorly visible over jet's warm region.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
1. Adopt one Okabe-Ito method palette and lock it in a shared style file, used in **every** figure and in the tables' captions:
   periodogram = grey (#999999); untapered→box = orange (#E69F00); multitaper ($L=2\alpha-1$) = black; multitaper ($L=6$ / $L=2\alpha-2$) = sky blue (#56B4E9) with dashes; adaptive multitaper = bluish green (#009E73) with dots; Hann→box = sky blue; Bohman→box = yellow (#F0E442) with dashes; recipe b′ (cosine→parabola) = vermillion (#D55E00); Hann→Gaussian = reddish purple (#CC79A7); Welch = blue (#0072B2); true PSD = black dashed (or reserve one colour, e.g. #CC79A7, and never reuse it).
   This removes every red/green pair and every duplicate mapping.
2. Replace jet in figure8(c,e,g) and figure9(b–g) with **cividis** (CVD-optimised) or viridis; keep the single shared dB range, use one shared colorbar per figure, and switch the event markers to black-outlined white dashed lines so they read on any background.
3. Re-colour figure2(a–d) and figure3 to the locked palette: figure2(a) grey (periodogram), (b) vermillion-light, (c,d) vermillion — or, if the row is meant to be method-agnostic, keep all four black and say so in the caption; and in figure3(b) recolour noise/bias to two non-reserved hues (e.g. #0072B2 dashed and #999999 solid with distinct markers) so red no longer means "bias" in one figure and "recipe b′" in six others.
4. Convert ordinal colour to sequential ramps: figure4(c) $L=1,3,5,7,9,N$ → viridis light→dark; figure5(a)/efigure4(a) seven Welch segment positions → viridis; figure5(b,c)/efigure4(b,c) four shifts → 4 viridis levels; taper index $i=1..4$ → 4 cividis levels. This also makes these panels legible in grayscale.
5. Add redundant non-colour encoding wherever three or more series overlap: keep the marker shapes in efigure3 (they already rescue it), add dash patterns to efigure1 (all six series), and in efigure2(b) either reduce to three series or replace fills with thin lines + hatching and direct in-plot labels.
6. In figure7(b), change the Welch difference trace to blue (#0072B2) and keep recipe b′ vermillion, or plot the two differences in separate stacked sub-panels.
7. Run all final figures through a deuteranopia/protanopia simulator and a grayscale conversion; every legend entry must remain identifiable in all three renderings.
8. Preserve the already-consistent elements (navy bars, dark-green kernel traces, black = multitaper) and state the method→colour mapping once in the Methods or in the first figure caption so readers can carry it across the 13 panels.

CROSS-FIGURE:
  - **Typeface:** all 13 figures use a single serif (Computer-Modern/DejaVu-Serif-like) family for both text and math. Internally consistent, but non-compliant with the common journal requirement for sans-serif (Helvetica/Arial) figure lettering; math italics would then need a matching sans-serif math font (e.g. Helvetica + mathastext / DejaVu Sans mathtext) to avoid a serif/sans clash.
  - **Effective font size is not uniform** because the figures are rendered at different pixel widths for the same nominal output width. At the intended widths, equivalent elements land anywhere from ~5.5 pt (insets in figure2/figure6, colour-bar ticks in figure8) to ~7 pt (figure1, figure8 main ticks). efigure4 (16 panels) and figure2/6 insets fall below the 6 pt floor.
  - **figure3 and figure7 are the only two with single-column aspect** (≈992 px wide). If they are placed at 3.25 in their lettering is ~6.5 pt while double-column figures are ~7 pt; if they are placed at 6.875 in their lettering will be roughly double everything else. The intended width per figure must be fixed and the point sizes equalised after scaling.
  - **Panel-label size:** ~8–8.5 pt in most figures, ~9 pt in figure1/figure4/figure8/figure9. Below the 9–10 pt recommendation in the majority.
  - **Capitalisation style is mixed across the set.** Title/sentence-initial capitals: "Frequency (Hz)", "Time (s)", "Difference (dB)", "RMS error (dB)", "Pixels", "Achieved half-power width (Hz)", "EEG (µV)", "PSD (dB…)". All-lowercase: "half-power width × 𝑁Δ", "kernel power beyond twice the half-power half-width", "peak beyond 1.5 times the half-power half-width (dB)", "max. |difference| (dB)". Pick one convention (sentence case with initial capital is recommended) and apply to every axis title.
  - **Quantity naming is inconsistent for the same quantity:** "Ŝ(𝑓) (dB)" (efigure1–2, figure1, 2, 6, 7) vs "PSD (dB)" / "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)" (figure8, figure9). Likewise "𝓚(𝑓) (dB)" vs "𝓚(𝑓)/𝑁Δ".
  - **dB reference levels are inconsistent:** only figure9 states one ("dB re 1 µV²/Hz"); every other dB axis is bare.
  - **Legend capitalisation** is consistent (sentence case: "Untapered, then box", "25% cosine taper, then parabola", "True PSD") — keep this.
  - **Panel-label format** "(a)", "(b)" lowercase in parentheses, upper-left — consistent across all 13 figures. Good.
  - **Abbreviation practice is inconsistent:** PSD is expanded only in figure1's caption; EEG and C4/C4-M1/ELROND are never expanded; RMS is expanded in figure3. Journals normally require each caption to be self-contained.
  - **Prime glyph** ("𝑓′𝑁Δ", "Recipe (b′)") renders with an unnatural gap in figure5, efigure4 and figure7 — same artefact in three figures.
  - No statistical significance markers (*, **, ns) anywhere in the set — nothing to define.
  - No in-figure titles anywhere — compliant across the set.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Text below the 6 pt floor at intended print width: efigure4 tick labels and panel legends (~6.0–6.3 pt); figure2 and figure6 inset tick labels (~5.5–6 pt); figure8 colour-bar tick labels (~5.5–6 pt); figure9 panel (h) annotations and row labels (~6.0–6.3 pt).
  - [BLOCKING] figure8: the three spectrogram colour bars have no label and no units. A colour scale in dB with no stated reference is uninterpretable.
  - [BLOCKING] Undefined abbreviations: EEG (figure7, figure8, figure9 — never expanded), PSD (undefined in the captions of efigure2, figure2, figure6, figure8, figure9), C4 / C4-M1 electrode-montage notation, ELROND dataset acronym, "Recipe (b′)" used as an in-figure label without in-caption definition in figure8.
  - [BLOCKING] figure4: in-figure y-axis normalisations contradict the caption — panel (b) shows "|𝐻^{(i)}(𝑓)|²/𝑁Δ²" vs caption "|H^{(i)}(f)|²/Δ"; panel (c) shows "𝓚(𝑓)/𝑁Δ" vs caption "∑λ_i|H^{(i)}(f)|²/2αΔ".
  - [BLOCKING] Non-sans-serif typeface throughout (13/13 figures) if the target journal specifies Helvetica/Arial for figure lettering.
  - [ADVISORY] Mixed intended widths (figure3, figure7 single-column aspect vs the rest double-column) produce unequal effective point sizes for equivalent elements across the set.
  - [ADVISORY] Capitalisation of axis titles is inconsistent across figures (and within efigure3/figure1).
  - [ADVISORY] Every dB axis except figure9's colour bar lacks a reference level; dimensionless axes ("𝑓Δ", "𝑓𝑁Δ", "𝑅𝑁Δ", "𝑁ₛ/𝑁", "half-power width × 𝑁Δ", "𝑘", "𝑖") are unannotated.
  - [ADVISORY] figure3(a) y-axis "𝑥ₖ" has no unit (µV or "arbitrary units"?).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel labels are ~8–8.5 pt in most figures, below the 9–10 pt recommendation; they are also not uniform across the set.
  - [ADVISORY] figure6: the sole legend ("Estimate", green) does not match the curve colours in panels (b)–(d).
  - [ADVISORY] figure4: two different curve-labelling conventions in adjacent panels (ℎ_k^{(i)} vs 𝑖 = 1, 2, 3).
  - [ADVISORY] "max." (figure1c) and "Pixels" (figure8h) are abbreviated/under-specified axis titles.
  - [ADVISORY] Prime glyph spacing artefact in "𝑓′𝑁Δ" and "Recipe (b′)" (figure5, efigure4, figure7).
  - [ADVISORY] efigure3: 9-entry legend and its markers are the smallest graphical elements in the set; marker shapes will not be resolvable in print.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Set one global style file and regenerate all 13 figures: `font.family = sans-serif`, `font.sans-serif = ['Helvetica','Arial']`, and `mathtext.fontset = 'dejavusans'` (or Helvetica + mathastext in LaTeX) so text and math share one sans-serif face.
  - Declare the intended width for each figure, then fix absolute point sizes after scaling: 7 pt ticks, 8.5 pt axis titles, 8 pt legends, 10 pt bold panel labels. Export at the final width (e.g. `figsize=(6.875, h)`, `dpi=600`) so no post-hoc scaling changes the type size.
  - Enlarge inset axes in figure2 and figure6 (or drop their tick labels and state the inset ranges in the caption) so no label falls below 7 pt.
  - Split efigure4 into two 8-panel figures, or increase panel spacing and reduce tick density (e.g. ticks only at 0, 128, 256), to bring all lettering to ≥7 pt.
  - Add "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)" labels to all three colour bars in figure8, matching figure9 exactly.
  - Add a reference level to every dB axis: "Ŝ(𝑓) (dB re 1 unit²/Hz)" or state "dB relative to peak" for kernel plots; add "(dimensionless)" or an explicit definition for normalised-frequency axes.
  - Standardise the quantity name: use either "Ŝ(𝑓)" or "PSD" everywhere, and "Estimated PSD, Ŝ(𝑓) (dB re …)" as a compromise.
  - Adopt sentence case with an initial capital for all axis titles: "Half-power width × 𝑁Δ", "Kernel power beyond twice the half-power half-width (fraction)", "Peak beyond 1.5 × half-power half-width (dB)", "Maximum |difference| (dB)", "Number of pixels", "Equivalent degrees of freedom, ν".
  - Make each caption self-contained on abbreviations: define at first use in every caption — "EEG, electroencephalogram; PSD, power spectral density; RMS, root mean square; C4-M1, right central electrode referenced to left mastoid (10–20 system); ELROND, <full name>". Expand "re" as "relative to" at first use.
  - Replace "Recipe (b′)" in figure7 and figure8 legends with a self-explanatory label such as "Cosine taper + parabola" (keep the recipe cross-reference in the caption only), which also removes the (b)-panel ambiguity.
  - Reconcile figure4's axis normalisations with the caption text; unify (a) and (b) legend conventions to "𝑖 = 1, 2, 7".
  - Fix the prime glyph: use `$f'$` with the correct math font rather than a superscript apostrophe, and verify no gap appears in the rendered PDF.
  - figure6: add per-panel legends or relabel the (a) legend entry so curve colour and legend agree.
  - Add units to figure3(a) y-axis ("𝑥ₖ (µV)" or "𝑥ₖ (arbitrary units)") and rename index axes "Sample index, 𝑘" and "Taper index, 𝑖".
  - efigure3: enlarge the legend to ≥8 pt (it sits in an empty quadrant, so there is room), increase marker size to ≥4 pt, and add a "(d)"-style note or leave the quadrant empty with the caption sentence retained.
  - Keep all figure titles in the bold caption text only (already compliant) — do not add in-panel titles when regenerating.

## Technical Format Checker

### efigure1.png

---
**DIMENSIONS:**     2089 × 687 px — recommended **double column** — **304 DPI** at 6.875 in (single column, 3.25 in, would give 643 DPI but the 3.04:1 panel pair would compress each axis to ~40 mm wide, which is unusable for two multi-curve log-scale panels). Note: at the journal's nominal double-column width of **181 mm (7.126 in) the figure drops to 293 DPI**, i.e. just *below* the 300 DPI floor. Aspect ratio (3.04:1 → 57 mm tall at double column) is acceptable and within the 230 mm height cap.

**RESOLUTION:**     Marginal / Below threshold depending on placement — figure type is **line art with color** (kernel traces, dotted grid, no photographic content). 304 DPI at 6.875 in; 293 DPI at 181 mm. Both are far below the 600 DPI preferred for line art, and the dotted grid lines and the fine high-frequency ripple of the black multitaper curve in (b) are exactly the features that alias at ~300 DPI.

**FORMAT:**         Needs conversion — **PNG → TIFF (LZW) or, preferably, vector EPS/PDF** for final submission. PNG is acceptable for review only.

**FILE SIZE:**      OK — 0.23 MB, well under the 10 MB limit.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all axis numerals, axis labels ($fN\Delta$, $\mathcal{K}(f)/N\Delta$), legend entries and panel labels are bitmapped at the source raster. Pixel-stepped edges are visible on the italic math glyphs and on the minus signs.

**COLOR MODE:**     RGB — OK for submission. Potential shift on CMYK conversion: the saturated red, pure green (0,128,0-like), and especially the **cyan** trace will desaturate/darken; cyan is also the lowest-contrast line against white at 0.5 pt stroke width. The olive dashed and green solid curves converge in grayscale.

**ISSUES:**
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Legend, tick and axis labels will show pixel edges in print; this is a quality failure for graph-type figures.
  - [BLOCKING] If typeset at the full 181 mm double-column width the effective resolution is 293 DPI, below the 300 DPI minimum for color line art. Even at 6.875 in (304 DPI) the figure fails the 600 DPI line-art preference.
  - [BLOCKING] Format: PNG is not an accepted final-submission format (TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS required).
  - [ADVISORY] Estimated type size at double-column width is ~7 pt for tick labels and ~7 pt for legend text — above the 6 pt hard floor but below the 8–10 pt target. The subscripted/italic math ($\nu$, $L=7$) in the legend will be smaller still.
  - [ADVISORY] Font appears to be a DejaVu/Computer-Modern mix (sans numerals, serif-italic math). Acceptable in principle, but not consistent with a single sans-serif family across the figure.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption uses "recipe (b$'$)" in the same sentence as panel label (b), creating ambiguity between a panel reference and a method label.
  - [ADVISORY] Cyan and olive traces are poorly separable in grayscale reproduction and for color-vision-deficient readers; line identity depends entirely on hue plus one dash pattern.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: degrees of freedom ($\nu$) are stated for every estimator, which satisfies the DOF requirement. Because these are deterministic design kernels rather than sample averages, the caption should state explicitly that the curves are analytic/expected kernels (no realizations averaged), so a reader does not look for an unreported window count.
  - [ADVISORY] The claim "the skirts differ by 40 dB" is not tied to a stated frequency; panel (b) spans −100 to 0 dB, so the comparison point should be specified.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend block in (a) overlaps the dotted grid and sits close to the upper data plateau of the multitaper curve; verify no occlusion after rescaling.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
  - Regenerate from the plotting source as **vector PDF or EPS** with fonts embedded (matplotlib: `savefig('efigure1.pdf')`, `rcParams['pdf.fonttype']=42`). This resolves the rasterized-text and resolution issues simultaneously and keeps the file far below 10 MB.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandatory, re-export at **600 DPI for the final 181 mm width** → 4275 × 1406 px, saved as TIFF with LZW compression.
  - Set the figure size explicitly to the target physical dimensions before export (e.g. `figsize=(7.13, 2.34)` in inches) and set all text to a single sans-serif family (Arial/Helvetica) at **8 pt minimum**, including the legend and the math italic labels.
  - Increase line widths to ≥0.75 pt (≥1.0 pt for the cyan trace) and give each curve a distinct dash pattern (solid / long-dash / dash-dot / dotted) so identification survives grayscale printing.
  - Lighten the dotted grid to a light gray (e.g. 0.8 gray) or drop the minor grid in (b); at ~300 DPI the current dense dotted grid competes visually with the multitaper ripple.
  - Move the panel labels (a) and (b) to a consistent offset outside the axes box in lowercase parentheses, and rename "recipe (b$'$)" to something non-conflicting (e.g. "recipe B2") in both caption and text.
  - Add to the caption: the number of frequency points / evaluation grid used, an explicit statement that the kernels are analytic (no realization averaging), and the frequency at which the 40 dB skirt separation is quoted.
  - Remove nothing from the figure interior regarding titles — the figure correctly carries no internal title; keep all descriptive text in the caption below.

### efigure2.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2089 × 747 px — recommended **double column** (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **≈292 DPI** at that width
                (for reference: 2089 px ÷ 3.5 in = ≈597 DPI at single-column width; aspect ratio 2.80:1 makes single-column placement impractical because the two side-by-side panels would each be only ~42 mm wide and the legend/axis text would fall to ~3.5 pt)
                Height at double column: 747 px ÷ 292 DPI ≈ 2.56 in ≈ 65 mm — well within the 230 mm limit.

RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — figure type: line art (2-D line plots, dotted grid, text legend, no continuous-tone content). Actual ≈292 DPI at 181 mm, versus 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for colour line art. The dense grey/black/cyan noise traces and the 1-px dotted gridlines are exactly the content that aliases at <600 DPI.

FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW, RGB) or, preferably for line art, EPS/PDF with embedded vector text and vector strokes. PNG is accepted for review only, not for final submission.

FILE SIZE:      **OK** — 0.29 MB (limit 10 MB). Substantial headroom exists to re-export at 600 DPI or as vector.

TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis numerals, axis labels ($f\Delta$, $\hat S(f)$ (dB)), legend entries and panel labels are pixel-based by virtue of the PNG container. Glyph edges will show stair-stepping in print, and italic math symbols ($L$, $f$, $\nu$) with thin strokes are the most vulnerable.

COLOR MODE:     **OK as submitted (RGB required and presumably present)** — flag for potential shift if the production workflow converts: the saturated cyan ($L=6$) and green (untapered box) traces will desaturate/darken noticeably in CMYK, and the olive/yellow (adaptive) trace is already low-contrast against white and will lose further distinguishability.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution ≈292 DPI at the recommended 181 mm width — fails the 300 DPI floor and far below the 600 DPI line-art preference.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text and rasterized line work in a line-art figure; thin dotted gridlines and 1-px noise traces will not reproduce cleanly.
  - [BLOCKING] Format: PNG is not an accepted final-submission format.
  - [BLOCKING] Statistical reporting incomplete: panel (b) explicitly compares noise floors between estimators, but the equivalent degrees of freedom are given for only four of the six estimates. The adaptively weighted $L=7$ estimate (olive) has no $\nu$ quoted — and since adaptive weights make $\nu$ frequency-dependent, the caption must state either the frequency-averaged $\nu$ or the range (e.g. $\nu$ rising from ~2 in the high-dynamic-range band to ~14 in the flat band). Without it, the claim that "the adaptive weights … track the truth" is not comparable against the fixed-$\nu$ estimators.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption opens with a bolded-in-effect title phrase, "Dropping the leakiest taper." IEEE prefers a purely descriptive caption with no title; consider folding it into a descriptive sentence.
  - [ADVISORY] Only a single realization is shown ($N=1024$). Since the figure is used to argue about noise-floor behaviour, state explicitly that $n=1$ realization is plotted and that the $\nu$ values are theoretical (not empirical over repeated windows), so readers do not mistake the traces for ensemble averages.
  - [ADVISORY] Quantitative claims in the caption ("floors 20 dB high", "floors 10 dB high", "halves that") are read off panel (b) by eye; give the frequency band over which these floor levels were averaged.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (a) and (b) use different $y$-ranges and different tick intervals (20 dB vs 5 dB). This is defensible but should be obvious; consider noting the vertical-scale change in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Seven overlapping traces, several of them broadband-noisy (grey periodogram, black $L=7$, cyan $L=6$, olive adaptive), create heavy occlusion above $f\Delta \approx 0.25$. The olive and cyan are hard to separate where they overlap; the red curve is partly buried under the blue dashed "True PSD".
  - [ADVISORY] The legend sits inside panel (a) and overlaps the upper plotting region; at 88 mm-per-panel reproduction it will crowd the 40–60 dB range.
  - [ADVISORY] Colour-blind accessibility: the green/olive/red triplet is not distinguishable under deuteranopia, and the figure relies on colour alone to identify six of seven curves.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export as vector PDF or EPS at the final physical size (181 mm × 65 mm) with fonts embedded, or as a Type-1/TrueType-embedded PDF; this removes both the resolution and the rasterized-text blockers at once. If the plotting toolchain cannot emit vector output, render a TIFF at 600 DPI for 181 mm, i.e. **≥4276 × 1529 px** (LZW compression, RGB). 300 DPI (2138 × 765 px) is the bare minimum and is not recommended for this dense line content.
  - Keep the figure at double-column width; do not attempt single-column placement. If a single-column version is ever needed, stack panel (a) above panel (b) rather than shrinking the side-by-side layout.
  - Set all text to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 8–10 pt **as measured at 181 mm final width**. Current axis numerals and legend text compute to roughly 7–7.5 pt at that width; nudge them up to 8 pt so nothing lands near the 6 pt floor.
  - Add the missing degrees of freedom for the adaptively weighted $L=7$ estimate, and state that $\nu$ is frequency-dependent for that estimator. Recommended caption insertion: "…and the adaptively weighted estimate with $L=7$ (olive, $\nu$ varying from $\approx$2 to $\approx$14 with frequency)".
  - State the number of realizations explicitly, e.g. "A single realization ($n=1$) of the AR(4) process is shown; the quoted $\nu$ are theoretical equivalent degrees of freedom, not estimated from repeated windows."
  - Recast the caption opening to remove the title-like phrase, e.g. "Effect of dropping the leakiest taper on the noise floor of multitaper spectral estimates. (a) One realization of…".
  - Quantify the floor claims with a band, e.g. "averaged over $0.35 \le f\Delta \le 0.5$, the untapered box estimate floors $\approx$20 dB high and $L=7$ $\approx$10 dB high, while $L=6$ halves the latter."
  - Improve trace separability: reduce the grey periodogram to a lighter tint or plot it behind a semi-transparent fill; differentiate the noisy multitaper traces by line style as well as colour (solid / dash-dot / dotted) or add short direct labels near the right-hand plateau in panel (b) so colour is not the sole identifier.
  - Move the legend outside the axes (a horizontal legend spanning the top of the two panels, or a single shared legend at the right) to free the 30–55 dB region of panel (a).
  - Choose a colour-blind-safe palette (e.g. replace the green/olive/red set with blue/orange/purple from a Tol or Okabe–Ito palette) while keeping the true-PSD curve visually distinct as the only dashed line.
  - If the publisher converts to CMYK, pre-check the cyan and green traces; darken the cyan slightly and replace the olive/yellow with a darker ochre so both survive conversion and greyscale printing.

### efigure3.png

---
**DIMENSIONS:** 2087 × 1319 px (aspect 1.58:1, landscape) — recommended **double column**
- At single column (3.25 in / 88 mm): 2087 / 3.25 ≈ **642 DPI**, but the rendered height would be only ~2.05 in and the 3-panel + legend layout would be illegible → not viable.
- At double column (6.875 in): 2087 / 6.875 ≈ **304 DPI**; at the full IEEE TBME 181 mm (7.13 in) text block: ≈ **293 DPI**. Rendered height at double column = 4.35–4.51 in (110–115 mm) — well inside the 230 mm limit.

**RESOLUTION:** **Below threshold** — figure type: **line art with color** (2-D line plots, markers, grid, text). Actual ≈ 293–304 DPI at the recommended width, versus 600 DPI preferred (and only marginally at/below the 300 DPI hard floor once the full 181 mm width is used). Marker outlines, dotted/dashed line styles and the log-axis minor grid in (b) are exactly the features that degrade first at ~300 DPI.

**FORMAT:** **Needs conversion** — PNG → **PDF or EPS (vector, preferred)**; TIFF at ≥600 DPI acceptable as a raster fallback. PNG is fine for the review upload only.

**FILE SIZE:** **OK** — 0.33 MB, far below the 10 MB per-figure limit. A vector PDF of this content will also be well under the limit.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick numbers, math symbols ($\nu$, $2\alpha-1$, $N\Delta$) and the 9-entry legend are baked into the PNG raster. For a line-art figure this is a BLOCKING quality issue at production stage.

**COLOR MODE:** **Potential shift** — appears RGB (correct for submission). The palette is heavily saturated: magenta (Welch), cyan (Hann–box), yellow-olive (Bohman), pure red (cosine–parabola) and mid-green (untapered–box). Saturated magenta/cyan and the olive-yellow are the most at-risk hues if the publisher converts to CMYK; the yellow-olive trace also has the weakest luminance contrast against white.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Effective resolution ~293 DPI at the 181 mm double-column width — below the 300 DPI minimum for color line art and far below the 600 DPI preferred value.
- [BLOCKING] All text and line work are rasterized; dotted vs. dashed vs. solid black curves in (a)–(c) and the small open-circle/open-square/triangle markers will not be cleanly distinguishable in print at this sampling.
- [ADVISORY] Tick-label and axis-label glyph heights correspond to roughly 7–9 pt at 7.13 in reproduction; the smallest items (log-axis exponents in (b), legend math) are close to the 6 pt floor and should be verified after final scaling.
- [ADVISORY] The lower-right quadrant contains only the legend, leaving a large area of whitespace; the three data panels are therefore smaller than they need to be at fixed column width.
- [ADVISORY] Colour-only encoding of nine methods. Black solid / black dashed / black dotted are distinguished by line style, but green vs. olive-yellow vs. red vs. magenta rely on hue alone — problematic for colour-blind readers and for any grayscale reproduction.
- [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the caption correctly states that curves are exact kernel and degrees-of-freedom computations with no sampling variability, and reports $\nu$ explicitly in (a) — this satisfies the "state the estimator's degrees of freedom" requirement. However, the number of bandwidth parameter values swept per method (i.e. the plotted points per curve) and the sweep range are not stated; add these for reproducibility. Also state $\Delta$ (or that abscissae are dimensionless in units of $1/(N\Delta)$).
- [ADVISORY] Caption contains the layout instruction "and the legend is at the lower right", which is redundant with the figure itself and is not descriptive content.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Re-export directly from the plotting source (e.g. `matplotlib.savefig('efigure3.pdf')`) as a **vector PDF or EPS** sized to 181 mm width; this resolves both the DPI and the rasterized-text issues at once. If a raster is required, re-render at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm (≥4280 px wide) with `dpi=600` rather than upscaling the existing PNG.
- Set all fonts to a sans-serif family (Arial/Helvetica) at 8–10 pt **at final 181 mm size**; check that the log-axis exponent labels in (b) and the legend entries do not fall below 6 pt.
- Move the legend into the empty lower-right panel slot as a compact 2-column box, or place it inside panel (a), and reclaim the freed area to enlarge panels (a)–(c) — this also increases effective label size.
- Reduce the whitespace between panels and trim outer margins so the three axes occupy more of the 181 mm width.
- Replace the olive-yellow with a darker, higher-contrast hue (e.g. dark goldenrod/brown) and assign each of the nine methods a distinct line-style + marker combination so the plot remains readable in grayscale and under CMYK conversion. Keep the file in RGB for submission and verify magenta/cyan traces in a soft-proof.
- Ensure panel labels remain lowercase parenthesized (a), (b), (c) — currently correct — and keep them outside the axes as they are.
- Revise the caption: delete "and the legend is at the lower right"; add the number of bandwidth-parameter values per curve, the parameter ranges swept, and a definition of $\Delta$; keep the explicit statement that no Monte-Carlo realizations are involved so readers know no error bars are expected.

### efigure4.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2091 x 1947 px — recommended **double** column — **304 DPI** at 6.875 in (≈293 DPI if laid out to the full 181 mm / 7.13 in trim). At single-column width (3.25 in) the file would be 643 DPI but the 4 × 4 panel grid would be unreadable.
                Printed height at double column: 1947/304 ≈ 6.4 in ≈ 163 mm — within the 230 mm limit. Aspect ratio (1.07:1, near-square) is acceptable for a 16-panel grid.

RESOLUTION:     **Below preferred threshold** — figure type: line art (all panels are vector-style curve plots and bar charts). 304 DPI meets the 300 DPI color line-art minimum but falls well short of the 600 DPI preferred for line art; hairline curves (e.g. the dotted reference lines in m–p and the dense oscillations in p) will show stair-stepping and possible dropout at print size.

FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW) or, preferably, EPS/PDF with embedded vector graphics. PNG is acceptable for initial review only.

FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.48 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to re-export at 600–1200 DPI.

TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis numerals, axis labels, panel labels and legend text are bitmap (PNG container). Tick digits measure roughly 26–30 px, i.e. ≈6.5–7 pt at 304 DPI — legible but at/near the 6 pt floor and below the 8–10 pt target.

COLOR MODE:     Potential shift — assumed RGB (consistent with requirements; confirm no ICC/CMYK profile is embedded). The palette includes saturated cyan, magenta, yellow, pure blue and pure green in panels (a)–(h) — cyan and magenta shift noticeably under RGB→CMYK conversion, and the yellow curve in (a) has very low contrast against white in both RGB and print.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text and curves in a line-art figure. Type and 1-px strokes are bitmapped; at 304 DPI effective print resolution this will visibly degrade, and the figure cannot be rescaled without further loss.
  - [BLOCKING] Format: PNG is not an accepted final-submission format for this journal.
  - [ADVISORY] Effective resolution (293–304 DPI at double-column) is below the 600 DPI preferred for line art.
  - [ADVISORY] Text size ≈6.5–7 pt at final size — above the 6 pt absolute minimum but below the 8–10 pt preferred range; the legend entries in (b), (d) and (e) are the smallest and most at risk.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the caption quotes half-power widths (5.84, 8.03, 8.03, 7.41 /(NΔ)) and out-of-band power fractions (0.05 %, 0.13 %, 1.39 %, 0.22 %) but never states the number of tapers/windows K used in each column, nor the resulting estimator degrees of freedom (e.g. DOF ≈ 2K for equal weights, or the effective DOF implied by the eigen-weight ladders in i–l). Since the four columns are being compared as noise-reducing estimators, the DOF must be stated.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption opens with "Fig.~5 in full" and "Columns as in Fig.~5" — a supplementary figure caption must be self-contained; a reader of the supplement should not have to reconstruct the column definitions from the main text.
  - [ADVISORY] Series in (a)–(h) are distinguished by hue only (black/blue/green/red, plus cyan/magenta/yellow in a). Yellow-on-white is near-invisible; red/green pairing is problematic for deuteranopic readers.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (b), (c), (f), (g), (h) lack visible y-axis labels present on their row-leading panels; (f)–(h) share the label of (e) only by position, which is fine, but (b) and (c) carry different quantities and their labels are set in different notation from (a) — verify axis-label consistency.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source as vector PDF or EPS (matplotlib: `savefig('efigure4.pdf')`) with fonts embedded as Type 42/TrueType or converted to outlines; this simultaneously resolves the rasterized-text and resolution issues. If a raster deliverable is required, export TIFF at 600–1200 DPI for a 181 mm width (≈4280–8560 px wide).
  - Set the figure canvas to exactly 181 mm (7.13 in) wide and specify font sizes in points at that size: 8 pt tick labels, 9–10 pt axis labels and panel letters, 8 pt legend text. Reducing tick-label density (e.g. 0.0/0.1/0.2 instead of 0.00/0.05/0.10/0.15/0.20) will free space for larger type.
  - Confirm RGB output with no CMYK profile; replace yellow with a darker distinguishable hue (e.g. dark orange or olive) and consider adding line-style variation (solid/dashed/dash-dot/dotted) so i = 1…4 and f′NΔ = 0…3 remain separable in grayscale and for colorblind readers.
  - Make the caption self-contained: restate the four column cases explicitly (rather than "as in Fig. 5"), and add the number of tapers/windows per column plus the estimator degrees of freedom, e.g. "K = 7, 12, 9 and 7 tapers, giving ν = 2K = 14, 24, 18 and 14 degrees of freedom" (substitute the correct values).
  - Keep panel labels lowercase in parentheses as already done, and confirm no internal panel titles are added during re-export.
  - Increase the linewidth of the dotted |f| = R/2 reference lines and of the curves in (m)–(p) to ≥0.75 pt so they survive rasterization and printing.

### figure1.png

---

**DIMENSIONS:** 1829 × 1038 px (aspect ratio 1.76:1) — recommended **double column** — **266 DPI** at 6.875 in (255 DPI if placed at the full 181 mm / 7.16 in text width). At single-column 3.25 in the same file would deliver 563 DPI, but the three-panel layout and tick-label sizes make single-column placement impractical (see ISSUES).

**RESOLUTION:** **Below threshold** — figure type: **line art** (multi-panel line plots, grid rules, text legends); actual 266 DPI at double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred for color line art.

**FORMAT:** **Needs conversion** — PNG → **PDF or EPS (vector, preferred)**; TIFF (LZW) at ≥600 DPI acceptable as a raster fallback. PNG is acceptable for initial review only.

**FILE SIZE:** **OK** — 0.3 MB (limit 10 MB). There is ample headroom to re-export at 600–1200 DPI.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick numbers, legend entries and panel letters are baked into the PNG raster; pixel stair-stepping will be visible on the thin serif strokes and on the 1-px grid/dotted lines at print size.

**COLOR MODE:** **Potential shift** — assumed RGB (correct for submission). The saturated magenta (≈#FF00FF) and pure green (≈#008000) traces are exactly the hues most prone to shift/flatten if the publisher converts to CMYK for print; magenta will also lose contrast against white on uncoated stock.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the recommended double-column width is 266 DPI, below the 300 DPI minimum and well below the 600 DPI preferred for line art. Panel (b)'s dense noise-floor traces and the dotted grid lines will alias.
- [BLOCKING] Text and line work are rasterized in a line-art figure. IEEE requires vector or ≥600 DPI raster line art; rasterized type at 266 DPI will show pixel edges on the serif glyphs, subscripts ($\lambda_i$, $N_s$) and superscript exponents ($10^{-15}$, $10^{0}$).
- [BLOCKING] Format PNG is not an accepted final-submission format for TBME (TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS).
- [ADVISORY] Aspect ratio/layout precludes single-column use: at 88 mm the tick labels (~34 px tall in the source) would render at roughly 4–5 pt, below the 6 pt floor. Keep the figure double column, or split (a) from (b)/(c) into two single-column figures if a narrower layout is needed.
- [ADVISORY] Typeface is serif (Computer Modern / Times-like). Times is permitted, but TBME prefers sans-serif (Arial/Helvetica); mixing a serif figure font with the journal's figure style is visually inconsistent.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c) is sparse relative to its allotted width — the plot box occupies only ~40% of the lower row while panel (b) legend sits in dead space; tightening whitespace would let all panels be enlarged at the same physical width, improving effective DPI of the data region.
- [ADVISORY] Green/magenta is a poor pairing for deuteranopic readers; in (a) the dash/dot line styles carry the distinction adequately, but in (b) both traces are solid and rely on color plus vertical separation alone.
- [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is mostly compliant — the caption gives ν = 17 equivalent degrees of freedom and states a single 8 s epoch (one realization). However, panels (b) and (c) present single-realization difference curves with no error bars; state explicitly that no averaging over windows/realizations was performed (n = 1 epoch, 1 channel) so readers do not mistake the traces for expectations. Also state the number of frequency bins plotted and, for (c), that each point is a maximum over those bins for a single epoch.
- [ADVISORY] Caption ends with a cross-reference (`Section~\ref{sec:periodogram}`); confirm it resolves, and consider stating the dof definition inline so the caption is self-contained.
- [ADVISORY] "de-identified scalp EEG … from the authors' archive" — TBME will expect a data-provenance/ethics statement in the Methods; the caption alone is not sufficient.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Re-export directly from the plotting source (e.g., matplotlib `savefig('figure1.pdf')` or MATLAB `exportgraphics(..., 'ContentType','vector')`) as **PDF or EPS with embedded fonts** at a canvas width of 181 mm. This resolves the resolution, format and rasterized-text issues simultaneously.
- If a raster deliverable is mandatory, export **TIFF, RGB, LZW-compressed, ≥600 DPI at 7.16 in → ≥4296 px wide** (1200 DPI → 8592 px is safer for the dotted grid and thin noise traces); estimated size remains well under 10 MB with LZW.
- Set all figure text to Arial/Helvetica at 8–10 pt **at final printed size** (i.e., specify point sizes in the export, not by scaling a large canvas down); verify no element, including exponents and subscripts, falls below 6 pt.
- Increase grid-line and thin-trace stroke widths to ≥0.5 pt at final size so they survive rasterization/printing.
- Reduce inter-panel whitespace and widen panel (c) (or move its legend inside the axes) so the data regions fill the 181 mm width.
- Keep the file in RGB; if the publisher requests CMYK, substitute a CMYK-safe palette (e.g., dark magenta #C0007C and dark green #006633) and re-check contrast, or rely on line style alone in panel (b) by making one trace dashed.
- Add to the caption: total number of frequency bins, "single epoch (n = 1); no averaging across realizations, so no error bars are shown," and retain the explicit ν = 17 statement when comparing noise floors in (b).

### figure2.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2090 x 1107 px — recommended **double column** — 292 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in); 304 DPI at 6.875 in; 597 DPI at single-column 88 mm (3.5 in)
RESOLUTION:     Below threshold — **line art** (4×2 grid of dB-scale line plots, no photographic content); effective 292 DPI at full double-column width vs. the 600 DPI preferred / 300 DPI minimum for color line art. Rendered height at double column = 1107/292 ≈ 3.8 in (96 mm), well inside the 230 mm limit.
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (acceptable for review only) → TIFF (LZW), EPS, or PDF for final submission; vector PDF/EPS strongly preferred for this figure type
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.28 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample budget to raise resolution or embed vector art
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all tick labels, axis labels, panel letters and legend entries are bitmap glyphs; pixel-edge artifacts will be visible in print at 292 DPI
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB per submission requirement) — palette is a saturated dashed blue plus dark forest green on white. If the production workflow converts to CMYK, the saturated blue dashed "True PSD" curve is the most at-risk element (hue shift toward violet) and the dark green may flatten toward near-black, reducing its distinction from the black "Estimate" trace.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution insufficient at the recommended double-column width: 292 DPI < 300 DPI minimum and far below the 600 DPI preferred for line art. The dense oscillatory traces in panels (a), (b), (e) and (f) contain near-Nyquist detail that will alias/moiré at this sampling density — the solid black fill in (e) and the sidelobe comb in (f) are exactly the structures that degrade first.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Tick labels, `$f\Delta$`, `$\hat S(f)$ (dB)`, `$\mathcal{K}(f)$ (dB)` and the panel letters must be vector to meet quality requirements.
  - [BLOCKING] Text size below the 6 pt floor. Main-axis tick digits measure roughly 24–26 px cap height, which is ≈6.0–6.2 pt at 181 mm — marginal. The four inset axes are worse: their tick labels (0, −25, −50, 0.39, 0.40, 0.41, 0.36, 0.44) measure ≈16–18 px, i.e. ≈4.0–4.5 pt at final size. These are unreadable in print and violate the minimum font specification.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend appears only in panel (a) while the same two curve styles recur in (b)–(d). That is acceptable but should be stated in the caption ("legend in (a) applies to (a)–(d)").
  - [ADVISORY] Green kernel color in (e)–(h) is introduced with no legend or in-axes annotation; the reader must infer from the caption that green = expected-value kernel.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely compliant — the caption states one realization, N = 1024, and gives the equivalent degrees of freedom ν = 2, 2, 16.7, 98.4 for the four estimators, which satisfies the requirement to state the estimator's degrees of freedom where noise levels are compared. However, the number of realizations should be stated explicitly as "a single realization (one record, no ensemble averaging)" so that readers do not mistake the smooth curves in (c)/(d) for ensemble means, and the mapping of ν values to panels (a)–(d) should be made explicit rather than positional.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (e) and (f) are clipped at −150 dB and panel (e)'s kernel fills to the axis floor, so the lower bound is a plotting artifact rather than a data feature; state this in the caption or extend the axis.
  - [ADVISORY] Inset zoom windows differ between (e)–(g) (0.39–0.41) and (h) (0.36–0.44). The change in x-span is easy to miss at print size and could be read as a change in kernel width; consider annotating the span inside each inset.
  - [ADVISORY] Aspect ratio 1.89:1 across 8 panels is acceptable at double column but leaves each subpanel only ~43 mm wide; tick label crowding on the shared 0–0.5 x-axes is likely after downscaling.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting source as vector PDF or EPS sized to 181 mm × ~96 mm at creation time (e.g. matplotlib `figsize=(7.13, 3.78)`), so text and lines are vector and no resolution question arises. This is the single fix that clears the first two blocking items.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for the final width: 7.13 in × 600 = **≥4280 px wide** (≈2270 px tall), saved as LZW-compressed TIFF in RGB. Expected size remains well under 10 MB for this mostly-white line art.
  - Set all fonts in the figure-generation script to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 8–9 pt for axis and tick labels at final size, and no smaller than 7 pt for the inset ticks. Reduce the number of inset tick labels (e.g. only the two endpoints and center) to buy room for larger type.
  - Enlarge the insets or move them to a shared, larger single inset if space permits; at present they carry the figure's key quantitative claim (main-lobe shape) at the smallest type size in the figure.
  - Add "Legend in (a) applies to (a)–(d); green curves in (e)–(h) are the expected-value kernels" to the caption, or place a small key in panel (e).
  - Revise the statistics sentence to, e.g., "One realization (N = 1024, no ensemble averaging); the equivalent degrees of freedom are ν = 2 (a), 2 (b), 16.7 (c) and 98.4 (d)." This ties each ν to its panel and makes the single-window basis of the noisy traces unambiguous.
  - Confirm RGB is preserved end-to-end; if any CMYK proof is generated, substitute a print-safe blue (e.g. a slightly darker, less saturated blue) and lighten the green so it remains separable from the black estimate trace.
  - Verify no in-figure title is present (none observed) and keep all descriptive text in the caption below the figure, with lowercase panel labels (a)–(h) as currently used.

### figure3.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     992 x 1258 px — recommended **single** column — 305 DPI at 3.25 in (144 DPI at 6.875 in double column)
RESOLUTION:     Below threshold (for line art) — figure type: line art (2-panel time series + error curves, colored strokes, no photographic content); 305 DPI at single-column width. Meets the 300 DPI floor for colored line art but falls well short of the 600 DPI preferred for line art; unusable at double-column width (144 DPI).

FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.22 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to re-export at 600–1200 DPI
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all axis labels, tick labels, legend entries and panel labels are bitmap glyphs; stem/serif strokes in "RMS error (dB)" and the italic math ($x_k$, $RN\Delta$) show pixel stepping at 100 % zoom

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Axis/legend/panel-label text is not vector, which violates the line-art quality requirement and will look soft in print even at single-column width.

  - [BLOCKING] File format PNG is not an accepted final-submission format (review only). Must be supplied as TIFF, EPS, PDF or PS.
  - [ADVISORY] Font size at final size is marginal. Assuming the image is placed at 3.25 in, the tick labels scale to roughly 7 pt and the four legend entries to roughly 6.5–7 pt — inside the 6 pt absolute floor but below the 8–10 pt preferred band. The legend box occupies a large fraction of panel (b) and will be the first thing to become illegible if the figure is reduced further.
  - [ADVISORY] Blue-vs-red encoding of "Noise" and "Bias" is the only distinguishing feature of those two curves (both are solid, unmarked lines). This is not robust to grayscale reproduction or to red–green/blue color-vision deficiency, and the black solid/black dashed pair is already carrying the linestyle distinction.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is partially complete. The caption correctly states the number of realizations (300) and explicitly states that no error bars are drawn — good. However, the figure compares a noise level (the blue standard-deviation curve) against a bias level, and the estimator's degrees of freedom are not reported. The equivalent number of degrees of freedom of the parabola-smoothed, cosine-tapered periodogram is a function of $R$ (and of the taper's variance-inflation factor) and must be stated.
  - [ADVISORY] The circled points in (b) and the dotted vertical lines are defined only in the caption; the circles are thin unfilled rings that at 305 DPI single-column will be about 1.5 mm across and easy to miss.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) has the dashed taper envelope, black raw record and red tapered record overlapping heavily in the interior of the window, where the red almost completely occludes the black. The visual point (what the taper does at the record ends) is confined to the first and last ~60 samples.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption opens with a standalone title-like phrase ("Choosing the resolution."). No title appears inside the artwork, which is correct, but check that the journal's copy editor does not read the lead-in as a figure title; a descriptive sentence is safer.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting source as a vector PDF or EPS sized to 88 mm (3.46 in) width, with all text kept as embedded Type 1/TrueType outlines rather than rasterized. This resolves the format, resolution and text-rendering blockers in one step.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandatory, render to TIFF (LZW) at 600 DPI minimum for an 88 mm placement, i.e. at least 2080 px wide, and 1200 DPI if the line weights below ~0.5 pt are retained. Current 992 px is insufficient.
  - Set all figure text in a sans-serif face (Arial or Helvetica) at 8 pt minimum as measured at the 88 mm final size; raise the legend text to 8 pt and consider shortening the entries to "Total, whole band" / "Total, peak" / "Noise" / "Bias" so the legend box can shrink.
  - Differentiate the Noise and Bias curves by linestyle as well as hue (e.g. blue dash-dot for noise, red long-dash for bias), or label them with short in-axes text annotations near the curves, so the panel survives grayscale printing.
  - Increase the circled-point markers to ~2.5 mm diameter with a ~0.8 pt stroke, and add short in-panel annotations "(b)", "(c)", "(d)" next to them keyed to Fig. \ref{fig:motif}, so the cross-reference does not depend entirely on the caption.
  - In panel (a), consider plotting the untapered record in light gray behind the red tapered record, and/or insetting a magnification of the first 128 samples, so the effect of the 25 % cosine taper is visible at single-column size.
  - Add the estimator's degrees of freedom to the caption. For example: "the smoothed periodogram has an equivalent number of degrees of freedom of $\nu(R)\approx\ldots$, ranging from $\nu=\ldots$ at $RN\Delta=1$ to $\nu=\ldots$ at $RN\Delta=64$; the blue curve is the corresponding standard deviation." Also state explicitly that the 300 realizations are independent draws of the same AR(4) process with $N=1024$, and whether the RMS error is averaged over frequency before or after the ensemble average.
  - Confirm the delivered file is RGB (not CMYK or indexed-color PNG) and, if the publisher converts to CMYK, pre-check the blue (#0000FF-like) curve for the typical shift toward violet; a slightly desaturated blue such as #1F5FBF reproduces more predictably.
  - Keep the two panels in a single stacked file as they are; the 1:1.27 width-to-height ratio at 88 mm gives a 112 mm tall figure, well within the 230 mm page limit, and is the right choice for this content.

Reconcile the normalizations.

  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution is 292 DPI at the mandatory double-column width (8 panels across 4 columns cannot be legibly reduced to 88 mm). This is below the 300 DPI floor and less than half the 600 DPI preferred for line art.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and all curves are rasterized. For a line-art figure of this type IEEE TBME expects vector artwork (or ≥600 DPI raster); rasterized 1-px-wide dotted grid lines and thin kernel traces (panels f, h, where the oscillation envelope is drawn by densely packed lines) will alias or partially disappear at 292 DPI.
  - [ADVISORY] Inset tick labels (e.g. "−25", "−50", "0.39", "0.41") render at roughly 7 pt equivalent at 181 mm width — above the 6 pt floor but the smallest text in the figure; inset axis labels are absent, so the reader must infer units from the parent panel.

  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely compliant: the caption states one realization for (a)–(d), 300 realizations for the ensemble median absolute errors, and the estimator degrees of freedom (ν = 17.2, 14.0, 16.7, 17.6). However, no error bars or distribution spread are shown for the 300-realization values; if the reader is meant to compare noise levels, an interquartile range or equivalent dispersion measure should be reported alongside the medians (or explicitly deferred to Table ref).
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (a)–(d) share identical y-axes but repeat the full tick-label set; panels (e)–(h) likewise. This is acceptable, but the repeated labels consume width that could be given to the data at double-column size.
  - [ADVISORY] Green ("Estimate", panel a) and dark-green kernel traces (e–h) versus the blue dashed reference may be hard to separate for readers with deuteranopia, especially where the traces overlap near the 0.1–0.15 peak.

                (reference points: 1981/3.5 in = 566 DPI single column; 1981/6.875 in = 288 DPI; 1981/7.16 in = 277 DPI)
                Height at double-column width = 2078/1981 × 7.16 in = 7.51 in ≈ **191 mm** — within the 230 mm limit, but this is a near-full-page figure, so plan the layout accordingly. Aspect ratio (0.95 W:H) is acceptable for an 8-panel stack; it is *not* usable at single-column width (panel (h) tick labels and the ν annotations would fall to ~3–4 pt).

RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — mixed figure: panels (b)–(g) are halftone (continuous-tone spectrograms), panels (a) and (h) plus all axes/labels/colorbar are line art. Actual 277 DPI at 7.16 in: short of the 300 DPI halftone minimum and far short of the 600 DPI line-art preference.

FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → vector PDF or EPS (TIFF acceptable if a flat raster is unavoidable). PNG is accepted for review only.

FILE SIZE:      OK — 1.48 MB (well under 10 MB). Note that a flat 600 DPI TIFF of this canvas (≈4300 × 4500 px RGB) would be ~55 MB uncompressed; use LZW/ZIP compression or, preferably, the hybrid vector route below.

TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all text is baked into the PNG bitmap. In a figure whose line-art content (panel (a) traces, panel (h) markers, every axis and tick label) dominates the visual field, this is a blocking quality issue.

COLOR MODE:     Metadata does not state the color mode; confirm **RGB** before upload. Substantive concern: the spectrograms use a jet-type colormap. The saturated mid-cyan (~0 dB), yellow-green (~5 dB) and pure red (>12 dB) bands are exactly the hues that shift most under any CMYK conversion, and jet is not perceptually uniform — equal PSD steps do not read as equal color steps, which undermines the "one colour scale" comparison the caption relies on.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] 277 DPI at the recommended 181 mm double-column width is below the 300 DPI halftone minimum and well below the 600 DPI line-art target. Re-export at higher resolution rather than upscaling.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text throughout a figure that is predominantly line art (8 panels of axes, tick labels, legends, ν annotations in (h)).
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is a review-only format; a TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS submission file is required.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest type is marginal: axis labels measure ≈7.5–8 pt at 181 mm width, but the numeric annotations in (h) ("1.1 Hz", "ν = 2.0") and the colorbar tick labels come out at ≈6.5–7 pt. Any further reduction breaches the 6 pt floor. Verify all text ≥8 pt after final scaling.
  - [ADVISORY] Jet colormap: perceptually non-uniform and not colorblind-safe; the dark-red band below ~3 Hz appears clipped at the +12.5 dB ceiling in (b)–(g), so low-frequency differences between estimators are not readable. Caption does not state that the scale is clipped.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting incomplete per journal policy. The caption gives ν for each setting (good, and the degrees-of-freedom disclosure for the noise comparison is satisfied), but it does not state the number of time windows behind each spectrogram (40 s stepped by 0.1 s ⇒ ~391/381/361 windows for 1/2/4 s windows), nor the sampling rate, nor the number of tapers L actually used at each α (only the rule L = 2α − 1 and the L = 1 special case are given). No error bars are shown anywhere; state that panels (b)–(h) derive from a single 40 s epoch from one subject/recording (n = 1) so readers do not infer replication.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (h) reuses black/red marker coding that matches panel (a)'s black/red trace coding for an unrelated distinction; consider distinct colors or shapes to avoid implying a link.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (b)/(c) and (d)/(e) omit an x-axis label; only (f)/(g) are labelled "Time (s)". Acceptable as shared-axis practice, but confirm this is intentional and that (a)'s independent time axis is not read as shared.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export as a single vector PDF (or EPS) from the plotting library with `rasterized=True` applied **only** to the pcolormesh/imshow spectrogram surfaces in (b)–(g) and the colorbar gradient, at a raster DPI of 400–600. All axes, ticks, labels, legends, panel letters, (a)'s traces and (h)'s markers then remain true vector. This satisfies both the 600 DPI line-art preference and the 300 DPI halftone minimum while keeping the file a few MB.
  - If a flat raster is required, re-render (do not upscale) at 7.16 in × 7.51 in with dpi = 600, i.e. ≈4300 × 4500 px, and save as TIFF with LZW compression; verify <10 MB.
  - Set figure size explicitly to 7.16 in wide at creation time and set all font sizes to 8 pt (10 pt for axis labels), so nothing is scaled at the publisher's end. Raise the (h) annotations and colorbar tick labels to ≥8 pt.
  - Confirm/convert to RGB and embed an sRGB profile. Replace jet with a perceptually uniform, colorblind-safe map (viridis, or cmocean `thermal`/`balance` if a diverging scale is wanted) to preserve the cross-panel PSD comparison in print and under CMYK.
  - Either extend the color scale to include the low-frequency maximum or state explicitly in the caption that values above +12.5 dB (and below −5 dB) are clipped.
  - Extend the caption with: sampling rate; window/step counts per spectrogram (e.g. "391, 381 and 361 windows for the 1, 2 and 4 s cases"); the number of tapers L used at each α; and a statement that all panels come from one 40 s N2 epoch in a single recording (n = 1), with no averaging across realizations and hence no error bars. Keep the existing ν values — they are the required degrees-of-freedom disclosure for the resolution/variance comparison.
  - Caption formatting is otherwise compliant: no in-figure title, lowercase (a)–(h) panel labels matching the artwork, caption placed below the figure.
