The ideal box is included as a common reference. Normalization is explicit ($\mathcal{K}(f)/N\Delta$ in (a), dB relative to peak in (b)), so curves are genuinely commensurable.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    Present — not misleading, but two items to note.
  (i) Panel (b) is floored at $-100$ dB, which clips the Bohman (olive) curve where it descends past $-100$ dB near $|fN\Delta|\approx 20$–25. The reader cannot tell how much further it falls, which understates the best-performing kernel. This is conventional for dB plots but the floor should be acknowledged, or extended to e.g. $-120$ dB.
  (ii) The two panels use different abscissa ranges ($\pm10$ vs $\pm25$) without any visual cue (no shaded inset region, no bracket) indicating that (a) is a zoom of the central portion of (b). Readers may misread (b) as a different computation rather than a wider view.
  Panel (a) correctly includes zero on the ordinate; no exaggeration there.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present (mild) — In panel (a) the Hann (cyan), Bohman (olive dashed), untapered (green), and ideal box (black dotted) curves nearly coincide across the plateau $|fN\Delta|\lesssim 3$, and in panel (b) four curves converge and overlap in the $0$ to $-5$ dB main-lobe region. Which curve lies on top is determined by draw order, not by the physics. In panel (b) the cyan and olive traces also cross repeatedly between $-40$ and $-80$ dB, making them hard to follow. Additionally, the black multitaper trace has high-frequency ripple that is dense enough in (b) that at print size the oscillations may alias or blur into a band.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No significance annotations, p-values, or statistical tests are shown or implied. Nothing to correct.

TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — Abscissa is normalized frequency, not time. Curves are continuous evaluations with no gaps, so there is no misleading interpolation.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Caption claims "the skirts differ by 40 dB," but the panels appear to show a larger spread: in the far field ($|fN\Delta|\approx 20$) the untapered (green) trace sits near $-33$ dB while the Bohman (olive) trace is at or below $-95$ dB, i.e. a separation closer to 60–65 dB. The 40 dB figure may refer to a specific frequency or to a specific pair of kernels. Verify the number and state explicitly *which kernels* and *at what frequency* the stated separation is evaluated.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) ordinate is clipped at $-100$ dB, truncating the Bohman curve. The best-performing kernel is the one whose performance is hidden.
  - [ADVISORY] The $\nu$ (equivalent degrees of freedom) values — the quantity being traded against skirt decay — appear only in prose. They are not readable from the figure, so the central bias–variance tradeoff is not self-evident from the graphic.
  - [ADVISORY] Color-vision accessibility: the palette relies on red/green as distinct categories, and green vs. olive are close in hue. Bohman is dashed and the ideal box is dotted, which helps, but untapered (green), Hann (cyan), and the 25% cosine taper (red) are distinguished by hue alone.
  - [ADVISORY] No panel-(b) legend and no visual indication that (a) is a zoom of (b); the differing x-ranges are easy to overlook.
  - [ADVISORY] Curve overlap in the plateau/main-lobe regions makes z-order, rather than data, determine visibility.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Reconcile the "40 dB" claim with the plotted curves; restate as, e.g., "the skirts differ by more than 60 dB at $|f|N\Delta=20$" or specify the kernel pair and frequency to which 40 dB applies.
  - Extend the panel (b) ordinate to $-120$ dB (or annotate the $-100$ dB floor as a clipping limit) so the Bohman decay is not cut off.
  - Append $\nu$ to each legend entry, e.g. "Multitaper, $L=7$ ($\nu=14.0$)", "Hann, then box ($\nu=8.9$)". This makes the resolution-versus-variance tradeoff legible without the caption.
  - Add a shaded band or bracket in panel (b) spanning $|fN\Delta|\le 10$, with a note that panel (a) is that region on a linear scale; or state the ranges explicitly in the caption.
  - Differentiate curves redundantly by line style as well as color (e.g. solid / dash / dash-dot / dotted) and consider a colorblind-safe palette; reduce the linewidth or add slight transparency on the overlapping plateau traces, or offset them in a small inset, so coincident curves remain individually visible.
  - Repeat an abbreviated legend in panel (b), or label two or three curves inline in the skirt region where they are well separated, so panel (b) is readable standalone.
  - Confirm that the multitaper ripple is sampled densely enough that the plotted oscillation amplitude is faithful and not an artifact of the evaluation grid; if the ripple is visually aliased at final print size, consider a lightly smoothed envelope overlay with the raw ripple retained.
  - State in the caption the frequency grid / number of evaluation points used, so the figure is reproducible.
---

### efigure2.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — Line/trace plots are the correct choice for PSD estimates over a continuous frequency axis; no bar/pie misuse. One structural concern: the quantity of interest in (b) is a *bias floor*, but it is displayed as raw fluctuating traces, so the reader must eyeball a mean through ±10 dB of jitter. Consider overlaying the ensemble-mean bias (or a heavily smoothed envelope) as a bold line on top of the raw traces, or replacing the raw traces in (b) with mean ± quantile bands.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible (as an ensemble) — $N=1024$ data points is stated in the caption, but the figure shows **one realization only** ($n=1$ realization). This is the critical statistical-visibility issue: every claim in the caption about "floors X dB high," "halves that," and "track the truth" is a statement about *expected* behavior, yet it is supported by a single draw. The quoted equivalent degrees of freedom ($\nu = 2, 17.2, 14, 12, 16.7$) are theoretical and are not represented graphically at all.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — The seven estimators are all computed from the *same* realization, i.e. this is inherently **paired data**, and the pairing is exploited correctly in (b) (each estimate minus the same truth). Good. However: (i) the comparison of variance between estimators is confounded by the differing $\nu$, and nothing in the figure encodes $\nu$; (ii) the visual "halving" claim (black → cyan) is a comparison of two traces whose relative vertical offset is partly random at $n=1$; (iii) the periodogram (grey) and True PSD (blue dashed) appear in the legend but not in panel (b), so the legend over-promises for the right-hand panel.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** None (misleading) — Both panels use decibel (log-power) axes, which is standard and disclosed by the axis labels. (a) spans −35 to 60 dB, (b) spans −10 to 30 dB. No baseline suppression of the kind that exaggerates differences in bar charts. Minor note: the two panels use different y-ranges and different gridline spacings (20 dB vs 5 dB), which visually magnifies panel (b) differences relative to panel (a); this is defensible for a residual plot but should be obvious to readers, so state the (b) scale explicitly in the caption.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — Substantial, and it is the most serious presentational defect. In (a) beyond $f\Delta \approx 0.25$ and throughout (b) beyond $f\Delta \approx 0.2$, the high-variance traces (grey, black, cyan, olive) are solid bands of ink drawn in an arbitrary z-order. The black $L=7$ band visibly occludes the cyan $L=6$ band, and cyan in turn occludes olive; a reader cannot verify that cyan's floor is half of black's because cyan's upper excursions are hidden. Draw order is doing analytical work that should be done by the data.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No inferential annotations, p-values, or significance brackets are present; none are appropriate here. Flag only that the caption makes six implicit estimator-vs-truth comparisons with no stated uncertainty, which is a *precision* rather than a multiplicity problem.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — The abscissa is normalized frequency, evenly sampled across $[0, 0.5]$ with no gaps; no misleading interpolation across missing points. (The underlying time series is stated to be complete at $N=1024$.)

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Single-realization evidence for ensemble claims. The caption asserts specific bias floors (20 dB, 10 dB, "halves that") and that two estimators "track the truth." These are expectation-level statements shown with $n=1$. Either soften the caption to descriptive language about this realization, or add ensemble evidence.
- [BLOCKING] Overplotting/occlusion determines the reader's conclusion. The relative floor heights of black ($L=7$), cyan ($L=6$), and olive (adaptive) in panel (b) cannot be verified because later-drawn traces mask earlier ones. This is exactly the case where ink density, not data, drives interpretation.
- [ADVISORY] Equivalent degrees of freedom $\nu$ are quoted for five of seven traces but never encoded in the figure, so the variance–bias trade-off central to the message is invisible. A reader cannot see that the grey periodogram's $\nu=2$ explains its huge spread.
- [ADVISORY] Legend placement implies it covers both panels, but the periodogram and True PSD entries do not apply to (b).
- [ADVISORY] Caption/figure numerical mismatch: green in (b) plateaus at roughly 18 dB, not 20 dB; black peaks near 12–13 dB with a mean nearer 10 dB. State whether the quoted values are read from the plot or are theoretical.
- [ADVISORY] Colour accessibility: green vs olive vs cyan are difficult to separate under common colour-vision deficiencies and in greyscale print, and they are the three traces the argument hinges on.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a third panel (or a supplementary panel) showing the **ensemble-mean bias over $M$ independent realizations** (state $M$ explicitly, e.g. $M=1000$) with shaded interquartile or 5–95% bands per estimator. Retain the present single-realization panels to convey texture, but let the ensemble panel carry the quantitative claims.
- In panel (b), plot each estimator's smoothed/mean bias as an opaque bold line and its raw fluctuation as a low-alpha (α ≈ 0.25) band of the same hue. This removes the z-order dependence entirely and makes the "halves that" claim directly readable.
- Alternatively, split panel (b) into small multiples (one estimator per mini-panel, shared axes) so no trace occludes another.
- Encode $\nu$ in the figure: append it to each legend entry (already partly done in the caption) and/or draw the theoretical $\chi^2_\nu$ 95% interval as a thin band around 0 dB in panel (b) for each estimator. This lets a reader judge whether an observed offset exceeds sampling noise at $n=1$.
- Recolour to a colour-vision-safe, greyscale-separable palette, and differentiate the three multitaper variants by line style (solid / dashed / dotted) in addition to hue.
- State the panel (b) y-scale and the number of realizations in the caption's first sentence; revise "floors 20 dB high" to the value actually plotted, or label it as the theoretical asymptote.
- Give each panel its own legend, restricted to the traces it actually contains.

### efigure3.png

---
**CONTEXT NOTE:** This is a deterministic/analytical performance-characterization figure (exact kernel and degrees-of-freedom computations for spectral estimators), not an empirical dataset with sampling variability. Criteria 1–3 and 6 of the standard distributional-integrity checklist therefore apply only in modified form; they are reported below for completeness rather than as substantive defects.

PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Line-plus-marker plots against a continuous resolution axis are the correct choice for a parameter sweep of deterministic quantities. No bar graphs, no pie charts, no error bars implying a distribution that is not shown. Panel (b) correctly uses a log ordinate for a quantity spanning ~7 decades; panel (c) correctly uses dB. No plot-type substitution (dot/box/violin) is warranted because there is no underlying sample to display.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Visible (in the relevant sense) — There is no statistical n; the caption states $N=256$ and that curves are exact with no sampling variability. Every computed parameter value is marked with a discrete symbol, so the number and location of evaluation points is directly readable from the figure (roughly 6–12 per curve). This is the deterministic analogue of "individual data points shown" and is good practice. The caption should nevertheless state *which* parameter values were swept (e.g., $\alpha$ or $L$ ranges, segment lengths for Welch) so the marker spacing is interpretable.

COMPARISON:         Concern — Two issues, neither statistical:
(i) Panel (c) plots a quantity that is *not the same quantity* for all curves: for the Gaussian, parabolic and Welch kernels it is the main-lobe skirt, for the others it is a genuine side-lobe peak. The caption discloses this, but the figure itself gives no visual cue, so a reader comparing the magenta and grey curves against the cyan/olive curves at equal resolution will draw an invalid conclusion. This is the closest analogue to an invalid group comparison in this figure and should be marked graphically.
(ii) Curves terminate at widely different maximum half-power widths (Welch extends to ~24.6, most others stop near 16, sine tapers and Hann-then-Gaussian near 9.5). The truncation points are sweep limits, not data limits, and without explanation they read as if some methods "stop working."

AXIS TRUNCATION:    None (not misleading) — Panel (a) ordinate begins at 0, which is correct for a ratio-scale quantity. Panels (b) and (c) are log/dB scales where a zero origin is undefined, so the absence of a zero baseline is unavoidable and not deceptive. The panel (c) ordinate top limit (~-5 dB) clips close to the flat magenta/grey curves; this compresses nothing of substance but leaves those curves visually pinned to the frame edge.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present (mild) — Two locations: (1) the low-resolution corner of all three panels (half-power width ≲ 4), where up to nine curves converge and markers collide, and (2) panel (a) between widths 5–16, where the red, green, grey, black and magenta curves are nearly coincident and the distinct series cannot be separated. Marker-shape and line-style encoding partially rescues this, but at print size the black circle/black square/black triangle distinction plus dotted/dashed/solid is hard to resolve.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No p-values, significance brackets, or inferential annotations appear. Nine methods are compared, but comparison is by exact computation, so no correction question arises.

TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — The abscissa is achieved resolution (a swept design parameter), not time. However, the same interpolation caveat applies: straight segments are drawn between discrete, in some cases necessarily integer-valued, parameter settings (taper count $L$, number of Welch segments). The connecting lines imply a continuum of achievable operating points that may not exist for the multitaper and Welch families.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (c) superimposes two non-equivalent quantities (side-lobe peak vs. main-lobe skirt) on one axis with no graphical distinction. Caption-only disclosure is insufficient; a reader scanning the figure will compare them as like-for-like and conclude that Welch/Gaussian/parabolic kernels have side lobes ~40 dB worse than Bohman-then-box, which is not what the numbers mean.
  - [ADVISORY] Unexplained and unequal curve termination points across the three panels. Sweep ranges must be stated so that truncation is not read as method failure or as a data gap.
  - [ADVISORY] Lines interpolate between discrete, possibly integer-constrained parameter settings, implying achievable intermediate operating points. Markers mitigate but do not remove the implication.
  - [ADVISORY] Severe curve overlap in panel (a) for widths 5–16 and in the low-width corner of all panels; nine series distinguished largely by hue, with three black series separated only by line style and small marker shape.
  - [ADVISORY] Figure relies on colour as the primary series key; grey/black/olive and green/cyan pairs are at risk under greyscale printing and for red–green colour-vision deficiency.
  - [ADVISORY] The vertical dotted reference line at $8/(N\Delta)$ is unlabelled in-figure; its meaning is recoverable only from the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) ordinate label and panel (b) ordinate label describe thresholds ($1.5\times$, $2\times$ the half-power half-width) that are easy to conflate; the two panels use different multipliers, which invites misreading.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - In panel (c), visually separate the two quantity types: either split into (c) side-lobe methods and (d) no-flat-top methods, or plot the main-lobe-skirt curves with a distinct rendering (e.g., open/greyed markers with a thin line) plus an in-panel annotation such as "skirt, not side lobe" and a legend sub-heading. At minimum add a legend group label.
  - Add the swept parameter values to the caption or as light secondary annotations (e.g., $L$ printed beside the first and last multitaper markers, segment count for Welch) and state explicitly that curves end at the sweep limit, not at a performance boundary.
  - Where the swept parameter is integer-valued, either retain markers without connecting lines, or keep the lines and state in the caption that segments are guides to the eye between discrete realizable settings.
  - Reduce overlap in panel (a): consider an inset or a second panel showing $\nu$ normalized by half-power width (or $\nu$ divided by the untapered reference), which will spread the near-coincident black/red/green/grey/magenta curves.
  - Make the series key redundant to colour: retain the existing distinct marker shapes but enlarge them, ensure each of the three black series has a clearly different marker, and verify legibility in greyscale and under a deuteranopia simulation.
  - Extend the panel (c) ordinate top limit by ~3–5 dB so the flat magenta and grey curves are not pinned to the frame, and label the $8/(N\Delta)$ reference line directly within each panel.
  - Consider harmonizing the two leakage thresholds (both at $2\times$, or both at $1.5\times$) or annotating each panel with its threshold in-figure to prevent conflation.
  - State in the caption that no error bars are shown *because* the computation is exact, so reviewers and readers do not read the absence of uncertainty as an omission.
---

### efigure4.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a deterministic/analytical figure (filter-bank responses, eigen-tapers, eigenvalue ladders, kernel transfer functions), not a display of sampled empirical data. Line plots in (a)–(h) and (m)–(p) are the correct choice for continuous functions of $k$ and $f$. The bar charts in (i)–(l) are also appropriate here: each bar is a single exact eigenvalue $c_i/\sum c_i$, not a mean of a distribution, so the usual "bar chart hides the distribution / show individual points" objection does not apply. No pie charts, no error bars, no inferential statistics — the standard distribution-hiding concerns are not triggered.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible — Not a statistical $n$, but the analogous determining parameters are also not inferable from the figure alone. The $k$ axis implies a series/filter length of 256 samples, and the truncation of the ladders in (i)–(l) implies the number of retained tapers ($K\approx 7$, 12, 11, 7 respectively), but $N$, $\Delta$, the time–bandwidth product, $R$, and the nominal $K$ are never stated in-figure or in the caption. Only four of the eigen-tapers are drawn (e–h) while the ladders show 7–12 non-negligible eigenvalues, so the displayed subset is not self-describing.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — The figure is built as a 4-column × 4-row matrix inviting column-to-column comparison, but the columns are unlabelled in this panel (the caption defers to "Columns as in Fig. 5"). A supplementary/extended figure should be readable standalone. Additionally, the $y$-limits differ between panels within the same row: (d) spans about $-0.1$ to $0.2$, (f) appears to top out near $0.1$, and (g) extends to $\sim0.25$, while (e) and (h) use yet other limits. Unequal scaling across panels intended for side-by-side comparison visually exaggerates or suppresses amplitude differences between methods. Legends appear only in (d)/(e) but the same colour code is reused in (f)–(h) and, apparently, in (c) (which reuses the $f'N\Delta$ code from (b)) without restatement.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present — but mostly defensible. (i)–(l) correctly begin at zero, which is the case where truncation would have been misleading. (m)–(p) are truncated at $-80$ dB; this is conventional for log-magnitude kernels, but it does hide how far the $-40$ dB ripple floor in (p) and the $-30$ dB shoulders in (o) actually extend, and the caption's leakage figures (0.05 %, 0.13 %, 1.39 %, 0.22 %) cannot be verified against a clipped dB view. The unequal $y$-limits noted above in (d)–(h) are the more substantive concern.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — Two forms. (1) In (p), the ripple oscillation period approaches the pixel pitch of the raster; at this resolution the sidelobe envelope is subject to aliasing/moiré and the true ripple depth is not recoverable. (2) In (a)–(h), four to seven similarly shaped coloured curves cross repeatedly (especially (e), where all four traces interleave heavily); colour alone is doing all the discrimination, and red/green adjacency is a colour-vision-deficiency hazard.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No hypothesis tests, $p$-values, or significance annotations are present; nothing to correct.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — Abscissae are sample index $k$ and normalised frequency $fN\Delta$, both regularly and completely sampled. No gaps, no interpolation across missing timepoints.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Panel columns are not identified within the figure; the caption points to another figure for column identity, so this extended figure is not standalone-interpretable.
- [ADVISORY] Inconsistent $y$-axis limits across panels (d)–(h) intended for cross-method comparison; this distorts apparent relative taper amplitudes.
- [ADVISORY] Defining parameters ($N$, $\Delta$, time–bandwidth product, $R$, number of tapers $K$ per column) are absent from both figure and caption; the reader cannot reconstruct what was computed.
- [ADVISORY] Legends omitted in (c) and (f)–(h) despite reuse of colour codes; panel (c) in particular has no key at all.
- [ADVISORY] Possible raster aliasing of the fine ripple structure in (p); reported leakage percentages are not verifiable from the clipped dB axis.
- [ADVISORY] Red/green curve pairing in (d)–(h) is not colour-blind safe.
- [ADVISORY] Quantitative claims in the caption (half-power widths 5.84, 8.03, 8.03, $7.41/(N\Delta)$) have no corresponding graphical annotation in (m)–(p).

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add column headers naming the four methods (e.g., sliding sinc, Thomson, etc.) directly above panels (a)–(d).
- Harmonise $y$-limits within rows 1–2 (e.g., fix all eigen-taper panels to $-0.12$ to $0.20$) so amplitudes are directly comparable; if a panel genuinely needs a different range, mark the break explicitly.
- State $N$, $\Delta$, $R$, time–bandwidth product, and the number of tapers retained per column in the caption; annotate $K$ on each ladder panel (i)–(l), e.g., with a vertical marker at the cutoff index.
- Repeat or share a single legend for the $i=1\ldots4$ colour code and add an explicit key to (c).
- Replace pure red/green with a colour-blind-safe qualitative palette, and/or differentiate the four tapers additionally by line style (solid/dashed/dash-dot/dotted).
- Render (m)–(p) as vector output, or increase raster resolution/apply mild anti-aliasing, so the (p) ripple envelope is faithful; consider adding a shaded envelope or a horizontal reference line at the mean sidelobe floor.
- Annotate the half-power width on each kernel panel (e.g., a horizontal $-3$ dB segment spanning the measured width) and report the out-of-band leakage as a number inside each panel, so the caption's claims are visually verifiable.
- Consider adding a zoomed inset near $|f|\le R$ in (m)–(p) so the shoulder behaviour distinguishing the four kernels is resolvable.
---

### figure1.png

---
**Context note:** This is a methods/validation figure showing deterministic numerical agreement between three algebraically related PSD estimators, not a group-comparison figure. Most items in the statistical-visualization checklist (bar graphs, error bars, paired designs, multiple comparisons) are therefore not applicable. The relevant risks here are (i) replicate/sample visibility, (ii) log-axis clipping and precision floors, and (iii) overplotted dense traces and perfectly coincident curves.

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Line traces vs. frequency for panels (a)/(b) and a log–log convergence plot for (c) are the correct choices; no distributional summary is being hidden behind a bar or error bar. One caveat: panel (a) is, by design, three curves that lie exactly on top of one another, so it conveys "no visible difference" rather than quantitative agreement; it depends entirely on panel (b) to carry the evidence. Consider explicitly stating in the caption that coincidence in (a) is the intended result and that the dashed/dotted styles are chosen so underlying curves remain visible.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — The figure itself does not display $N=1024$, $R=1$ Hz, $\alpha=4$, $\nu=17$, or the fact that this is **one** epoch from **one** channel of **one** subject; all of this lives only in the caption. For a validation claim, n = 1 record. Because the three estimators are related analytically (the eigenvalue-weighted multitaper sum is a boxcar smoother in the limit), the agreement is expected to be data-independent — but the figure as presented cannot distinguish "algebraic identity" from "coincidence on this particular record." Individual-replicate display is warranted in the sense of showing the result across several records.

COMPARISON:         Valid — The three estimates are computed from the *same* data record, and panel (b) correctly shows the paired, per-frequency difference rather than an unpaired overlay of separate summaries. This is the right structure. Minor: panel (b) plots $|{\cdot}|$, which discards the sign of the sliding-window bias; a signed difference (or a second trace) would reveal whether the $O(1/N_s)$ error is a systematic bias or zero-mean fluctuation, which matters for interpreting panel (c).

AXIS TRUNCATION:    None (misleading) / Present (benign) — Panel (a) y-axis spans roughly 17–47 dB with no zero baseline, but dB is a relative log scale with no meaningful zero and the curves are not bar heights, so this is not a misleading truncation. Panels (b) and (c) use log y-axes, which is necessary given the 13-decade range. **However:** the green "Box − multitaper" trace in (b) sits at ~$10^{-15}$–$10^{-14}$, i.e. flush against the lower axis limit of $10^{-15}$. Verify that no samples are being clipped at the axis boundary (and that no exact zeros were silently dropped, as zeros cannot be rendered on a log axis). If zeros were dropped, the visual density of that trace is misleading.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Panel (b): both traces are high-frequency, densely sampled noise floors drawn as solid thin lines across ~513 frequency bins; the magenta trace in particular is a solid block of overlapping excursions in which individual structure is unreadable and the apparent "thickness" is a rendering artifact rather than data density. Panel (a): three coincident curves are intentionally overplotted; readability rests entirely on dash/dot phasing, and at this figure width the dotted magenta may be partly obscured where the black line is thick.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No inferential comparisons or significance annotations are present. Panel (b) does implicitly report a maximum over ~513 frequency bins ("agrees to $3\times10^{-13}$ dB", "0.06 dB"), which is a max-statistic rather than a p-value, so no correction applies; but the caption should state that these are maxima over the plotted frequency range, not typical values.

TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — Abscissae are frequency (a, b) and window length (c), not time. In panel (c), five points at $N_s/N = 1,2,4,8,16$ are connected by straight segments on a log–log axis; the intervals are even in $\log_2$, so the connection is not misleading, but the line between points is interpolation, not measurement.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Sample/replicate provenance is invisible in the figure and thin in the record: a single 8 s epoch from a single channel of one de-identified archival recording. A numerical-equivalence claim stated to 13 significant figures should be demonstrated to be record-independent, or explicitly framed as a verification of an analytic identity on one illustrative record.
  - [BLOCKING] Possible axis-limit clipping of the green trace in panel (b) at $10^{-15}$, and unstated handling of exact-zero differences on a log axis. The headline claim "agrees to $3\times10^{-13}$ dB" cannot be audited if part of that trace is clipped or censored.
  - [ADVISORY] The $3\times10^{-13}$ dB agreement is at or near double-precision round-off for this computation; the caption does not state arithmetic precision, FFT implementation, or whether the box smoother was applied with identical edge/wrap conventions. Without this, the reader cannot tell whether the residual is round-off or a genuine (if tiny) systematic discrepancy.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) shows only $|{\rm difference}|$, hiding the sign and therefore whether the sliding-sinc error is a bias or a fluctuation. This weakens the interpretation of the $1/N_s$ trend in (c).
  - [ADVISORY] Severe overplotting in panel (b); the magenta trace reads as a filled band. Reported quantities (max, median) are not recoverable by eye.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) rests on five points; the $1/N_s$ guide is visually offset from the data at both ends (data fall below the guide for $N_s/N \ge 4$), suggesting the empirical slope is slightly steeper than $-1$. No fitted slope or uncertainty is reported, so "$\propto 1/N_s$" is asserted rather than demonstrated.
  - [ADVISORY] In-panel identification of key parameters ($N$, $R$, $\alpha$, $\nu$) is absent, making the panels non-self-contained if reproduced or viewed separately.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) relies on coincident curves; equivalence is shown only as visual indistinguishability, with no in-panel cue that this is the intended result.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add an in-panel annotation block to (a): "$N=1024$, $f_s=128$ Hz, $R=1$ Hz ($\alpha=4$), $\nu=17$, 1 epoch, 1 channel." Repeat $N$ in (c).
  - Demonstrate record-independence: overlay the panel-(b) difference traces for, say, 20–50 epochs/channels as light translucent lines with a dark median, or add a small inset histogram of the per-record maximum difference. If the equivalence is an exact algebraic identity, state that in the caption and cite/derive it, so the figure is framed as a numerical check rather than an empirical finding.
  - Extend the panel-(b) lower y-limit (e.g. to $10^{-16}$) or clamp exact zeros to a stated floor with a note; confirm in the caption that no data are clipped. State the arithmetic precision (e.g. IEEE 754 double) and note that the box-smoother residual is at the round-off level.
  - Reduce overplotting in (b): use thin lines with alpha ~0.5, or replace each dense trace with a median line plus a shaded 5th–95th-percentile band computed over frequency bins; alternatively decimate or plot on a coarser frequency grid and report the max separately as a horizontal reference line with a text label.
  - Plot the signed difference in (b) (or add a companion panel), so bias vs. fluctuation is distinguishable.
  - In (c), report a fitted log–log slope with a confidence interval, or state that the dotted line is an eye guide anchored at $N_s/N=1$ and not a fit. Consider plotting markers only (no connecting line) to avoid implying intermediate measurements, and add more $N_s$ values if cheap.
  - In the caption, specify that $3\times10^{-13}$ dB and $0.06$ dB are maxima over $0$–$40$ Hz, and give median values alongside them.
  - Increase dash/dot period or reduce the black line width in (a) so both the green dashed and magenta dotted traces remain visible everywhere; verify at final print size and in grayscale.

### figure2.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — Line plots of PSD estimates vs. frequency and of kernel response in dB are the correct representation; no bar/pie misuse. One caveat: in panel (e) the Fejér kernel's sidelobe oscillations are so dense relative to the pixel grid that they render as a solid filled block, which visually reads as a broadband floor rather than as an oscillatory sidelobe structure. This is a rendering/undersampling artifact of the same family as overplotting, and it makes (e) non-comparable in appearance to (f)–(h).

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Partially visible — $N=1024$ is stated in the caption but not annotated in the figure, and the fact that panels (a)–(d) show a **single realization** is only inferable from the caption. Because $n=1$ realization, the displayed scatter is one draw from a $\chi^2_\nu$ sampling distribution; nothing in the figure conveys the uncertainty of the estimator other than the visible roughness. The quoted equivalent degrees of freedom ($\nu = 2, 2, 16.7, 98.4$) are the actual statistical "sample size" of each estimate and appear only in prose.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — Two issues:
(i) The insets in (e)–(h) do **not** use a common x-range: (e)–(g) span $0.39$–$0.41$ while (h) spans $0.36$–$0.44$, a 4× wider window. A reader comparing main-lobe widths across insets will substantially underestimate how much wider the (h) kernel is. This is the visual-comparison equivalent of changing scale between conditions.
(ii) Panels (a)–(d) are the *same realization* processed four ways — i.e., paired/repeated-measures data — but are shown as four independent panels. There is no direct overlay or difference view that lets the reader confirm that the smoothing, not realization-to-realization variability, causes the change.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present — Two forms, one benign, one potentially misleading:
- (a)–(d) share a $[-40, 60]$ dB range, but in (a) and (b) the estimate excursions appear to reach and be clipped at the $-40$ dB bottom edge. Clipped data at an axis boundary should be flagged: the reader cannot tell how deep the periodogram nulls go, which affects the apparent dynamic range of the "noise" claim.
- (e)–(h) are floored at $-150$ dB. In (f) the deep nulls are clipped against the floor; in (e) the kernel structure is entirely lost in the fill. dB floors are conventional in spectral analysis and not deceptive per se, but the floor value should be justified (e.g., numerical precision limit) so readers do not read the flat bottom as data.
- Inset axes run $0$ to $-50$ dB while the parent axes run to $-150$; the change of vertical scale between inset and parent is not annotated.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — Panel (e) is the clearest case: the sidelobe train is over-dense and fills to a solid region, destroying the information the panel is meant to convey (that the Fejér skirts *oscillate* and decay as $f^{-2}$). Panel (f) is borderline for the same reason in the range $f\Delta < 0.25$. Panels (a) and (b) also show heavy trace overlap in the high-frequency noise region, where the single black trace saturates and the true PSD (blue dashed) is hard to follow underneath.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No inferential annotations, p-values, or pairwise significance markers appear in this figure.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — The abscissa is normalized frequency $f\Delta$ on $[0, 0.5]$, sampled uniformly to the Nyquist limit. No gaps, no interpolation across missing points.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Inset x-ranges are inconsistent across (e)–(h) ($0.39$–$0.41$ for e, f, g vs. $0.36$–$0.44$ for h) while the insets are visually formatted identically. This systematically understates the main-lobe broadening in (h), which is the central claim of the panel. Any cross-panel width comparison made by eye is invalid as drawn.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (e) renders the Fejér sidelobes as a solid fill. The structure the panel exists to demonstrate is not legible, and (e) is not visually commensurate with (f)–(h) even though the caption invites direct comparison ("The skirts of the Fejér kernel fall slowly").
- [ADVISORY] Apparent clipping of the estimate traces at the $-40$ dB floor in (a)–(b) and of the kernel nulls at the $-150$ dB floor in (f). Clipped data at an axis boundary is not distinguishable from data that stops there.
- [ADVISORY] $n = 1$ realization with no indication of sampling variability. The reader cannot distinguish features of *this* draw from properties of the *estimator*, which is what the caption claims to illustrate. The relevant effective sample size ($\nu$) is prose-only.
- [ADVISORY] $N=1024$, the taper fraction (25%), the smoother half-widths $R$, and $\nu$ for each column appear only in the caption. The figure is not self-contained if reproduced or viewed in a slide.
- [ADVISORY] Inset vertical scale differs from the parent axis without annotation; no scale-break or bracket marks the change.
- [ADVISORY] Legend appears only in panel (a); the blue dashed "True PSD" recurs in (b)–(d) unlabeled, and the green kernel trace in (e)–(h) is never keyed.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Use a single common inset x-range for (e)–(h). If the (h) main lobe will not fit in $0.39$–$0.41$, keep that window for all four and let (h) run off the inset edges, or add a second, clearly-labeled wider inset for (h) only — with an explicit note that its scale differs.
- Re-render (e) and the low-frequency part of (f) so the sidelobe oscillation is resolvable: increase the frequency sampling and line-width ratio, plot with partial transparency, or (preferred for kernels spanning many oscillations) overlay the sidelobe **envelope** as a solid line with the oscillating kernel behind it at low alpha. State in the caption which is which.
- Extend the (a)–(d) y-axis to accommodate all plotted values, or state explicitly in the caption that traces are clipped at $-40$ dB. Same for the $-150$ dB floor in (e)–(h): either extend or annotate the floor as a display limit.
- Add a $\chi^2_\nu$ confidence band (e.g., 95%) around the true PSD in (a)–(d), or inset a light ensemble of 20–50 additional realizations at low alpha behind the single highlighted trace. This converts "here is one draw" into "here is the estimator's sampling behavior," which is what the caption asserts.
- Annotate $N=1024$ and the per-panel $\nu$ and $R$ directly in each panel (small text, upper right). Keep the caption as the fuller explanation.
- Consider a fifth row or a difference panel showing $\hat S(f) - S(f)$ for the common realization, which makes the paired structure of (a)–(d) explicit and separates bias from variance visually.
- Reduce the alpha or line-width of the black estimate in (a)–(b) so the blue dashed true PSD remains visible where the two overlap; alternatively plot the true PSD on top with a light halo/casing.
- Repeat the legend in one lower panel (or add a shared figure-level legend) so the green kernel trace and the blue dashed reference are keyed for all panels.

### figure3.png

---

**Scope note:** This is a simulation/methods figure (a single realization trace plus Monte-Carlo-averaged error curves), not a group-comparison figure. Several of the standard checks (bar-vs-distribution, pairwise annotation, paired-group plotting) are therefore only weakly applicable; the substantive issues concern visibility of Monte-Carlo variability, overplotting in (a), and uneven sampling of the abscissa in (b).

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Panel (a): line plot of a uniformly sampled record is the correct choice; no bar/pie misuse anywhere. Panel (b): line-with-marker plots of a summary statistic against a continuous tuning parameter are appropriate. However, panel (b) plots only the *centre* of the sampling distribution (an ensemble mean) with no depiction of spread; a light shaded band (±1 Monte-Carlo SE, or the 10–90th percentile across the 300 realizations) would make it a distribution-aware plot rather than a point-summary plot.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — n = 300 realizations appears only in the caption, not in the figure or legend. Panel (a) does not state N = 1024, though it is inferable from the *k* axis (0–1023). Individual realizations are not shown and need not be at n = 300, but the reader cannot see the number of Monte-Carlo replicates from the graphic alone.

COMPARISON:         Concern — The "whole band" and "peak region" curves are, by construction, computed from the *same* 300 realizations at the *same* R values, i.e. they are paired. They are drawn as two independent curves, so the reader cannot see the realization-to-realization consistency of the key claim (that the peak-region optimum sits at smaller R). A difference curve, or a band on each, would make the paired structure visible. Separately, "Noise" and "Bias" are plotted alongside "Total" without indicating that Total is their quadrature sum; the visual invitation to check Total ≈ √(noise² + bias²) should be made explicit in the caption or legend.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    None — Panel (b) y-axis begins at 0 dB and the x-axis at RNΔ = 0; no exaggeration of the depth or location of the minimum. Panel (a) y-axis is not truncated (the taper envelope at ≈ ±70 is fully contained), although it is labelled only at ±50, so the reader must extrapolate the taper's amplitude. Adding ticks/labels near ±70 would help, since the caption states the taper is scaled to the record's peak amplitude.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Panel (a) superimposes two 1024-sample oscillatory traces (raw = black, tapered = red) at ~1 px per sample. Over the central region the red trace almost entirely occludes the black, so the very thing the panel is meant to show — that the taper leaves the interior essentially unchanged and attenuates only the ends — is visible only at the two edges. Line-density aliasing is also likely at this pixel pitch. Panel (b): mild marker occlusion where the solid and dashed curves cross near RNΔ ≈ 7–9, compounded by the large open annotation circle sitting over the filled marker.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No pairwise significance annotations, p-values, or inferential brackets are present; nothing requires correction.

TIME SERIES:        Concern (panel b abscissa), OK (panel a) — Panel (a) is a complete, evenly sampled record with no gaps or interpolation. Panel (b), however, is sampled unevenly in RNΔ (markers imply roughly 0,1,2,3,4,6,8,10,12,16,20,24,28,32,40,48,56,64), and straight segments interpolate across gaps that widen from 1 to 8 units. The markers do disclose the sampling — good practice — but the "Noise" and "Bias" curves are drawn as smooth marker-free lines, which implies they were evaluated analytically or on a much finer grid. If they were in fact computed on the same coarse grid, the smooth rendering misrepresents the resolution; if they were computed analytically, this should be stated. The flat plateau of the dashed peak-region curve beyond RNΔ ≈ 40 rests on only ~4 sampled points.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) shows ensemble means with no indication of Monte-Carlo variability. The figure's purpose is to justify a choice of R, and the caption makes a quantitative claim about where the minimum sits; the reader cannot judge whether the broad minimum's location (or the offset between the whole-band and peak-region optima) is resolved by 300 realizations. This escalates to BLOCKING if the optimal R is reported as a numerical result elsewhere in the paper.
  - [ADVISORY] Overplotting in panel (a): the red tapered trace occludes the black raw trace throughout the record interior, undermining the panel's intended message.
  - [ADVISORY] n (300 realizations) and N (1024 samples) are not shown in the figure; the figure is not self-contained.
  - [ADVISORY] Uneven, undisclosed-in-caption sampling of RNΔ with linear interpolation across gaps that grow eightfold; inconsistent rendering (markers on black curves, none on blue/red) implies differing evaluation grids.
  - [ADVISORY] Paired structure of the two "Total" curves (same realizations, same R grid) is not visualized.
  - [ADVISORY] Annotation circles in panel (b) obscure the data markers they identify, and the circle near RNΔ ≈ 48 sits ambiguously between the solid black and red curves.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a semi-transparent band around each curve in panel (b) giving ±1 Monte-Carlo standard error (spread/√300) or the interquartile range across realizations; alternatively overlay ~30 thin individual-realization traces at low alpha behind the mean. Remove the caption phrase "carry no error bars" once variability is shown, or, if bands are deliberately omitted, state the Monte-Carlo SE numerically in the caption.
  - Add an inset or a companion difference curve (peak-region minus whole-band RMS error, computed per realization) with its own uncertainty band to support the claim that the optimum shifts to smaller R.
  - Panel (a): either split into two stacked sub-panels (raw above, tapered below, shared axes) or plot the black raw trace with 40–50 % alpha and the red tapered trace on top at full opacity; alternatively plot only the raw trace plus the taper envelope and show the tapered record separately, since the interior is visually identical by construction.
  - Annotate n directly: "300 realizations" in the panel (b) legend or as in-axes text, and "N = 1024" in panel (a).
  - Mark the actually evaluated R values on the blue and red curves with faint markers (or state in the caption that they were evaluated analytically / on a fine grid), and note the R grid in the caption. Consider plotting panel (b) against log RNΔ so the dense low-R sampling and sparse high-R sampling are not visually conflated.
  - Redraw the annotation circles as open rings offset from, or larger than, the markers, with leader lines and labels "(b)", "(c)", "(d)" so each circled point is unambiguously assigned to a curve.
  - State in the caption that Total is the quadrature sum of the Noise and Bias curves, so the reader can verify the decomposition visually.
  - Add y-axis ticks near ±70 in panel (a) so the taper's peak amplitude can be read off.
---

### figure4.png

---
**Context note:** This is a deterministic/analytical figure (Slepian tapers, spectral kernels, eigenvalue ladder). There are no sampled observations, replicates, or group comparisons, so most distribution-integrity criteria are inapplicable. The review below focuses on the criteria that do transfer to deterministic curves: axis handling, curve occlusion/overplotting, and self-documentation of parameters.

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate — Line plots for continuous functions (a–c) and a marker-and-line plot for a discrete index sequence (d) are the correct choices. No bar charts, no pie charts, no error bars, so no distribution-hiding risk. One caveat: in (d) the quantity is indexed by integer *i*, and connecting the markers with a solid line implies interpolation between integers; markers alone (or markers + faint guide line, stated as a guide) would be more honest.
SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — The governing parameters (N = 256, R = 8/(NΔ), α = 4) exist only in the caption. Nothing in the panels themselves states N, α, or the number of tapers summed in (c) beyond the legend labels. For deterministic figures this is advisory rather than blocking, but the figure should be self-contained: annotate N and α in-panel (e.g., a text box "N = 256, 2α = 8"). In (d), all 17 plotted values are individually marked — good practice, equivalent to showing individual points.
COMPARISON:         Concern (labeling, not statistics) — Two mismatches between axis labels and caption formulas that a reader cannot reconcile from the figure alone:
  • (b) axis reads |H^(i)(f)|²/NΔ², caption says |H^(i)(f)|²/Δ.
  • (c) axis reads 𝒦(f)/NΔ, caption says Σ λ_i|H^(i)|²/2αΔ.
  Also, (b) and (c) are implicitly meant to be compared (the running sum in (c) is built from the kernels in (b)), but their y-axes use different normalizations and different ranges (0–0.65 vs 0–0.20), which defeats the visual comparison the caption invites. The i = 1 kernel peaks at ≈0.49 in (b) while the L = 1 curve in (c) peaks at ≈0.061 — the factor is unexplained in-panel.
AXIS TRUNCATION:    Present — Three instances, none appear deliberately misleading, but two warrant attention:
  • (a) y-axis is hard-clipped at ±0.20 and the lower-left legend box sits inside the data region, occluding the area where h^(7) is largest in magnitude; a reader cannot verify that no curve excursion is hidden behind the legend or clipped at the axis.
  • (c) y-axis ends at exactly 0.200 while the plotted maxima are ≈0.125; the top 40% of the panel is empty, compressing the ripple structure that the caption explicitly asks the reader to see ("rounded shoulders and small ripple"). Not exaggerating differences, but it *understates* the feature being claimed.
  • (d) log y-axis spans 10⁻¹⁶ to ~10⁰ while the data span only ~3×10⁻¹⁰ to ~10⁰. Six empty decades. Additionally, the smallest plotted value (~3×10⁻¹⁰) for 1 − λ₁ is within a few orders of magnitude of double-precision limits for eigenvalues computed near unity; the reader should be told how λ_i was computed and whether values that small are numerically trustworthy.
OVERPLOTTING:       Present — In (c), the L = 9 (magenta) and L = N (black) curves are visually coincident over most of the band; magenta is essentially invisible except for a sliver near f NΔ ≈ −5 and at the left shoulder. Likewise the "Box" dashed reference is stated in the caption to be indistinguishable from L = N, and indeed the black curve overwrites it. Drawing order determines what a reader sees, which is a reproducibility hazard: the central claim ("cannot be distinguished from the box itself at this scale") is asserted by occlusion rather than demonstrated.
MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No inferential annotations, p-values, or significance brackets anywhere in the figure.
TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — (a) is an index-domain waveform and (d) is an ordered discrete index; neither is longitudinal data with timepoints. See the interpolation note under PLOT TYPE for (d).
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Axis labels in (b) and (c) contradict the normalizations written in the caption (|H|²/NΔ² vs |H|²/Δ; 𝒦(f)/NΔ vs Σλ_i|H^(i)|²/2αΔ). One of the two is wrong and the quantitative claim in (c) (that the sum equals the box convolved with Fejér's kernel, i.e. the stated identity) cannot be checked until this is resolved.
  - [BLOCKING] The key claim of (c) — that the L = N sum is indistinguishable from the box — is supported only by the black curve being drawn on top of the dashed box. As plotted, the claim is unfalsifiable by the reader.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend in (a) is placed inside the plotting area at the y-axis limit, obscuring data and preventing verification that no curve is clipped.
  - [ADVISORY] L = 9 (magenta) is effectively hidden beneath L = N (black) in (c); the legend promises a curve the reader cannot see.
  - [ADVISORY] (c) wastes the upper 40% of its y-range, compressing the ripple/shoulder structure the caption highlights.
  - [ADVISORY] (d) wastes ~6 empty decades of log range; no statement of numerical method or precision floor for 1 − λ_i values of order 10⁻¹⁰.
  - [ADVISORY] Solid connecting line in (d) implies continuous interpolation across integer taper indices.
  - [ADVISORY] Parameters N, α, R appear only in the caption, not in the panels.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Reconcile the (b) and (c) y-axis labels with the caption; use one consistent normalization so (b) and (c) can be read against each other, ideally with the same y-limits or an explicit note on the scaling factor between them.
  - Replace the occlusion argument in (c) with a residual inset: plot L = N minus the box (or minus box⊗Fejér) on its own small axis with a fine y-scale. This demonstrates the identity quantitatively instead of hiding it under a thick black line. Quote the maximum absolute residual in the caption.
  - In (c), draw the dashed box reference *on top* of all curves, or draw L = N as a thick semi-transparent band with the box as a thin solid line inside it; increase the L = 9 line width or use a dash pattern so it remains discernible where it nearly coincides with L = N.
  - Set (c) y-limit to ~0.13–0.14 so the shoulder rounding and ripple occupy the full panel height; optionally add a zoomed inset on the shoulder region near |f NΔ| ≈ 4.
  - Move the (a) legend outside the axes (or to an empty region) and either widen the y-limits to ±0.14 to fit the curves with margin, or state explicitly that no clipping occurs.
  - In (d), plot markers only (or markers with a light dotted guide) and state in the caption that the connecting line is a visual guide, not interpolation. Trim the y-axis to ~10⁻¹¹–10⁰ and add a note on the eigenvalue solver and its precision floor; if values below ~10⁻¹² are not trustworthy, indicate the floor with a shaded band.
  - Annotate N = 256, 2α = 8, and R = 8/(NΔ) directly in one panel (or in all four) so each panel is interpretable without the caption.
  - Clarify in the caption of (b) whether the plotted kernels are weighted by λ_i, since (c)'s running sum is stated to include λ_i weights while (b) appears not to.
---

### figure5.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a deterministic/analytical figure, not sampled data. Panels (a–d) are line plots of window/taper sequences (densely sampled over k = 0…256), which is the correct representation. Panels (e–h) are bar charts of a discrete, exactly-computed eigen-weight ladder cᵢ indexed by integer i; bars are appropriate here because the quantity is a discrete spectrum of exact values, not a sample mean. No error bars are present and none are expected — critically, no error bars are implied either, so the usual "bar chart hides the distribution" objection does not apply. Minor: for eigenvalue/weight ladders, a stem/lollipop plot is often preferred to bars because it avoids implying an integrable area over i; consider this as a stylistic upgrade only.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible (low severity) — There is no statistical n. However, the figure's analogues of n are *not* readable from the graphic: N = 256, R = 8/(NΔ), L = 7, and especially the equivalent degrees of freedom ν = 13.4, 16.7, 17.2, 14.0 exist only in the caption. Since ν is the headline quantity that (e–h) are meant to convey, it should be annotated in-panel. Likewise the number of contributing tapers/segments (7, ~12, ~12, 7) must currently be counted by eye.

**COMPARISON:** Valid, with one concern — The four methods are compared on shared, identical axes in both rows (x: 0–256 in the top row; x: 0–~13, y: 0–0.20 in the bottom row), which is exactly right and makes the flatness comparison honest. Concern: panels (a) and (c) carry no legend while (b) and (d) do, and the color assignments are not consistent across panels (e.g. green/red/cyan/blue in (c) vs. the fʹNΔ legend in (b) and the i-index legend in (d)). A reader can easily mis-map curve identity between (b), (c) and (d). Also, the column-wise pairing (a)↔(e), (b)↔(f), etc. is only stated verbally in the caption; nothing in the figure marks the correspondence.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** None — All four bar panels (e–h) start at y = 0 and share an identical 0–0.20 range; no exaggeration of the differences in ladder flatness. Line panels (a–d) use ranges appropriate to their data, and (b)/(d) legitimately include negative values with a visible zero crossing. No misleading truncation anywhere.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present (mild, panel c) — Panel (c) plots four sinc-window curves whose side-lobes all oscillate in a narrow band around zero; below roughly |0.1| the four traces are visually inseparable, and the green and cyan curves are additionally clipped by the panel edges at k = 0 and k = 256 so their peaks are ambiguous. Panel (a)'s seven overlapping Hann lobes also crowd at the 50%-overlap crossing points. No point-density issue (no scatter plots).

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No inferential annotations, no p-values, no significance brackets. Nothing to correct.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — k is a sample index over a dense, complete, evenly spaced grid (0–256 with no gaps), so continuous interpolation between samples is legitimate and not misleading. The bottom row is indexed by integer i and correctly uses discrete bars rather than a connected line.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] The quantity the bottom row is designed to communicate (ν = 13.4, 16.7, 17.2, 14.0) is caption-only and cannot be recovered from the graphic; the figure therefore does not stand alone.
- [ADVISORY] Panels (a) and (c) lack legends; curve identity (which time shift, which sinc position) is unrecoverable from the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Color–meaning mapping is inconsistent between (b), (c) and (d), inviting cross-panel misreading.
- [ADVISORY] In panel (c), the four traces overlap indistinguishably in the side-lobe region, and the green/cyan curves are clipped at the k-axis limits.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-row x-axes extend to ~13 while (e) and (h) contain only 7 bars, leaving a large blank region that could be misread as suppressed/zero-valued weights rather than nonexistent indices.
- [ADVISORY] Column correspondence (a→e, b→f, c→g, d→h) is not indicated graphically.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-row x-ticks appear only at i = 5 and i = 10, so individual bar indices must be counted by hand.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Annotate ν (and L or the effective taper count) directly inside each of (e–h), e.g. "ν = 13.4"; optionally also print N = 256 and R = 8/(NΔ) once in the figure.
- Add legends to (a) and (c), or label a representative curve inline (e.g. "segment 1…7", "n = 0, 64, 128, 192").
- Harmonize the color palette so a given index/shift keeps one color across (a–d), or deliberately use distinct palettes per panel and say so in the caption.
- In (c), use transparency or a modest vertical offset/inset zoom on the side-lobe region, and extend the x-range slightly (e.g. −8 to 264) so the green and cyan peaks are not clipped.
- Set each bottom-row x-limit to the actual index range for that method, or grey/hatch the unused region; alternatively keep shared limits but add a short note that absent bars mean "no such taper."
- Add integer minor ticks (or tick every 2) on the bottom-row x-axes so bar indices are directly readable.
- Consider stem/lollipop markers instead of filled bars in (e–h) to emphasize that cᵢ is a discrete eigen-weight, not an area-bearing quantity.
- Optional: overlay a light horizontal reference line at 1/L (the perfectly flat ladder) in each of (e–h) to make the flatness comparison quantitative at a glance.
---

### figure6.png

---
PLOT TYPE:          Appropriate (with one caveat) — Line plots of estimated vs. true PSD (a–d) and kernel magnitude responses (e–h) are the correct display for continuous frequency-domain functions; no bar/pie misuse. Caveat: the *comparative claim* of the figure (which estimator is better) is a distributional claim over realizations, yet it is displayed as four single-realization traces. A companion panel showing the ensemble (median ± 5th/95th percentile envelope over the 300 realizations, or a dot/beeswarm plot of the 300 median-absolute-error values per estimator) would be the distribution-honest display.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Not visible — N=1024, one realization, L=7 tapers, 9 Welch segments, ν values and the 300-realization ensemble all appear only in the caption/table, not in or on the figure. Nothing in the panels identifies n or the number of realizations. For the ensemble comparison (n=300 realizations, effectively n=1 shown) the individual realizations are not shown at all.

COMPARISON:         Concern — (i) The four estimators are applied to the *same* record (paired/matched design, correctly so), but they are drawn in four separate axes with four different colors, so the paired nature is not exploited visually; a single overlay panel (or difference-from-true-PSD panel, estimate − true in dB) would make the paired comparison directly readable. (ii) More seriously, the caption concedes "This realization has more leakage than is typical," and the quoted numbers confirm the displayed draw penalizes exactly the two methods the figure argues against (a: 11.6 dB shown vs. 5.3 dB typical; b: 4.6 vs. 2.3) while the two favored methods are unaffected (0.9→1.0, 0.8→1.0). The chosen realization therefore visually exaggerates the performance gap by roughly a factor of two for (a) and (b). Disclosure in text does not repair the visual impression.

AXIS TRUNCATION:    Present — Not of the misleading bar-chart kind, but three items need attention: (1) Top row is clipped at −40 dB while the true PSD asymptote sits near −21 dB; fine, but the (a) and (b) estimates are cut at the bottom of nothing — no data loss apparent. (2) Bottom row is clipped at −150 dB, which truncates the nulls of the Welch kernel in (h) and (arguably) the low-frequency floor in (g); the reader cannot see how deep the nulls go, and (h) reads as a dense picket fence rather than as resolvable lobes. (3) Insets are clipped at −50 dB top-limited at ~+5 dB; confirm the main-lobe peak (0 dB) is not clipped in any inset. All four panels within a row share identical limits — good, and this should be stated in the caption.

OVERPLOTTING:       Present — Severe in (f) and (h), and moderate in (b). In (f) the multitaper kernel's rapid oscillation renders as a solid filled band between ≈−50 and −67 dB, so neither the lobe structure, the local density, nor the true envelope is legible; the same occurs in (h) below ≈−100 dB. In (b) the periodogram's high-frequency ripple saturates into a solid block above 0.25. At 2090 px width with ~513 frequency points per panel the line width, not the sampling, is the limiting factor.

MULTIPLE COMPARISONS: No concern — No pairwise significance annotations are present. Note only that the six implicit pairwise contrasts among four estimators are reported as point summaries (median absolute error) without any interval or correction; if any claim of "significantly better" is made in the text, the 300-realization distributions should support it.

TIME SERIES:        Not applicable — Frequency-domain display; frequency axis is uniform 0–0.5 with no gaps. (If the 300-realization ensemble were shown as a function of frequency, missing/edge bins should be indicated.)

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] The primary comparative evidence is a single, explicitly non-representative realization that inflates the error of estimators (a) and (b) by ~2× relative to their 300-realization medians, while leaving (c) and (d) unchanged. As drawn, the figure overstates the advantage of the favored methods. Text disclosure is insufficient; the visual must carry the ensemble information.
  - [ADVISORY] No sample-size or parameter information is inferable from the figure itself (N, number of realizations, L, ν, number of Welch segments all live in the caption only).
  - [ADVISORY] Overplotting in (f) and (h) (and partially in (b)) collapses oscillatory structure into solid ink, making lobe spacing, side-lobe level, and null depth unreadable — the exact quantities the caption asserts (−22 dB skirt, "side lobes spaced at the scale of the peaks").
  - [ADVISORY] Bottom-row clipping at −150 dB truncates the Welch kernel nulls in (h); readers cannot judge whether the dense region is a floor or an artifact of line rendering.
  - [ADVISORY] Paired design (same record through four estimators) is displayed unpaired across four axes with four arbitrary colors; the legend appears only in (a), so panels (b)–(d) rely on the reader inferring that black/red/magenta all mean "Estimate."
  - [ADVISORY] Quantitative claims (median absolute errors 11.6/4.6/0.9/0.8 dB and 5.3/2.3/1.0/1.0 dB; leakage fractions 1.4/0.22/0.12/0.48%) are text-only point estimates with no dispersion shown.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a fifth column or a second figure showing, for each estimator, the ensemble over the 300 realizations: median estimate with a shaded 5th–95th percentile band against the true PSD. Retain the single realization only if it is labeled in-panel as "one realization (atypically high leakage)."
  - Add a beeswarm/dot plot (or box plot with all 300 points overlaid, jittered, alpha ≈ 0.3) of median-absolute-error per realization for the four estimators, with the value of the displayed realization highlighted. This replaces the Table-only summary with a distribution-visible comparison and makes the four-way contrast paired (connect the four values for each realization with faint lines, since all estimators share each record).
  - Annotate n and key parameters inside each panel: "N=1024, 1 realization, ν=17.2" etc., plus "n=300 realizations" on the ensemble panel.
  - For (f) and (h), plot the kernel envelope (upper envelope of |K(f)| via local maxima) as a solid line with the raw oscillation in light gray at alpha ≈ 0.25, or add a linear-frequency zoom inset over a narrow band (e.g. 0.30–0.35) where individual lobes are resolvable. Reduce line width to ≤0.5 pt.
  - Extend the bottom-row lower limit (e.g. to −180 dB) or state explicitly in the caption that values below −150 dB are clipped; do the same for any inset clipping.
  - Add a difference panel (estimate − true PSD, in dB, all four estimators overlaid with the legend) to make the paired comparison and the 20 dB / 10 dB floors quantitative rather than read off two curves by eye.
  - Repeat the "Estimate / True PSD" legend (or at minimum a color key) in every panel, and state in the caption that all panels in a row share identical axis limits.
---

### figure7.png

---

**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — Overlaid line plots of spectral density estimates versus frequency are the correct display for this data type; none of the bar-chart/pie-chart concerns apply. Panel (b) (difference-from-reference) is a good complement to (a), analogous to a Bland–Altman-style residual panel, and is preferable to asking readers to judge agreement from superimposed curves alone. One structural gap: the figure asserts agreement "within their own noise," but no uncertainty band is drawn on either panel, so the central claim is not visually testable.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible — This is a single EEG epoch (n = 1 record), which is stated in the caption but not inferable from the figure. The effective degrees of freedom (ν = 14, 16.7, 17.6), which are the relevant "sample size" quantities for a spectral estimate, appear only in the caption; the legend gives taper/segment counts but not ν. Individual-point display is not applicable here, but readers cannot see how much smoothing/averaging underlies each curve.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — The comparison is intrinsically paired (three estimators applied to the *same* epoch, evaluated at the same frequency bins), and panel (b) correctly exploits that pairing. However, (i) the differences are taken against the multitaper estimate as though it were a gold standard, which privileges one arbitrary reference; a symmetric display (each estimator minus the three-estimator mean, or pairwise differences including recipe-b′ minus Welch) would be more even-handed and is easy to add. (ii) The quantitative agreement statistics that carry the argument (median absolute difference 0.6/0.9 dB, 95th percentiles 4.2/5.9 dB, noise level ≈1.5–1.6 dB) exist only in prose; none is drawn on panel (b). (iii) The three estimators have different half-power widths (0.91, 1.03, 0.97 Hz), so part of the plotted difference is a deterministic resolution mismatch rather than estimator noise — this confound is not visually separable.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** None — Panel (a) spans roughly 7–47 dB; because dB is already a log scale there is no meaningful zero to anchor to, and the range is set by the data, so this is not misleading. Panel (b) is symmetric about zero (±15 dB) with an explicit zero reference line and comfortably contains the data (−5 to +12.5 dB), which if anything understates the differences rather than exaggerating them. Good practice on both panels.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — Not point-density overplotting, but serious line occlusion. Three curves of equal weight are drawn on nearly coincident paths; draw order (black, then red, then magenta) means the magenta Welch curve systematically hides red where they agree, and both hide black. Where the curves agree best — the paper's actual claim — the reader sees only the last-drawn curve and cannot confirm agreement. The same problem recurs in panel (b), where red is largely buried under magenta at the peaks. Additionally, the fine-scale black multitaper structure in the harmonic valleys (the feature the caption specifically discusses) is exactly where occlusion is worst.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** Flag for verification — No formal pairwise tests are annotated, so no correction is required in the conventional sense. However, panel (b) is effectively a pointwise comparison across several hundred frequency bins, and the caption quotes a 95th percentile of the difference distribution. With that many bins, excursions of 4–6 dB are expected under the null of "agreement within noise" and should not be read as localized disagreement; conversely, adjacent bins are correlated by the smoothing, so the bins are far from independent and the effective number of comparisons is much smaller than the bin count. Authors should confirm that the quoted percentiles are interpreted with this correlation in mind and state the number of frequency bins summarized.

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — Data are in the frequency domain. The frequency axis appears uniformly sampled and continuous from 0 to 40 Hz with no gaps, so there is no misleading interpolation. (If the underlying epoch itself contained rejected or missing samples, that should be stated, as it would affect all three estimators.)

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The figure's central claim — that the estimators agree to within their own noise — cannot be verified from the figure, because no noise band is plotted. The ≈1.5–1.6 dB per-bin standard error exists only in the caption text.
- [BLOCKING] Line occlusion from equal-weight, opaque, near-coincident curves. Agreement regions show only the top-drawn curve; the black multitaper curve is substantially hidden in both panels, including in the harmonic valleys that the caption singles out for discussion.
- [ADVISORY] Differences are referenced to the multitaper estimate as an implicit gold standard; no justification is given, and the Welch-versus-recipe-b′ difference is never shown.
- [ADVISORY] Half-power widths differ across estimators (0.91–1.03 Hz), so plotted differences conflate resolution mismatch with estimator variance; this is not visually distinguishable.
- [ADVISORY] Effective degrees of freedom ν, the quantity that determines each curve's noise, appear only in the caption and not in the legend.
- [ADVISORY] Reliance on a red/magenta pair that is difficult to separate for readers with red–green or general colour-vision deficiency, compounded by the occlusion problem.
- [ADVISORY] Caption summary statistics (medians, 95th percentiles) have no graphical counterpart; the reader must take them on trust.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) grid lines are dotted and fairly prominent, adding visual texture that competes with the fine spectral structure being compared.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a shaded ±1.6 dB (or ±2 SE) noise band about zero in panel (b), labelled in the legend. This converts the paper's key claim from a caption assertion into something the reader can check at a glance, and immediately shows which excursions exceed noise.
- Fix draw order and line weight: plot the multitaper reference last, or draw it as a thick light-grey/black underlay with the two comparison curves as thinner lines on top. Alternatively apply ~50–60% opacity to the overlying curves so coincidence is visible as colour blending rather than as disappearance.
- Consider splitting panel (a) into two stacked sub-panels (multitaper vs. recipe b′; multitaper vs. Welch), or adding a magnified inset over one harmonic peak-and-valley pair (e.g. 8–10 Hz), where the differences are largest and the occlusion most damaging.
- In panel (b), offset the two difference traces vertically by a stated constant, or plot them in separate stacked sub-panels, so the red trace is not buried under magenta at the peaks.
- Add horizontal dashed lines (or annotated markers) at the quoted median absolute difference and 95th percentile values so the caption statistics are readable off the figure.
- Add the third pairwise difference (recipe b′ minus Welch) or re-reference all differences to the three-estimator mean, to avoid privileging the multitaper estimate.
- Report the number of frequency bins and the approximate number of independent bins (bandwidth ÷ half-power width) in the caption, so readers can calibrate how many large excursions to expect by chance.
- Replace the red/magenta pair with a colour-blind-safe triple (e.g. black, orange, blue) and/or differentiate by line style (solid/dashed/dotted) so the curves remain separable in greyscale and for all readers.
- Move ν into the legend entries (e.g. "Multitaper, L = 7, ν = 14") so each curve's noise level travels with the curve.
- Lighten or thin the grid lines in panel (a) so they do not compete with the spectral detail.

### figure8.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — Time-series traces, spectrograms (image plots), overlaid PSD curves, and a difference histogram are all correct choices for this data type. No bar graphs, no pie charts, no error-bar-hides-distribution problem. One substantive caveat: panels (d) and (f) show point estimates of four spectral estimators with **no uncertainty representation**. Since the whole point of the comparison is bias/variance trade-off, these panels have the same defect as a bar chart without data points — the reader cannot see the variability that ν quantifies. Add jackknife/χ²-based confidence bands (or a single representative ν-derived error bar per estimator) so the visual differences between estimators can be judged against their own scatter.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible — This is effectively an n = 1 demonstration (one subject, one channel, one seizure, one 10-minute record), and nothing in the figure itself conveys that. Panel (h) is stated to be "all pixels" but the total pixel count (599 windows × number of frequency bins) is not given on the figure, nor is the bin width. Add "n = 1 record, channel C4" in the panel (a) corner and "N = X pixels" plus bin width in (h). If the paper draws any general conclusion about estimator performance, a companion figure over multiple records/channels is needed; a single hand-picked channel ("the one with the largest ictal rise") is a selected extreme, which should be stated on the figure, not only buried in the caption.

**COMPARISON:** Valid, with one concern — Panel (h) is the correct paired visualization: the pixelwise difference between (e) and (g) on identical windows, rather than two unpaired marginal distributions. Good. Panels (d)/(f) likewise overlay estimators computed from the same data segment, which is appropriately paired. Concern: (d) and (f) use **different y-ranges** (≈ −5 to 45 dB vs 20 to 85 dB), so the pre-ictal → ictal change cannot be read across panels; and the two marker instants are colour-coded (blue/red) in (a) but rendered **both white** in (c, e, g), so the reader cannot map lines to panels.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present — Three instances, two of them potentially misleading:
1. Panel (a) is "displayed between ±500 µV" and the ictal burst visibly rides the ceiling; the trace appears **clipped**, understating ictal amplitude and making the seizure look amplitude-limited. State explicitly that the display is clipped (and give the true peak amplitude) or use a broken/compressed amplitude axis.
2. The shared colour scale is fixed at 10–45 dB and **saturates** over the whole <3 Hz band and throughout the seizure in all three spectrograms. Saturation is the 2-D equivalent of a truncated axis: the very regions the figure is about are rendered as uniform dark red, so the three estimators cannot be compared where it matters most. Extend the upper limit, or add a second row with a clipped-residual/difference map.
3. Panel (h) x-axis appears to terminate at ≈ ±8 dB; if the difference distribution has longer tails, they are being cut. Either extend the axis or annotate the fraction of pixels outside the plotted range.
4. Minor: panel (b) is missing its upper y-tick label, so the positive excursions cannot be read.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — (i) Panels (a) and (b) render ~120,000 and ~1,200 samples into a limited pixel width; the dense trace is a rendering-aliased envelope, not resolvable waveform, which can create spurious apparent amplitude structure. Use explicit min–max envelope decimation (and say so) or thin the linewidth. (ii) In (d) and (f) the multitaper (black), Recipe b′ (red) and Welch (magenta) curves lie nearly on top of one another; line ordering determines which is visible, so the comparison is decided by draw order. Use partial transparency, slightly offset line widths, or add a companion "difference from multitaper" sub-panel. (iii) The legend appears only in (f) — add it to (d) or state that it applies to both.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No pairwise significance annotations or p-values are present; nothing requires correction. (If the text makes any claim that the (e)−(g) distribution is "centred on zero," report a formal summary — median, IQR, and the fraction of |difference| > 1 dB — rather than leaving it to visual inspection.)

**TIME SERIES:** Concern —
- There is an unexplained near-flat segment in (a) at roughly 365–400 s, mirrored by an abrupt dark-blue block in (c, e, g) and a sharp vertical discontinuity near 400–410 s. This looks like a data dropout, amplifier saturation/recovery, or a rejected/interpolated segment, and it is not marked or mentioned. If any data are missing or interpolated, **the gap must be shown as a gap** (white mask, hatching, or axis break) rather than rendered as valid low-power signal; otherwise the spectrograms imply genuine post-ictal suppression where there may be no data.
- The spectrogram x-axes are offset from panel (a) (window-centre convention means they begin ~1 s in and have no 0 tick), so the shaded seizure interval in (a) does not visually align with the corresponding column in (c, e, g). Force identical x-limits and ticks across (a), (c), (e), (g).
- Window length (2 s) and step (1 s) imply heavy overlap and therefore temporally smoothed, non-independent columns; note the effective time resolution on the figure.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Colour scale saturates (10–45 dB) across the low-frequency band and the entire ictal period in all three spectrograms, making the estimators indistinguishable precisely where the paper's claim lies.
