- [BLOCKING] Unmarked flat/discontinuous segment at ≈365–410 s in (a, c, e, g); if these are missing, clipped, or interpolated data they must be masked and labelled, not displayed as valid low-power signal.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (a) trace is amplitude-clipped at ±500 µV without annotation, understating ictal amplitude.
- [ADVISORY] Panels (d, f) present spectral estimates with no confidence bands, so estimator differences cannot be assessed against estimator variance.
- [ADVISORY] n = 1 record/channel is not visible in the figure, and the channel was selected for maximal ictal power — a selection criterion that should appear on the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Marker instants colour-coded blue/red in (a) but both white in (c, e, g); legend present only in (f).
- [ADVISORY] Different y-ranges in (d) vs (f) prevent cross-panel reading of the pre-ictal → ictal change.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (h) lacks N, bin width, a zero reference line, and summary statistics; x-axis may truncate tails.
- [ADVISORY] Overplotted traces in (a, b) and near-coincident curves in (d, f); visibility determined by draw order.
- [ADVISORY] Jet colormap is perceptually non-uniform and introduces false contours/edges that can be mistaken for spectral structure; three identical colorbars are redundant and their unit reference ("dB re 1 µV²/Hz"?) is unstated.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) missing upper y-tick label; its time axis is relative (0–6 s) rather than absolute (318–324 s), breaking the link to (a).

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Raise or split the colour-scale limits (e.g. 10–60 dB), or add a dedicated difference map (e)−(g) with a diverging, zero-centred colormap; switch to viridis/magma for the power maps.
- Mask the 365–410 s interval explicitly (white/hatched block with a "dropout / artefact" label) in (a) and all three spectrograms, and state in the caption whether any samples were interpolated.
- Annotate panel (a) with the true peak amplitude and label the display as clipped, or add an inset at full scale.
- Add jackknife or χ²(ν) confidence bands to (d) and (f), or a single ν-based error bar per estimator; add a small residual sub-panel showing each estimator minus the multitaper reference.
- Harmonise y-limits between (d) and (f), or add an inset difference spectrum; place the legend in both panels.
- Recolour the two vertical markers consistently (blue = pre-ictal, red = ictal) in all panels, and label them "pre-ictal" / "ictal" directly.
- Force identical x-limits and tick positions for (a), (c), (e), (g); include a 0 s tick on the spectrograms and draw the shaded seizure interval on all four.
- In (h), report N pixels, bin width, median and IQR, draw a vertical line at 0, and either extend the x-axis to include the tails or annotate the percentage of pixels beyond the plotted range.
- Use explicit min–max envelope decimation for (a) and (b) and state the rendering method; give (b) an absolute time axis and a complete set of y-tick labels.
- Add "n = 1 subject, 1 channel (C4); channel chosen for maximal 2–20 Hz ictal rise" as on-figure text so the single-case, selected nature of the example is not caption-dependent.
- Collapse the three redundant colorbars into one shared bar with explicit units.

### figure9.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a methods-demonstration figure, not a group-comparison figure, so the bar-graph/error-bar concerns do not apply. (a) time-domain trace, (b–g) spectrograms and (h) a paired dot/dumbbell plot are all well-matched to the data. Two secondary concerns: (i) panels (b–g) use a rainbow ("jet"-like) colour map for a quantitative dB scale — this is a known perceptual-integrity issue (non-uniform lightness creates false gradient edges and can manufacture apparent structure such as the sharp red/yellow spindle boundaries, and it is not colour-vision-deficiency safe); a perceptually uniform map (viridis, cividis, magma) is preferable. (ii) The colour scale appears to saturate at both ends (large uniform dark-red region below ~3 Hz in all panels, uniform dark blue above ~20 Hz), so clipped values are indistinguishable from in-range extremes.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible — No n is shown or inferable. The caption implies a single 40-s epoch from one channel of presumably one subject, but this is never stated. Individual data points are shown in (h) (each setting is a single computed value, so this is complete), but the reader cannot tell whether (b–g) are representative of a cohort or a hand-picked illustrative epoch. State n = 1 subject / 1 epoch explicitly and say whether the epoch was selected as typical.

**COMPARISON:** Valid — Panel (h) is exemplary: multitaper and smoothing values at the same window setting are inherently paired (same data, same target resolution) and are correctly connected by a line within each row, preserving the pairing. Panels (b–g) use a single shared colour scale, which makes the left/right and top/bottom comparisons legitimate. Minor concern: the white resolution boxes are the only in-panel indication of resolution, and their area encodes ν only approximately ("twice its area is approximately ν") — area is a weak perceptual channel, so the quantitative claim should rest on (h), not on box comparison.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present (non-misleading, with one caveat) — Panel (h) x-axis begins near 0.4 Hz rather than 0, which is acceptable for a dot plot of a ratio-scale quantity where no bars invite length comparison; the numeric labels above every marker further protect against misreading. Caveat: because the axis is truncated, the *visual* gap between the 1.1 Hz and 2.1 Hz markers in the top row looks proportionally much larger than the true ~1.9× ratio; readers comparing the length of the top connector to the bottom connector (0.8→1.0 Hz) may over-read the difference. Panel (a) has no amplitude scale for the red 11–16 Hz trace (it is offset and presumably differently scaled), so its amplitude is uninterpretable.

**OVERPLOTTING:** None — Spectrogram density is intrinsic to the representation. Panel (h) has only six markers with no occlusion. Panel (a) black trace is dense but legible at the stated figure resolution.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No inferential tests or significance annotations are present; nothing to correct.

**TIME SERIES:** Concern — Two issues. (1) The windows are stepped by 0.1 s while being 1–4 s long (90–97.5% overlap), so adjacent spectrogram columns are highly correlated. The resulting temporal smoothness is an artefact of overlap, not independent evidence of sustained spindle activity; the horizontal "streaks" in (f,g) are partly window-induced. This should be stated so readers do not over-interpret temporal continuity. (2) All six spectrograms span the full 0–40 s, which is impossible for centred windows without edge padding or reflection (a 4-s window loses 2 s at each end). The edge-handling convention is undocumented, and padded edges are not visually distinguished from valid interior data. No missing timepoints or uneven intervals are otherwise implied.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] n is neither shown nor stated; single-epoch, single-channel provenance and whether the epoch was representative or selected are not documented.
- [ADVISORY] Rainbow colour map for a quantitative dB field: creates false contour edges, is not perceptually uniform, and is not colour-blind accessible.
- [ADVISORY] Apparent saturation/clipping at both ends of the colour scale (<~3 Hz and >~20 Hz); clipped pixels are indistinguishable from extreme in-range values.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (h) x-axis truncated at ~0.4 Hz; connector lengths exaggerate the multitaper-vs-smoothing discrepancy relative to the true ratio.
- [ADVISORY] Spectrogram time axes span the full record despite window lengths up to 4 s; edge padding/handling is undocumented and padded regions are not flagged.
- [ADVISORY] 90–97.5% window overlap makes adjacent columns statistically dependent; temporal smoothness is partly an artefact.
- [ADVISORY] Red 11–16 Hz trace in (a) has no amplitude reference, so its scale is uninterpretable.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add "n = 1 subject, one 40-s N2 epoch (representative example)" to the caption, and state the selection criterion.
- Replace the rainbow map with a perceptually uniform sequential map; if divergence about 0 dB is intended, use a diverging map with a defined midpoint.
- Use extended colourbar arrows (or "≥/≤" end labels) to make clipping explicit, or widen the limits so no data saturate.
- Extend the panel (h) x-axis to 0, or keep the truncation but add a break marker and a note that the axis does not start at zero; alternatively plot the ratio (achieved / target width) so the comparison is scale-free.
- Mark or shade the first/last half-window of each spectrogram as edge-affected, and state the padding method in the caption.
- State the overlap fraction in the caption and note that adjacent columns are not independent.
- Add a µV scale bar (or a second y-axis) for the offset 11–16 Hz trace in (a), and state its filter order/type.
- Consider adding a small inset or second row in (h) showing achieved vs. target width on the same axis, so the "at α = 2 the widths are much closer" claim is directly visible rather than inferred from labels.
---

## Cross-Figure Consistency Orchestrator

# CROSS-FIGURE SYNTHESIS — FINAL REVIEW

**Scope note:** the seven specialist reports have already covered within-figure defects (resolution/format, rasterized text, caption completeness, individual overplotting, jet colormap, missing ν values). Below I report only the issues that arise *between* figures, plus the final synthesis. Where a specialist item recurs across the set in a way that changes its severity, I note the cross-figure dimension explicitly rather than re-litigating the single-figure finding.

---

## BLOCKING ISSUES (must resolve before submission)

**1. The same method is drawn in different colours, and the same colour denotes different methods, across the set.** This is the single most damaging cross-figure defect: a reader who learns the colour code in one figure will misread the next.
 - *cyan* = "Hann, then box" [efigure1, efigure3] but = "Multitaper, L = 6" [efigure2] but = "L = 7" [figure4c]
 - *olive/yellow* = "Bohman, then box" [efigure1, efigure3] but = "Multitaper L = 7, adaptive" [efigure2] but = a window-shift copy [figure5a, efigure4a]
 - *magenta* = "Welch" [efigure3, figure6d, figure7, figure8d/f] but = "sliding sinc window" [figure1]
 - *red* = "25 % cosine taper → parabola" [efigure1–3, figure6c, figure7, figure8] but = the **bias** curve [figure3b], the tapered record [figure3a], the 7th Slepian taper [figure4a], the 2α reference line [figure4d], and the "smoothing" marker [figure9h]
 - *blue* = **true PSD** [figure2, figure6, efigure2] but = the **noise** curve [figure3b]
 - the periodogram is grey [efigure2, figure8] but black [figure2a]; recipe (b′) is red everywhere except figure2, where it is black
 — affects: [all 13 figures, most acutely efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1, figure2, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9]
 **Fix:** one locked method→colour table in a shared style file, applied to every figure and stated once in the Methods; reserve blue exclusively for "true PSD" and never reuse red for a non-method quantity.

**2. The same method carries different in-figure labels across figures.** "Recipe (b′)" [figure7, figure8f] vs "25% cosine taper, then parabola" [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3]; "Box" [figure1b] vs "Untapered, then box" [efigure1–3]; "Multitaper" [figure8f] vs "Multitaper, L = 7" [efigure1, efigure2, figure7] vs "Thomson's estimate" [captions of figure5, figure6]; "Estimate" [figure2a, figure6a] with no method name at all. A reader cannot align legends between figures, and "(b′)" additionally collides with panel label (b). — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8]
 **Fix:** a single controlled vocabulary of five to six method strings, used verbatim in every legend, every caption and Table 2.

**3. Figures 2 and 6 are built as explicitly parallel 4×2 grids (estimate over its kernel) but use different conventions, defeating the intended comparison.** (i) Inset zoom span: 0.39–0.41 in figure2(e–g) and **0.36–0.44** in figure2(h), versus 0.39–0.41 for **all four** panels of figure6 — so the reader who compares main-lobe widths between the two figures is comparing different magnifications in identically styled frames. (ii) Estimate colour: black in all four panels of figure2, but green/black/red/magenta in figure6. (iii) figure6's kernel row uses the same −150 dB floor and dark-green trace as figure2 — good — but figure2(e) fills solid while figure6(e) does not, so the two rows do not read as the same object. — affects: [figure2, figure6]
 **Fix:** identical inset span, identical estimate colour policy, identical kernel rendering (envelope or reduced-alpha) in both figures.

**4. The two spectrogram figures do not share a colour-scale convention.** figure8 carries **three** colourbars with no label and no units over a 10–45 dB range; figure9 carries **one** shared colourbar labelled "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)" over −5 to +12.5 dB. Both use jet. Since both are EEG power spectrograms in dB, the reader will assume the scales are comparable when they are not, and only one of the two states its reference level. — affects: [figure8, figure9]
 **Fix:** one perceptually uniform map (cividis/viridis) in both; one shared, unit-labelled colourbar per figure; state the dB reference identically; if the ranges must differ, say so on the figure.

**5. Physical width and effective type size are not controlled across the set.** Eleven figures are laid out for double column (1829–2101 px) while **figure3 and figure7 are single-column aspect (~992 px)**. At the intended widths, equivalent elements land anywhere from ~5.5 pt (figure2/figure6 insets, figure8 colourbar ticks) to ~7 pt (figure1, figure8 main ticks), with panel labels 8–9 pt. If figure3/figure7 are placed at double-column width their lettering will be roughly twice everyone else's. — affects: [all 13 figures, critically figure2, figure3, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure4]
 **Fix:** declare the target width per figure, regenerate at that exact `figsize`, and fix absolute point sizes after scaling (7 pt ticks / 8.5 pt axis titles / 8 pt legends / 10 pt bold panel labels). Do not scale finished artwork.

**6. Line weight carries no consistent meaning.** The heaviest stroke is the *reference estimate* in figure1(a) (thick black multitaper), the *ground truth* in figure2 and figure6 (thick blue dashed), and the *limiting case* L = N in figure4(c). A reader trained by figure1 will read the thick line in figure2 as the estimate. — affects: [figure1, figure2, figure4, figure6]
 **Fix:** reserve the heaviest weight for one role across the set (recommend ground truth / reference), and set all other series to one common weight.

**7. Uncertainty convention is not uniform, and the set contains three statistically different kinds of figure with no visual distinction.** Deterministic kernels (efigure1, efigure3, efigure4, figure4, figure5); single realizations (efigure2, figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9); and one ensemble over n = 300 (figure3). No figure shows any dispersion. ν is quoted for most estimators but is **missing entirely for figure3** (the only ensemble figure, and the one whose curves *are* variance and bias) and **missing for the adaptive L = 7 estimate in efigure2**. — affects: [efigure2, figure3, and by implication all figures that quote ν]
 **Fix:** adopand state one convention: (i) deterministic panels say so explicitly and show no bands; (ii) single-realization panels annotate ν in-panel; (iii) ensemble panels (figure3) carry a Monte-Carlo interval or SE band and quote ν(R). Apply the same wording in every caption.

---

## ADVISORY ISSUES (strongly recommended)

**1. Axis-title capitalization and quantity naming are mixed across the set.** Sentence-case titles ("Frequency (Hz)", "RMS error (dB)", "Achieved half-power width (Hz)") coexist with all-lowercase ones ("half-power width × NΔ", "kernel power beyond twice the half-power half-width", "max. |difference| (dB)"); the same quantity is "Ŝ(f) (dB)" in seven figures and "PSD (dB)" in two. — affects: [efigure1, efigure3, figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9]

**2. dB reference levels are stated in exactly one place in the whole set** (figure9's colourbar). Every other dB axis is bare, including two spectrogram colourbars, six PSD axes and eight kernel axes. — affects: [all figures with a dB axis]

**3. Gridline treatment is inconsistent.** Dense dotted two-way grids in eleven figures, no grid in figure3(a) and figure4(a), and grids at different densities (0.025 intervals in efigure1a vs decade-only in figure1b). In efigure1, efigure3, efigure4 and figure3 the dotted grid is stylistically **identical to substantive dotted reference lines** (ideal box, ±R/2, 8/(NΔ), peak-width markers), so decoration and data share one encoding across four figures. — affects: [efigure1, efigure3, efigure4, figure3, figure4, figure9]

**4. Axis furniture is uniformly non-standard rather than uniformly clean.** All 13 figures carry four-sided frames with mirrored ticks on top and right. This is at least *consistent*, so it is advisory — but the set would read as substantially more polished with left/bottom spines only, applied globally.

**5. Legend placement and coverage follow no convention.** Inside upper-left [figure2a], upper-right [figure6a, figure7a, figure8f], lower-left [figure4a], lower-right [figure4d], an empty grid quadrant [efigure3], panel-local in every relevant panel [figure1, efigure4], and **absent from the payoff panel** [efigure1b, efigure2b, figure5a, figure5c]. In figure2, figure6 and figure8 the legend appears in one panel only but is intended to govern four. — affects: [all multi-series figures]

**6. Marker conventions differ.** Open circles [efigure3, figure1c, figure4d] vs filled circles/squares [figure9h]; marker sizes range from ~3 px (efigure3, unresolvable in print) to large filled markers (figure9h); efigure3 is the only figure using marker *shape* as an information channel. — affects: [efigure3, figure1, figure4, figure9]

**7. Panel labels are stylistically uniform — lowercase, parenthesized, outside upper-left — which is a genuine strength of the set.** Remaining gaps are size (8–8.5 pt in most, 9 pt in figure1/4/8/9, all below the 9–10 pt target) and horizontal offset (figure1's "(a)" sits noticeably further left than its "(b)"/"(c)"). Also note efigure3 has a legend occupying the position a reader will expect panel (d) to hold. — affects: [all 13 figures]

**8. Visual weight is unbalanced.** efigure4 (16 panels) and figure9 (8 panels, six raster spectrograms) dominate; figure3 and figure7 are sparse single-column stacks; figure8/figure9 are heavy jet rasters while figure2 and figure3 are restrained black/blue/dark-green line art. figure2 and figure3 are the most polished figures in the set and should be the style target for the rest. — affects: [efigure4, figure3, figure7, figure8, figure9]

**9. Supplementary-figure numbering may not match citation order.** figure5's caption cites "Fig. S4" (= efigure4, "Fig. 5 in full"), whereas efigure1–efigure3 support the Table 2 / recipe-comparison material that appears later in the narrative. If S4 is the first supplement cited in the text, the supplement is out of sequence and should be renumbered (the "Fig. 5 in full" figure becomes S1). Verify against the body-text citation order. — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4, figure5]

**10. Shared data records are reused across figures without cross-figure signposting.** The same EEG epoch appears in figure1(a) and figure7(a); the same AR(4) record underlies figure2, figure3, figure6 and (a different realization of the same process) efigure2. Only figure3, figure6 and figure7 flag the reuse, and they do so via internal labels (`fig:motif`, `fig:abstract`) rather than consistently. State the shared provenance in every caption that reuses a record. — affects: [efigure2, figure1, figure2, figure3, figure6, figure7]

**11. figure1 forward-references the result it depends on.** Its central claim (all-N eigenvalue-weighted multitaper ≡ box-smoothed periodogram) is only established in figure4. Acceptable if figure1 is a graphical-abstract-style opener (its internal label `fig:abstract` suggests so), but the caption should say "as shown in Section/Fig. 4" so the logical order is explicit. — affects: [figure1, figure4]

**12. Abbreviation practice differs between captions.** PSD is expanded only in figure1's caption; EEG is never expanded anywhere; RMS is expanded in figure3 only; C4, C4-M1 and ELROND are never expanded. Captions must each be self-contained. — affects: [efigure2, figure1, figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9]

**13. Whole-set output pipeline.** All 13 files are PNG at 255–306 DPI with rasterized text — i.e. the deficiency is uniform, so a single fix serves the set: regenerate every figure as vector PDF/EPS (rasterizing only the spectrogram surfaces in figure8/figure9 at 400–600 DPI) from one shared style file at declared widths. Doing this once resolves the format, resolution, text-rendering, font-family, type-size and colour-palette items simultaneously. — affects: [all 13 figures]

---

## OVERALL ASSESSMENT

**Publication readiness: Major revisions**

The scientific content is well matched to the graphics and the set has real structural strengths — a uniform panel-label style across all 13 figures, a genuinely good parallel design in figure2/figure6, consistently dark-green kernel traces, consistently navy weight-ladder bars, and two exemplary figures (figure2, figure3) that show what the rest should look like. The blocking problems are almost entirely *consistency* problems rather than content problems, which means they are cheap to fix at the source but fatal if left: a reader moving through thirteen figures currently encounters cyan, olive, magenta and red each meaning two or three different things, the same method labelled three different ways, two deliberately parallel figures using mismatched inset magnifications and estimate colours, and two spectrogram figures with incompatible colourbar conventions.

The highest-leverage action is to regenerate the entire set from one shared style file that locks (i) a colourblind-safe method→colour→label table, (ii) one controlled method vocabulary, (iii) one line-weight hierarchy, (iv) declared physical widths with absolute point sizes, and (v) one axis/grid/legend convention — replacing jet with a perceptually uniform map in figure8/figure9 and exporting vector output at the same time. After that, the remaining work is the per-figure caption and annotation completion already itemized by the specialist agents.
